# Changelog

Changes that are merged but not yet in a release go under **Unreleased**. Newest first.

## Unreleased

- Fix: finished stories failed after waiting two hours for their turn to merge into `develop`, and every retry then failed at the first step. (1) The run that holds the merge turn starts its tests at once instead of queueing for a test slot behind other stories. (2) A run waits up to 8 hours for its turn instead of 2. (3) A retry of a story whose work an earlier run finished and committed goes straight to the merge (conflicts are resolved there) instead of failing or building it again.
- UI states 3b — the Board refreshes through the poller, with filter buttons that keep focus (#424). `renderBoard` in `ui/board.js` uses `poller` and `liveStates` from `ui/live.js`; its own timer, busy and visibility code is gone, and it still returns the cleanup at once. First load shows a skeleton under the heading; a first-load failure shows `errorState` with Retry. A failed refresh keeps the cards and shows "Could not refresh. Showing data from …" with Retry once; the next success removes it. "Updated HH:MM" is shown. A hidden tab makes no requests; on return the tick of a watcher whose GitHub link was opened is sent first, then one GET. Page scroll and the sideways scroll of `.board` (`data-scroll="board"`) are kept across a redraw, and a redraw waits while a select has focus. `filterBar` and `filterEmpty` in `ui/filters.js` take `{ focus = "filter" }` and name their buttons `${focus}-remove-${key}`, `${focus}-clear` and `${focus}-empty-clear`, so every Board Tab stop has a unique `data-focus`; the Runs lists keep the default names. Docs: Board row in `docs/ui-redesign/states.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Pages that refresh themselves"). New `tests/ui-board-live.test.ts`; `tests/ui-board.test.ts`, `tests/ui-filters.test.ts` and `tests/ui-a11y-live.test.ts` are updated.
- UI states 3a — `ui/live.js`: one poller, scroll keeping and live states (#423). New exports `poller`, `keepScroll`, `liveStates` and `dialogOpen`. The poller sends one request at a time, only in a visible tab and not offline (the offline signal is ignored on `localhost`, `127.0.0.1` and `[::1]`), awaits `wake()` on return to the tab, and draws only when the data changed. `ui/states.js`: new `staleText(at, failed)` (used by `staleNote`); `errorState` takes `{ focus }` and a `banner` action takes `focus` (sets `data-focus`). Existing callers behave as before. `FakeElement` in `tests/helpers/fake-dom.ts` gets `scrollTop` and `scrollLeft`. New `tests/ui-live.test.ts`; `tests/ui-states.test.ts` is extended. `docs/ui-redesign/states.md` has the section "Background refresh (part 3, #344)". No page uses it yet.

- UI states 5 — Repositories (#346). `ui/repos.js`, `ui/admin-repos.js`, `ui/admin-credentials.js` and `ui/watcher-form.js` use the standard states.
  - **Loading and failure.** My repositories, All repositories and Credentials draw a skeleton table on first load. A failed load shows `errorState` with Retry (the server text from `plainError`, wrapped by `explainError`), or `permissionState` on a 403. A failed `repoMethods` call is partial: the list shows with a warning banner (with Retry) that adding is not possible now.
  - **Stale.** A reload after an action keeps the list on screen and shows `staleNote` with Retry on failure, instead of an error toast only. Only the newest fetch of a page draws.
  - **Provider failure.** A failed connection test stays on the row ("Connection failed" with the reason; a test that could not run shows its error) until the next test; the button reads "Test again". Secrets never appear in these texts.
  - **Success.** "Repository added" stays a toast. For a deploy-key repository whose connection does not work yet, a banner on the page asks for the deploy key on GitHub until it works.
  - **Dialogs.** Remove repository and Generate a new key use `confirmDialog` with the same texts as before; no native `confirm(` is left in `ui/repos.js`. Read-only preview: no action buttons in any state.
  - **Code.** New exports in `ui/repos.js`: `connectionProblem`, `explainRepoError`, `loadFailed`, `needsDeployKey`. The watcher form links to My repositories when there are no repositories.
  - **Docs and tests.** `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md`, the Repositories row of the matrix in `docs/ui-redesign/states.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Repository pages"). New `tests/ui-repos-states.test.ts`; the repos, admin-repos, admin-credentials, watchers and a11y-admin tests are extended.
- UI states 8 — Administration (#349). Users, Watchers, Problems, Dashboard, Audit and Settings show a skeleton on first load and an `errorState` with Retry on failure. Dashboard: each failed call of the six (`partNote`, e.g. "Could not load evals.") is named in its own section and the rest is shown. Watchers: "Checking…" is an inline busy state next to the button; a failed monitor call shows an error in the monitor card only. Users, Watchers and Problems keep their list when a reload fails and show `staleNote` with Retry; the newest reload wins. Settings: "Saving…", "Saved at …" and "Not saved." next to Save, plus the toast. Delete watcher, Clean up workspaces and the unreadable state file use `confirmDialog` (`confirmUnreadable` in `ui/monitor.js`); no native `confirm(` is left in `ui/admin.js` and `ui/problems.js`. Audit keeps its `my === loads` guard and uses the shared empty and error states. Dashboard, Watchers, Problems and Settings draw into their own box in `ui/app.js`, so a late answer cannot touch the next page. Docs: Administration row of `docs/ui-redesign/states.md`, `inventory.md`, `dialogs.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`. New `tests/ui-users-states.test.ts`, `tests/ui-watchers-states.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings-states.test.ts`; the audit, dashboard, problems and monitor tests are extended.
- Component kit 6 — record components: evidence, timeline entries, logs and diffs (#330). New `ui/kit/records.js` and `ui/kit/records.css` export `evidence`, `timelineEntry`, `timeline`, `logView` and `diffView`. `logView` is a `role="log"` region with a name and `tabindex="0"`, coloured by tokens so it follows the theme. `diffView` takes the `{ stat, patch }` data of `api.diff`, marks file, hunk, added and removed lines by class, keeps the `+` and `-` characters and shows the "Diff truncated" notice when `truncated` is set. Neither cuts its data. All text is added as text nodes. Every component draws with missing data (no time, steps or lines). The gallery has sections for long lines, 500 log lines, a large diff, empty states and the narrow layout. `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md` has the five entries and the "Building a new screen" checklist. New `tests/ui-kit-records.test.ts`; `tests/ui-gallery.test.ts` and `tests/ui-kit-actions.test.ts` are extended. No existing page is changed.
- Skill runtime 2a — plan record store and the `plan-comment` tool (#414). New `src/skills/plan-record.ts` (store under `<data folder>/skill-plans/`, own lock `skill-plans.lock`, 20 records per issue, age clean-up with a `purged.json` note) and `src/skills/plan-record-rules.ts` (`pickPlanRecord`, `planChanges`, `technologyHashOf`, the parsers). New `tools/plan-comment`: hashes a comment body, and lists the plan comments of an issue as IDs and hashes, never text. `projectEvidence` is exported from `src/skills/run-lock.ts`. Nothing calls them yet; no behaviour change.
- Refinement 9e-3a — the publish plan shows how a split issue will be replaced (#417). Nothing is written; no UI change.
  - `GET /api/refinement/:id/publish` has `replaces: { issue, parts, ready, cut?, dependants }`, with `before`/`after` or `byHand` for each open issue that depends on the original.
  - `GET /api/refinement/:id` has `source.replace` (`waiting`, `due`, `done`) and no longer shows `source.replacing`.
  - New server option `openIssuePages` (default 10).
  - `mergeJournal` keeps an unfinished entry that the scan no longer finds.
- Skill runtime 5 — reuse skills across repairs and reload them on fallback (#180).
  - **Behaviour.** A Claude step with `resume:` that continues a session holding the same locked block gets a one-line reminder instead of the full skill text. A new session (retry, fallback to another model or provider, or a resume that cannot continue) gets the complete block once. The lock is still verified before every session; a changed registry stops the step.
  - **Logs and records.** One line per session: `skill context: reused|loaded|reloaded …` with ids and sizes only, and `! skill context: rejected (…)`. The step record `skills` gains `state`, `digest`, `attachedBytes` and `attachedEstimatedTokens`; the step record gains `skills_digest`. Older run files stay valid.
  - **Code.** `attachSkillPayload`, `skillPayloadDigest`, `skillReminder` in `src/skills/payload.ts`; `sessionToResume` in `src/agents/run.ts`; the load log line moved from `skillSession` to the runner. The agent note says the `<foundry-skills>` block may come from an earlier prompt of the session.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Skills in Claude sessions".
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-payload.test.ts`, `tests/agents.test.ts`, `tests/engine-skill-lock.test.ts`, `tests/skill-run-lock-flow.test.ts`.

- Skill runtime 6 — compact review checks from selected skills for reviewers (#181).
  - **Package.** A skill may hold an optional `REVIEW.md` (at most 8 KiB, not empty, needs the `reviewer` role or no roles). It is part of the digest. New `SkillPackage.review` and `SKILL_LIMITS.reviewMdBytes`.
  - **Flow.** New optional `skill_role: coder | reviewer` on `claude` steps. A reviewer step must be read-only, allow only `Read`, `Glob`, `Grep` and `LS`, and not `resume` (`reviewerStepProblem`, `readOnlyStep` in `src/flow/schema.ts`); checked at load and again before the step runs. `plan_review` and the `review_N` steps of `issue-plan` are reviewers (`scripts/build-flows.mjs`; `flows/*.yaml` regenerated).
  - **Context.** `renderReviewPayload` and `skillSession(…, role)` build a `<foundry-skills role="reviewer">` block with description and `REVIEW.md` only, from skills loaded for coding that allow the reviewer role. Plan review uses the draft plan's request (`draftSkillRequest`). The block is smaller than the coding block and limited by new `skills.review.max_tokens` (default 3000) and `max_skill_tokens` (default 1000).
  - **Agents.** Claude and Codex reviewers get the same block. A reviewer Claude session runs without the personal setup; Codex reviewers run with `--ignore-user-config`, no MCP servers and no hooks.
  - **Compatibility.** Steps without `skill_role` and runs without skills behave as before. The step record's `skills` gains an optional `role`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Review checks for reviewers"; `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` `skill_role`.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/skill-review.test.ts`; `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`.
- Work tracking 2c — dependency chain in a side panel instead of dimming the board (#375).
  - **Button.** A card or row with blockers has **What is in the way?** (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`). A card without a run opens the panel on Enter. Nothing on the board is dimmed.
  - **Panel.** New `ui/work-panel.js`: `chainOrder(item, items)` (pure; deepest blocker first, direct blockers from `after` marked, safe against cycles, uses `next.blockers` and `after` from the same `GET /api/board` answer, no new request) and `panelView`. It shows title, issue link, status, sentence, next move and where, owner, age, "Open run" and "In the way of #N" with each story's status and next move, or a GitHub link when it is not on the board. Chain entries on the board open in the panel; **Back** returns.
  - **Behaviour.** The panel is a `complementary` region named after the story (a `div` with an explicit role, because `.no-side aside` is hidden). Escape and **Close** close it and focus returns to the opener. Up to 760 px it is a dialog with focus kept inside. It follows the 5 s refresh and closes with "#N is no longer on the board" when the story is gone. Filters, layout and scroll do not change. GitHub links in the panel trigger the watcher check. The open panel is not saved. `keyOf` moved to `ui/work-model.js`.

- UI states 2 — Toasts, banners, confirmations and undo (#343).
  - **Toast.** `toast(msg, kind = "info", { action, sticky })` in `ui/dom.js`; the two-argument call works as before. Info goes after 3.5 s. An error or `sticky` toast stays until its ✕ button is pressed. A new toast replaces the old one and resets the timer. `action: { label, run }` draws one button that runs once and closes the toast; focus then goes back to the opener, or to `#main` if a redraw removed it. `#toast` in `ui/index.html` and `ui/user/index.html` is a live region: `role="status"` for info, `role="alert"` for errors. The same text within 5 s of its last call is announced once. Styles in `ui/css/components.css` (and `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`).
  - **`confirmDialog`.** `confirmDialog({ title, text, confirm = "Delete", cancel, danger = true })` returns `Promise<boolean>`, built on `modal()`. Focus starts on Cancel. Only the confirm button gives `true`; Escape, ✕ and the backdrop give `false`; focus returns to the opener. Native `confirm(` calls are not converted here.
  - **`banner`.** `banner(kind, text, actions?)` in `ui/states.js` for messages that stay on the page. It uses the kit banner (`error` → `fail`, `warn` → `warn`, `info` → `neutral`): `role="alert"` for errors, `role="status"` otherwise. No new CSS.
  - **Undo.** Dismiss on Home (`ui/turn.js`) and Drop in Refinement (`ui/refinement.js`) show an Undo toast that calls the restore route and redraws. Drop keeps its confirmation, and Undo is shown only for your own session (restore is for the owner). Both Undo toasts are sticky.
  - **Server.** `POST /api/your-turn/restore` accepts an optional `{ key }` and restores only that item (`restoreTurn(key?)` in `src/server/your-turn.ts`). Without `key` it restores everything as before. A `null`, empty or non-string `key` is 400. `ui/api.js`: `api.restoreTurn(key)`. No new route, so no new permission rule.
  - **Docs and tests.** `docs/ui-redesign/states.md` (rules for messages and confirmations), `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` (line of the Drop confirm), `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` ("Messages and confirmations"). Tests in `tests/ui-dom.test.ts`, `tests/ui-home-admin.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement.test.ts`, `tests/server-your-turn.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts`.
- Guide names — navigation names match `ui/ia.js` (#413). The page tables of `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` (section 1) and `docs/DESIGN.md` (section 15) list the seven places with their pages. The guides and `README.md` say Home where they said Your turn as a page. The guide has a new part "Old and new interface"; `docs/ui-redesign/rollout.md` (new) says what is built and what moved where. There is no switch to the old interface: #401 did not build one. `tests/guide-docs.test.ts` compares the tables, the user-display lines and the moved addresses with `ui/ia.js`. Docs and tests only.
- UI redesign navigation 2a — search API (#395). New `GET /api/search`, read-only, for users and admins.
  - **Behaviour.** `?q=` searches runs (also queued ones), issues (admin), refinement sessions, repositories and flows, and returns up to 8 hits per group with `more`. `?id=type:id` (1 to 8 values) checks single items with the same visibility. A user sees only their own things and the flows they can start; an admin sees all accounts, with owner names, and the board's issues. Hits have fixed fields only: never a server path, cost, repository URL or credential. A run's `status` is the `next.status` word of `GET /api/runs`. `as=` works with view-as.
  - **Errors.** 400 for `q` over 100 characters or a bad `id` (more than 8, unknown type, wrong shape). If one source fails, the answer is 200 with `incomplete` naming the type and the other groups present.
  - **Compatibility.** New route only. Run briefs gain two in-memory fields, `taskLine` and `issueTitle` (200 characters at most); nothing new is stored.
  - **Code.** New `src/server/search.ts`; `ticketTitleOf` in `src/engine/state.ts` (moved from `src/board.ts`); `checkedRun` and `runsListFor` in `src/server/api-runs.ts`; `userFlowList` in `src/server/api-flows.ts`; `queuedJobNext` in `src/server/next.ts`; permission rule for `GET search`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`: permission-table row and "Search (API)".
  - **Tests.** `tests/search.test.ts`, `tests/search-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/board.test.ts`, `tests/run-filter.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/briefs.ts`.
- Guide screenshots 3 — Home images, image links in the guides, and a doc test for images (#412). `SHOTS` has 19 entries: `home` (admin Home) and `user-home` (Home of a user) are new. `docs/images/your-turn.png` is removed; the guides, `README.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` show `home.png` instead. The user guide shows the Home of a user again. `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md` lists the 19 images as captured from the redesign. New `tests/guide-docs.test.ts` checks that every guide image is in the manifest, every manifest image is used, `docs/images/` holds exactly those files, and the table in `measurement.md` matches.
- Work tracking 2b — board layout with compact cards, a Display menu, and keyboard and touch use (#374).
  - **Layout.** The Work page has a **Board | List** switch (`aria-pressed`). Board is the default. Both layouts show the same items, filters, grouping, order and counts; switching sends no request and changes no filter. The board has one column per group; grouped by status all nine columns show, also when empty (the status filter now narrows the stories, not the columns). Grouping "none" on the board falls back to status (`boardPrefs`, `ui/work-model.js`) and the Display menu says so.
  - **Cards.** `workCard` and `boardLayout` (new `ui/work-board.js`): issue link and title, next move, "Blocked by …" links, repository, owner and age, at most four lines; `next.text` is the `title`. A your-turn card has a text mark, a blocked card a "Blocked" pill.
  - **Display menu.** `displayMenu` (new `ui/work-display.js`), a `details` element: visible properties (repository, next move, blockers, owner, age, step), Compact cards, group by and order by. It applies to list and board and is saved per account. `PROPS` and `PREFS_VERSION` are new in `ui/work-model.js`; `cleanPrefs` (`ui/work-prefs.js`) validates `layout`, `props` and `compact`, drops unknown props, and keeps "step" visible once for choices saved before it existed. `ui/work-list.js` honours `props` and `compact`.
  - **Keyboard and touch.** Roving `tabindex` (one Tab stop per column), arrow keys, Home/End and Enter through the pure `moveFocus`; cards have `data-focus="card:<key>"` so focus survives the 5 s refresh. Up to 760 px columns scroll sideways with scroll snap, column bodies scroll on their own with a sticky heading, and controls in cards are 44 px tall. The page redraws only when the answer or a preference changed.
  - **Docs and tests.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Work page". New `tests/ui-work-board.test.ts`; `tests/ui-work*.test.ts` and the style baseline are extended.
- Guide screenshots 2 — run pages, dialogs, Settings, Dashboard, repositories and watchers (#411). `npm run test:ui -- --grep @guide` now also writes `run-dialog`, `run-log`, `run-steps`, `run-diff`, `run-waiting`, `settings`, `dashboard`, `repos`, `watchers` and `watcher-form`. `run-diff` is taken on the large test server. `tests/screenshot-manifest.test.ts` checks the placeholders and that a `{diffRun}` shot uses the large server.
- Accessibility 4 — keyboard access and predictable focus during live updates (#388).
  - **Run rows.** The status pill in the first cell of a run row (`runRow`, `ui/runs.js`) is a real link to the run (`Open` when there is no status record), so a keyboard user can open a run. The flow name no longer links. The row click stays as a pointer shortcut.
  - **Focus after a redraw.** Controls on the board, Your turn, Runs, Since, health and My runs pages carry a stable `data-focus` name made from run ids, issue numbers or item keys, so `mount()` gives focus back to the same control after a timed redraw. When that control is gone, `mount()` (`ui/dom.js`) focuses the first heading in the redrawn area, then one in its parent, then the area itself (`tabindex="-1"`), never `<body>`.
  - **No redraw under your hands.** The Runs list skips a timed redraw while a select or text field has focus (`fieldFocused`, exported from `ui/runs.js`, also used by `ui/board.js`). The admin run page holds an update while the retry select has focus and draws it when focus leaves. Your turn and My runs hold their redraw while a dialog is open and then draw the newest answer. The Since strip focuses the page heading when it hides with focus inside it. Polling intervals and hashes are unchanged.
  - **Run log.** `logBox` (`ui/runs.js`) makes the log a labelled `role="log"` region with `aria-live="polite"` and `tabindex="0"`, so it scrolls with the keyboard. Used on the admin and user run pages.
  - **Status announcements.** `statusAnnouncer` reads a run's status change out once through a polite status region, on both run pages.
  - **Help mark.** The **?** in `ui/next.js` has a stable `data-focus` name and closes with Escape.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` "Keyboard" gains a table of keys by place. Four line numbers in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` follow the move in `ui/runs.js`.
  - **Tests.** `tests/ui-a11y-live.test.ts` (new: focus kept over a timer tick, `audit()` and one announcement for each polled page), `tests/ui-dom.test.ts`, `tests/ui-next.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fake-dom.ts`.
- Component kit 3 — display primitives: card, table, list, badge, banner, skeleton and emptyState (#327). `ui/kit/display.js` exports the seven components; `ui/kit/display.css` (imported by `kit.css`) styles them with `scf-` classes and tokens only. The table is a real `<table>` with a caption and `<th scope="col">` in a scroll box, so wide tables scroll inside the box (finding F14); with `onRowOpen` the first cell holds a button; with no rows it shows `empty`. Tones are `neutral | ok | fail | run | warn | accent`; `banner` uses `role="alert"` for `fail` and `role="status"` otherwise, and its dismiss control is an `iconButton` labelled "Dismiss". `skeleton` is `aria-busy` with a visually hidden label. The old `.card`, `.table`, `.pill`, `.badge` and `.empty` classes stay for the existing pages. Gallery sections for all seven (variants, long content, narrow layout) in `ui/gallery/registry.js`, plus a `.gallery-stack` rule in `ui/css/pages/gallery.css` (also in `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`). Docs: `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md` ("Display"). Tests: `tests/ui-kit-display.test.ts` (new), `tests/ui-gallery.test.ts`, `tests/ui-kit-actions.test.ts`. Users see no change.
- Runs list 3b — Search and filters for the run list (server) (#377).
  - **List.** `GET /api/runs` accepts `q`, `repo`, `flow`, `status` and `since`, in any mix, with `archived` and (admin only) `owner`. All are applied before the 200-run cap, so a run older than the newest 200 can be found. The answer has the same shape as before; without parameters it is unchanged.
  - **Values.** `q` is at most 200 characters and matches, without regard to case, a part of the run id, flow name, repository name, `owner/name#7`, `#7` and the first line of the task (for a user: the task line as the user sees it). `status` is a run status; `flow` and `repo` are exact names; `since` is an ISO date (`2026-10-01` or with a time) and matches runs started at or after it. A wrong value is 400 with a short sentence.
  - **Users.** A user gets only their own runs with every filter; `owner` from a user is ignored.
  - **New route.** `GET /api/run-filters` answers `{ repos, flows }`, the values of the runs the caller can see (a user: their own), for the filter menus. Rule `yes` in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **Code.** `src/server/run-filter.ts` (new: `parseRunFilter`, `matchesRun`, `runFilterValues`), `src/server/api-runs.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts` (`RunBrief.taskLine`, the first line of the task, at most 200 characters; optional). Tests: `tests/run-filter.test.ts` (new), `tests/run-archive.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`. Docs: the route table in `docs/USER_GUIDE.md`.
- Accessibility 5 — text equivalents for charts, status colours, icons and AI-written content (#389).
  - **Cost chart.** The bars are no longer tab stops and the tooltip no longer has `role="status"` (it is for the pointer only). "Show as table" now sits under the chart with one row (day, cost, runs) for every bar, including days with no runs (`costChart`, `costTable` in `ui/dashboard.js`).
  - **Rates.** `rateBar` hides the track from screen readers and reads as text, for example "12 of 15, 80%" (`rateText`).
  - **Status.** Colour is no longer the only difference: finished steps in `ui/runs.js` and `ui/user/runs.js` have a `.sr-only` word (Succeeded, Failed) next to the glyph, and the other status marks got text or icon differences. Status text still comes from the server.
  - **Glyphs.** New `glyph()` in `ui/dom.js` marks decorative glyphs (`✕`, `◆`, `›`, step icons) `aria-hidden`; their controls have names.
  - **AI-written content.** New `aiProps(what)` and `AI_LABEL` in `ui/dom.js` make a region named "Written by AI: <what>" around architect suggestions, talk, drafts, impact and planner questions (`ui/refinement-*.js`, `ui/turn-act.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`). The visible marking is unchanged.
  - **Tests.** `tests/ui-a11y-equivalents.test.ts` (new): table rows equal the chart data, the rate text, text for every run status, and `audit()` on the dashboard and a refinement session. Line numbers in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` and the style baseline are updated.
- Accessibility 3 — labels and error messages in administration, repositories and sign-in (#387).
  - **Behaviour.** Screen-reader users can fill in the administration, repository, watcher, model, user and sign-in forms and understand their errors. No API call changed.
  - **Names.** Every form control in `ui/admin.js`, `ui/watcher-form.js`, `ui/repos.js`, `ui/admin-repos.js`, `ui/users.js`, `ui/models.js`, `ui/audit.js`, `ui/monitor.js`, `ui/problems.js` and `ui/auth.js` has an accessible name: a `<label>` where the text is visible, `aria-label` otherwise. Checkboxes made by the local `input` and `check` helpers are inside their label.
  - **Errors.** Errors in these forms and dialogs use `role="alert"`. When the server error names a field, that field gets `aria-invalid="true"` and focus. Secret fields stay empty in the DOM.
  - **Tables.** Data tables on these pages have `<th scope="col">` headers and a `<caption class="sr-only">` or `aria-label`.
  - **Helpers.** `ui/dom.js` gets `markInvalid(fields, field)` (clears `aria-invalid` on all, marks and focuses one), `showError(line, message, { fields, field })` and `fieldFor(message, pairs)` (finds the field named by an error message).
  - **Tests.** `tests/ui-a11y-admin.test.ts` (new) runs `audit()` on each page and each dialog opened through `modal()`, with zero serious violations; `tests/ui-dom.test.ts` covers the new helpers. `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` has the new line numbers in `ui/repos.js`.
- Component kit 4 — overlay and navigation primitives (#328). New `ui/kit/overlays.js` exports `tabs`, `menu`, `tooltip`, `dialog`, `drawer` and `toast`, with roles, ARIA links, roving tabindex, menu keys, focus trap, Escape (not while `busy()`) and focus return built in. `dialog` and `drawer` return a promise like `modal`, mount into `#modal-root` and can stack (only the top layer gets keys; the ones below are `inert`). A backdrop click closes a dialog but not a drawer (`dismissOnBackdrop`, default on for dialog, off for drawer). The kit `toast` makes its own container; a `fail` toast stays until dismissed. Reuses `tabStops` and `trapTarget` from `ui/dom.js`. CSS: `ui/kit/overlays.css`, imported by `kit.css`. The old `modal` and `toast` in `ui/dom.js` and the `.tabs button.on` pattern in `ui/runs.js` are unchanged. Gallery sections for all six. Tests: `tests/ui-kit-overlays.test.ts` (new), `tests/ui-gallery.test.ts`, `tests/ui-kit-actions.test.ts`, `tests/helpers/fake-dom.ts` (focus tracking). Keys and props: `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md`. Users see no change.
- UI CSS architecture 3 — no inline styles in the flow editor, graph and library (#339).
  - **Behaviour.** No change for users. `ui/editor.js`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/step-types.js`, `ui/graph.js` and `ui/library.js` no longer use `style:` or `.style.`; they use utility classes or named classes.
  - **Dirty dot.** Shown and hidden with the class `.dirty-dot.clean` (`visibility: hidden`), set with `classList.toggle("clean", !dirty)`. It keeps its space when hidden.
  - **Legend.** The five legend lines take colour and line style from `.legend i.seq`, `.ok`, `.fail` (dashed), `.route` and `.par` (dotted) in `ui/css/pages/editor.css`.
  - **Checkbox row.** One combination everywhere: `row tight text-sm` on the label, `fit` on the input, plus `mt-4`, `mt-6` or `mt-8` where a top margin was needed. New utility `.mt-0` in `ui/css/utilities.css`.
  - **Tests and inventory.** The five files are off the "not cleaned yet" list in `tests/ui-inline-styles.test.ts`; `tests/ui-editor-styles.test.ts` is new; the style baseline is updated. `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md` now counts 53 uses in 11 files.
- UI CSS architecture 4 — no inline styles on the runs, repositories and refinement pages (#340). `ui/runs.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`, `ui/repos.js`, `ui/refinement.js` and the five `ui/refinement-*.js` files (suggest, talk, ready, publish, impact) use classes instead of `style:`. Both run pages use the same classes for the same things (`flush pre-wrap` for task text, `card mb-16`, `seg tabs mb-12`). Two new rules: `.cols-2` in `ui/css/utilities.css` and `.check-row` in `ui/css/components.css` (both also in `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`). The checkbox rows in the impact and publish views use `check check-row` with `fit`, not `row tight`, so the page rule `.drafts .row input` does not stretch the box. The guard `tests/ui-inline-styles.test.ts` lists the nine files as clean and checks the new classes; its "not cleaned yet" list stays for the editor files until #339 is built (whichever of the two is built last removes it). Repository and refinement tests assert classes instead of style objects. Users see no change. Docs: `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md` ("Inline styles" count) and `docs/ui-redesign/findings.md` (F12).
- Guide screenshots 1 — capture tool, manifest helpers and the first seven guide images (#410).
  - **Command.** `npm run test:ui -- --grep @guide` writes `docs/images/<name>.png` (1440×900, light theme) for `sign-in`, `board`, `runs`, `flows`, `flow-yaml`, `library` and `models`. Plain `npm run test:ui` does not register these tests (`UI_GUIDE`, set by the config).
  - **Private data.** Host paths are rewritten to demo paths in every text, title and field before the shot, and a guard fails the capture when a temporary folder, the home folder or the checkout is still on the page (checked before and after the screenshot). The `models` shot uses a fixed `/api/providers` answer, so it does not show the host's agents or local model servers.
  - **Flows.** `flows` and `flow-yaml` show the built-in `issue-gitflow`.
  - **Code.** `scripts/screenshots/shots.ts` (`fillPath`, `splitPath`, `demoPath`, `imageLinks`, name and path checks, `SHOTS`), `tests/browser/guide-prepare.ts`, `tests/browser/guide-shots.spec.ts` (new), `tests/browser/paths.ts`, `tests/browser/playwright.config.ts`. Tests: `tests/screenshot-manifest.test.ts`.
  - The guides are not edited in this part.
- Skill runtime 3 — load only the locked skills into Claude sessions (#178).
  - **Payload.** Before each Claude session the Foundry renders the skills of the run's lock into one `<foundry-skills>` block in front of the task prompt. It uses only the description and instructions of a package (never tools, profiles or files), says that Foundry safety rules and user/task instructions win and that a skill cannot grant tools, permissions or network access, and escapes text that looks like a block tag. No repository file is written.
  - **Boundary.** The agent note no longer says "Do not use skills"; it now allows only the `<foundry-skills>` block and forbids any other skill. A Claude session whose lock holds skills runs isolated (no personal skills, MCP servers, plugins, hooks or settings) even when `isolate_agents` is off. Codex sessions are unchanged (the lock is still checked).
  - **Budget.** The block stays within `skills.selection.max_tokens`. A skill is admitted with its dependencies or not at all; skills left out are logged. A mandatory skill that does not fit stops the step with `skill selection is blocked: … does not fit the skill context budget` (with a fix in `src/failure.ts`).
  - **Record.** `StepRecord.skills` (`loaded`, optional `omitted`, `bytes`, `estimatedTokens`) is new and optional; older run files and steps without a lock are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/payload.ts` (new), `src/engine/skill-lock.ts` (`skillSession`), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/steps/claude.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/failure.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-payload.test.ts` (new), `tests/agents.test.ts`, `tests/engine-skill-lock.test.ts`, `tests/failure.test.ts`, `tests/skill-run-lock-flow.test.ts`, fake `claude`.
- Refinement 9e-1 — building blocks for replacing a split issue (#403). `dependencyRange` and `DEP_REF_SOURCE` in `src/queue/deps.ts`; `src/refinement/dependants.ts` (new) with `rewriteDependsOn`, `rangeHash`, `findDependants`, `dependantComment`, `originalComment` and `replaceMarker`; `closeIssue` and `listOpenIssues` in `src/github.ts`; `updateIssue` can leave the title out. Nothing uses them yet, so nothing changes for users. Tests: `tests/deps.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-dependants.test.ts` (new), `tests/github-open-issues.test.ts` (new). `dependencyText` now is the exact piece of the body that `dependencyRange` names, so both agree for texts with Windows line ends (`\r\n`); the 1000-character cap counts the raw text, `\r` included.
- Component kit 2 — development-only component gallery and its server switch (#326). `scf ui --dev` and `scf serve --dev` serve the gallery at `/gallery/`; without `--dev`, every path under `/gallery` answers 404 (also `..`, `\` and upper-case spellings). The switch is on the command line only (`ServerOptions.dev`, default false), so a saved config cannot leave it on. The gallery shows every kit component with made-up data, in light and dark, comfortable and compact, wide and a 390 px frame; the choice is kept in the address query. It makes no API call. Code: `ui/gallery/` (`index.html`, `gallery.js`, `view.js`, `registry.js`), `ui/css/pages/gallery.css`, `isGalleryPath` in `src/server/http.ts`. No new API route, so no rule in `permissions.ts`. Tests: `tests/ui-gallery.test.ts`, `tests/ui-gallery-server.test.ts` (new), `tests/cli.test.ts`, `tests/ui-css.test.ts`, `tests/ui-kit-actions.test.ts`. How to open it and add a section: `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md`. Users see no change.
- Skill runtime 1 — persist a versioned skill lock in run state (#176).
  - **Lock.** At the first agent step after a checked, ready plan, the run resolves the plan's skill request (role `coder`) and writes `<run folder>/skill-lock.json` (mode 0600, atomic, version 1). Per skill: id, version, digest, source (`admin` or `builtin`, never a path), role, selection reason, the planner's reason and evidence, `requiredBy` and context estimate. Per lock: plan hash, repository commit (when readable), total estimate. The lock is made once per plan and never resolved again, so a resume uses the pinned versions, not the registry's current defaults.
  - **No private data.** Free text that looks like a host path or a secret is left out; `issue:` evidence is kept only as a number (or `issue:omitted`), so no issue text is stored. Code: `src/skills/run-lock.ts` (new), `src/engine/skill-lock.ts` (new).
  - **Run state.** `run.json` gets `skillLock`: lock digest, plan hash, time, estimate, commit and the list of `id`, `version`, `digest`, `selection`. The file is the authority; a missing or older summary is made again from it. The OS sandbox now denies agent writes to `skill-lock.json`.
  - **Verified before every session.** `runAgentStep` (`src/agents/run.ts`) checks the lock before each agent session, retries and fallbacks included. A skill that is missing, changed, no longer pinned or no longer approved, or a lock file that is missing, changed or invalid, stops the step before the agent runs, with a reason starting `skill integrity: `. A mandatory skill that cannot be used stops it with `skill selection is blocked: `. Both show in the failure text with a fix (`src/failure.ts`).
  - **Compatibility.** Runs without a lock (older runs, or runs that never had a checked plan) stay readable and nothing is checked for them. A run that plans again gets a new lock. `planGateRecord` is split out of `planSkillRequest` in `src/skills/request.ts`. Flows are unchanged.
  - **Tests.** `tests/skill-run-lock.test.ts`, `tests/engine-skill-lock.test.ts`, `tests/skill-run-lock-flow.test.ts` (new), `tests/skill-request.test.ts`, `tests/failure.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts`, `tests/runner.test.ts`.
- Component kit 1 — kit conventions, stylesheet, and action and form primitives (#325). New `ui/kit/actions.js` (`button`, `iconButton`, `link`) and `ui/kit/forms.js` (`field`, `textInput`, `textArea`, `select`, `checkbox`), built on `h`. The controls are unbound (`value` with `onInput`/`onChange`); `ui/fields.js` is unchanged. Styles are in `ui/kit/kit.css` (imports `actions.css` and `forms.css`), linked from `ui/index.html` and `ui/user/index.html`; they use `scf-` classes and tokens only. Kit modules never pass `style`. Tests: `tests/ui-kit-actions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-kit-forms.test.ts` (new), `tests/ui-css.test.ts` (kit CSS and source checks). Conventions and one entry per component: `docs/UI_COMPONENTS.md` (new). No page is migrated; users see no change.
- UI quality 4d — route compatibility test for the rollout (#401), first part.
  - **Behaviour.** No change for users. `tests/ui-rollout.test.ts` (new, unit tests, no server or browser) proves that every admin and user address in `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md` resolves to a page in `ui/ia.js` and not to the unknown-address fallback; that the old `#/your-turn` opens Home; that the `#/set-password/:token` link is read before the router; that `#/runs/<id>` opens the run on both displays; and that every link the notifier writes resolves.
  - **Known limit.** Status comments on GitHub write run ids as plain text, not as links, so only notification links are covered.
  - **Not done yet.** The `ui.version` setting, the `ui-classic/` copy, the Settings switch ("Interface version"), `docs/ui-redesign/rollout.md` (migration guidance, gate checklist, removal rule) and the user guide entry are not part of this change. The UI served is still the redesign.
  - **Tests.** `tableRows` and `code` moved unchanged from `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` to `tests/helpers/md-table.ts` (new) so both test files share them.
- Refinement 9e-2 — the session remembers a replacement in progress (#404). Stored fields and rules only; nothing uses them yet.
  - **Fields.** `source` may now hold `replacing` (`{ parts, cut?, dependants }`, at most 1,000 entries; each entry has `issue`, `title` and optionally `byHand`, `before`, `after`, `rangeBefore`, `rangeAfter`, `outcome`, `done`), `replacedBy` (issue numbers), `closed` (`not_planned`, `other` or `open`) and `closedAt`. All are optional.
  - **Rules.** `replaceState(session)` gives `undefined` (no source, or the draft that stands for the issue is not split), `"done"` (`replacedBy` set), `"due"` (every part of the split draft is on GitHub) or `"waiting"`. `replaceStarted(session)` is true when `replacing` exists or a part of the marked draft is on GitHub. `mergeJournal(stored, found)` is pure: an entry that is not done and has no `rangeAfter` takes `title`, `byHand`, `before` and `after` from the fresh scan; an entry with `rangeAfter` or `done` is kept as it is; an entry no longer found and without `rangeAfter` is dropped; new entries are added, ordered by number. It never returns more than 1,000 entries, and says `cut: true` when some did not fit.
  - **Store.** `recordReplacing` (merged journal; `cut` stays once true), `recordDependantWrite` (write evidence of one entry), `recordDependantDone` (`outcome` and `done` in one write) and `recordReplaced` (sets `replacedBy` from the journal's parts, `closed` and `closedAt`, removes `replacing`, adds one `issue-replaced` log line). They work only while the session is being published, like `recordPendingUpdate`. `markOf` moved to `replace-journal.ts` and is still exported from `store.ts`.
  - **Compatibility.** A stored file without the new fields loads as before; import, draft removal and publish are unchanged. An older build rejects a session file that has the new fields (as with `pending`).
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/replace-journal.ts` (new), `src/refinement/store.ts`. Tests: `tests/refinement-replace-journal.test.ts` (new).
- Skill planning 4 — handle missing and conflicting capabilities explicitly (#175).
  - **Behaviour.** After the plan gate the run resolves the skills of the plan's `SKILL_REQUEST` (and `skills.selection.include`). A skill that cannot be used stops the run (`stopped`) before the next step; the reason lists each skill with its reason code and an actionable message. A run with no request and no `include`, and older run files, are unchanged.
  - **Config.** New `skills.unresolved`: `unknown`, `missing`, `untrusted`, `conflict`, `oversized` (each `stop` (default) or `warn`) and `high_risk` (terms; default migration(s), security, messaging, kafka, rabbitmq, amqp, queue, outbox). Reject codes map to kinds in `UNRESOLVED_KIND` (`src/skills/resolve-rules.ts`).
  - **Risk.** High-risk skills (id, category or capability equals a term, whole hyphen-separated words; or `risk: high`) always stop. `medium` always stops; only `low` can warn. Mandatory skills that fail also stop.
  - **Record.** The decision is saved in `run.skillPlan` (action, selected, unresolved, warnings, reason, gate, time, check count; optional in `RunSummary`). Warnings are logged as `⚠ …`.
  - **Resume.** Resuming at the gate re-resolves, so installing, approving or pinning a skill lets the run go on. If the stored request cannot be read, the run stays stopped.
  - **Resolver.** `SkillDecision` has a new `cause` field for dependency failures.
  - **Not yet.** The `issue-plan` to `issue-code-daily` handoff still ends the plan run as succeeded with a recorded stop. The stop reason is not shown on the run page, in the UI or in GitHub comments.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/unresolved.ts` and `src/skills/run-plan.ts` (new), `src/skills/resolve-rules.ts`, `schema.ts`, `request.ts`, `resolve.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `src/engine/state.ts`, `src/engine/runner.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-unresolved.test.ts`, `tests/skill-gate-run.test.ts` (new), `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`, `tests/deliver.test.ts`, `tests/gitflow.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Missing and conflicting skills".
- Skill planning 3 — resolve requests under policy and context budgets (#174).
  - **Resolver.** `resolveSkills(registry, requested, opts)` in `src/skills/resolve.ts` (new) is pure and never throws for a bad request. It maps ids to the active version, checks each with `selectSkill` (so only approved, pinned, unchanged skills pass), adds declared dependencies first (sorted by id, depth limit 32), and returns `selected` (id, version, digest, reason, `requiredBy`, `estimatedTokens`), reason-coded `decisions` and the total `estimatedTokens`.
  - **Rules.** Mandatory ids first, then requested ids sorted by id; each skill is admitted with its new dependencies or not at all. Refusal codes: `excluded`, `unknown`, `unapproved`, `unpinned`, `mismatch`, `unverified`, `role`, `too-large`, `dependency-cycle`, `dependency-unavailable`, `dependency-version`, `conflict`, `over-count`, `over-budget`, `blocked`. A conflict keeps the earlier skill. A refused mandatory skill, or an invalid or too long include/exclude list, blocks the whole selection (`ok: false`, nothing selected), so mandatory skills are never dropped silently.
  - **Config.** New `skills.selection`: `max_skills` (default 6, 1–20), `max_skill_tokens` (5000), `max_tokens` (15000), `include` (mandatory ids) and `exclude`. `catalogue.exclude` also applies. The config is refused for duplicates, an id in both `include` and `exclude`, `include` longer than `max_skills`, or an `include` id in `catalogue.exclude`. Defaults and codes are in `src/skills/resolve-rules.ts` (new).
  - **Helpers.** `compareSkillVersions` (SemVer order) in `src/skills/schema.ts`; `skillContextTokens`; `resolveOptionsFrom(config.skills)`. `findSkill` and `selectSkill` now accept a smaller registry type.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing calls the resolver from a run yet (no run record, runner wiring or owner-pin source); flows and older run files are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/resolve.ts`, `src/skills/resolve-rules.ts`, `src/skills/schema.ts`, `src/skills/registry.ts`, `src/config.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-resolve.test.ts` (new), `tests/skill-schema.test.ts`, `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`.
- Visual system 3 — theme and density switches (#324). A new **Appearance** button next to **Change password** (admin and user display) opens a dialog with Theme (System, Light, Dark) and Density (Comfortable, Compact). A choice applies at once and is kept in `localStorage` (key `scf.prefs`, `v: 1`) per account id, with the last choice as fallback and at most 20 accounts. `ui/prefs-boot.js` (a classic script loaded before the stylesheets) sets `data-theme` and `data-density` before the page is drawn, also on the sign-in form. The chosen option is marked by `aria-pressed` and a check icon. Missing, blocked or broken storage falls back to System and Comfortable. Nothing is sent to the server; no new route.
- UI quality 4b — performance budgets checked in a real browser (#399).
  - **Behaviour.** `npm run test:ui` now also runs `tests/browser/performance.spec.ts`, one test per budget (13 budgets, 17 tests). Each measure is taken three times in fresh browser contexts (1440 px, admin) and the median is checked. Measured: render time on load for Home, Runs and Board; one click from Board to Runs; layout shift on load (Home, Runs, Board); layout shift, redraw time and number of requests across one 30 s polling tick (Home and Runs); and large data (500 runs, a 5,000 line log, a 2,000 line diff). The tests run in their own Playwright project `performance`, after all other specs and in one worker, so other tests do not skew the times. It is skipped when a functional spec fails. Run it alone with `npx playwright test --config tests/browser/playwright.config.ts --project performance --no-deps`.
  - **Second server.** The large data lives on a second seeded server (`UI_TEST_LARGE=1`), started next to the default one by `global-setup.ts`; `seed("large")` returns its address. The default seed is unchanged.
  - **Budgets.** Defined in `tests/browser/budgets.ts`, each with a reason. Fixed targets: layout shift on load 0.1, across a polling tick 0.05, at most 200 rows in the Runs table, one request per polling tick. Time limits: three times the measured median, rounded up to the next 500 ms, never above the cap (2,000 ms; 3,000 ms for the large-data measures). The numbers are in `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md`.
  - **Known failures.** A budget that is over is a `test.fixme` entry in `KNOWN` (still measured, not asserted): layout shift on load for Home, Runs and Board (0.137, follow-up: UI quality 5 — no layout shift when the page shell loads) and the 5,000 line log (5993 ms against 3000 ms, follow-up: UI quality 6 — draw a long live log in one step).
  - **Compatibility.** No production code change. `npm test` does not run browser tests and passes with no browser installed.
  - **Code.** `tests/browser/budgets.ts` (new), `perf.ts` (new), `performance.spec.ts` (new), `seed.ts` (`startSeeded({ large })`, `seedLarge`, `largeFromEnv`), `server.ts`, `global-setup.ts`, `helpers.ts` (`seed(which)`, `openAs` option `prepare`), `playwright.config.ts`, `tests/fixtures/fake-git.sh` (the server's own git calls run without the fake's variables). Tests: `tests/browser-seed.test.ts`, `tests/ui-budgets-doc.test.ts` (new).
  - **Docs.** Still to do: `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md` ("Performance budgets"), `docs/ui-redesign/README.md` and `docs/DESIGN.md` §16.
- UI CSS architecture 2 — utility classes, and no inline styles on the administration pages (#338).
  - **Utilities.** `ui/css/utilities.css` now has named classes for stacking (`.stack`, `.row.tight`), spacing (`.mt-*`, `.mb-*`, `.mx-12`, `.px-12`, `.flush`), width (`.fit`, `.full`, `.w-70/80/90`, `.maxw-520`), text size (`.text-sm/xs/2xs/3xs`) and text flow (`.pre-wrap`, `.wrap-anywhere`, `.break-all`, `.select-all`, `.block`, `.span-all`). Each has the same values as the inline style it replaces, so no pixel changes. Text sizes use the type tokens.
  - **Pages.** `admin.js`, `models.js`, `watcher-form.js`, `admin-repos.js`, `users.js`, `problems.js` and `monitor.js` have no inline style left. `dashboard.js` keeps only the chart tip position and the rate bar width, which come from data. The four show/hide toggles in `watcher-form.js` set `hidden`.
  - **`[hidden]`.** `ui/css/reset.css` has `[hidden] { display: none !important; }`, so `hidden` also works on elements with `display: grid`.
  - **Auth dialog.** Removed the unused `class: "stack"` in `ui/auth.js`, so the new `.stack` does not change that dialog.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-inline-styles.test.ts` lists every `style:` and `.style.` in `ui/**/*.js`. It fails for the cleaned files unless the line is on a short allow-list with a reason, and fails when a used utility class is missing. `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` compares the "Inline styles" table in `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md` with the code, with no minimum.
  - **Docs.** `docs/DESIGN.md`: "UI styles" lists the utility classes and the rule "inline style only for values computed from data".
- Skills foundation 3 — pinned versions, trust and integrity (#168).
  - **Digest.** Every loaded package has a `digest`: `sha256:` plus 64 hex digits (`SKILL_DIGEST_RE`). It is the SHA-256 of the header line `scf-skill-package-v1\n`, then each file sorted by path: path length (4 bytes, big-endian), path, content length (4 bytes, big-endian), raw content. Raw bytes are hashed, so any change (also whitespace) changes the digest. File modes, empty folders and `.DS_Store` are not covered.
  - **Trust.** Each entry has `trust`: data folder and `skills.roots` are `approved`, built-in is `builtin`, repository is `unapproved` and can never be pinned.
  - **Lock file.** `skills.lock.json` in the data folder (mode 0600, written with the guarded store code and `auth.lock`): `{ "version": 1, "pins": { "id@version": { "digest", "pinnedAt" } } }`. Discovery and health only read it and never create it. A pin with the same digest is left untouched (no rewrite).
  - **Pin states.** `pinned` (digest matches), `unpinned` (no pin), `mismatch` (the content changed since the pin) and `unverified` (the lock cannot be read). A pin follows the id, version and digest, not the folder, so moving a package between sources or reordering `skills.roots` keeps it.
  - **Gate.** `selectSkill(registry, id, version)` returns an entry only for an exact, pinned `id@version`. It never falls back to another version, and throws `unknown`, `unpinned`, `unverified` or `SkillIntegrityError`. Nothing calls it yet.
  - **Commands.** `scf skills` now shows trust, pin state and digest. New: `scf skills pin <id@version> <digest> [--replace]`, `scf skills pin --builtin` (exits 1 when a built-in changed since its pin) and `scf skills unpin <id@version>`. The command refuses options and operands it does not know (`--replace` only with `pin <key> <digest>`, `--builtin` only with `pin`); `--repo` still works.
  - **Server start.** `scf serve` pins built-in skills that have no pin before the banner (`pinned the built-in skills: …`). It never replaces a pin. If that fails (for example a corrupt lock) it prints a warning and starts anyway.
  - **Health.** New problem kinds `integrity` (id, version, source, label, expected and actual digest) and `lock` (`skill lock`, no path). They sort first, then root problems, then package errors. The reason cap is 400 characters.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing selects skills yet; flows and runs are unchanged. Existing installs get no lock file until a pin is made or the server starts.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/schema.ts`, `src/skills/package.ts` (`skillDigest`), `src/skills/lock.ts` (new), `src/skills/registry.ts` (`selectSkill`, `pinSkill`, `pinBuiltinSkills`, `pinBuiltinSkillsAtStart`), `src/skills/cli.ts` (new), `src/cli.ts`, `src/server/health.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-package.test.ts`, `tests/skill-lock.test.ts` (new, includes the data-folder move cases), `tests/skill-cli.test.ts` (new), `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`, `tests/health.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Pinned versions and integrity", the catalogue wording, the command table, the health line. Threat model: "Skill integrity".
- UI quality 4a — visual regression: screenshots across themes, densities, roles and widths that fail with image differences (#398).
  - **Behaviour.** `npm run test:ui` now also compares screenshots with committed baselines (`toHaveScreenshot`). Pages: the gallery stand-in (`docs/ui-redesign/visual-system-demo.html`, opened from disk — no component gallery exists yet), admin Home, Board, Runs, a failed run, Repositories, Administration > Users, user Home, My runs, a run, a Refinement session, and the sign-in page. Each is taken in light/dark × default/compact at 1440 px, plus light/default at 360, 768 and 1024 px. The matrix is defined once in `tests/browser/visual-matrix.ts`; a vitest unit test checks it.
  - **Failing.** A changed area above `MAX_DIFF_PIXEL_RATIO` (one constant in `playwright.config.ts`) fails the test, and expected, actual and diff images go to `tests/browser/test-results/`. A harness test proves it with an injected style change.
  - **Baselines.** Committed under `tests/browser/__screenshots__/{platform}/`; only `darwin` for now. On other platforms the visual spec is skipped with a message. Update with `npm run test:ui -- --update-snapshots`. Stories that change the look on purpose update them and list the changed images in the pull request.
  - **Helpers.** `openAs` takes an options argument (theme, density, fixed clock); new `openUrl`. Theme and density are set on `<html>` before page scripts run. The seeded hold run sleeps longer so the live running run lasts through long test runs.
  - **Known.** Admin baselines at 360 and 768 px show the known sideways scroll; refresh them when #264 part 2 fixes it.
  - **Compatibility.** No production code change. `npm test` does not run browser tests and passes with no browser installed.
- Refinement 9f — Backlog readiness view per repository (#336).
  - **Behaviour.** `GET /api/refinement/backlog?repo=owner/name` lists the open issues of one of your GitHub repositories that the Foundry is not building and has not built (the rule of "Refine an existing issue"), newest first, with number, title and link. Each issue has four checks done by code only: acceptance criteria, a value sentence ("As …, I want …, so that …"), every issue named under "Depends on" exists (`missing` and `unmatched` say which were not found), and no open questions (an "Open questions" heading with content, or an unchecked question item). An issue with an open session of yours carries its `session` id. `cut` is true when GitHub's page of 100 issues and pull requests was full, so older open issues may be missing. The repository is read with its sign-in. No architect run starts, nothing is submitted to the scheduler, nothing is written to GitHub and no label changes; the watcher is unchanged.
  - **UI.** The Refinement page has a **Backlog readiness** view (`#/refinement/backlog`): a repository chooser, the list with a ✓ or ✗ per check, and per issue **Refine** (starts a session from the issue and opens it) or **Open session**. When GitHub cannot be read, it says so and points to the repository's sign-in. Not shown in a preview.
  - **Errors and permission.** Only the owner of the repository can read the list (an admin who is not the owner: 403). The route matches before `refinement/:id`.
  - **Compatibility.** New route and view only; existing sessions, routes and the watcher are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/backlog.ts` (`quickChecks`, `dependencyRefs`), `src/server/api-refinement-backlog.ts` (route), `src/server/server.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule), `src/refinement/store.ts`, `src/github.ts`, `ui/refinement-backlog.js`, `ui/refinement.js`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/ia.js`. Tests: `tests/refinement-backlog.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-backlog-api.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-backlog.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ia.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table and "Backlog readiness"; `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` (moved line number of the drop confirm).
- UI quality 4c — the five baseline tasks: an automated effort check, and the script and result table for the owner (#400).
  - **Behaviour.** `tests/browser/journeys.spec.ts` (`npm run test:ui`) walks the five tasks of `docs/ui-redesign/measurement.md` in the real UI with the seeded data. A small helper (`tests/browser/journey.ts`) wraps click, fill and goto and counts navigation steps, clicks and typed fields; it also fails on a page change or dialog that the task did not declare. The test fails when a count is above the baseline, kept as data in `tests/browser/journeys-baseline.ts`.
  - **Open gates.** Task 1 (Start work) takes 2 / 3 / 1 against a baseline of 1 / 2 / 1 and is `test.fixme` with a named follow-up. Task 2 (Approve a waiting run) is `test.fixme` because the approve button needs a run started by a watcher for a GitHub issue. Both block turning the redesign on by default. The redesign counts were read from the UI code and are not yet confirmed by a browser run.
  - **Docs.** New `docs/ui-redesign/usability-check.md`: who, what to say, set-up, the five tasks with end states, what counts as an error, the confidence and clarity questions, a result table with baseline and redesign side by side (effort filled from the automated counts, people columns empty), the pass rule, and a link to open the follow-up issue for the owner (no `Factory_go`). Turning the redesign on by default waits for that result. Listed in `docs/ui-redesign/README.md`.
  - **Compatibility.** Test and docs only; no production code, no new dependency. The results table in `measurement.md` is unchanged.
  - **Code.** `tests/browser/` (`journey.ts`, `journeys-baseline.ts`, `journeys.spec.ts`, `helpers.ts`, `seed.ts`). Test: `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` (baseline against `measurement.md`).
- UI quality 1a — a browser test harness, `npm run test:ui`, and a navigation overflow check at four widths (#367).
  - **Behaviour.** Playwright (`@playwright/test`, new dev dependency) runs the web UI in a real browser against the real server, started on a free port with a temporary data folder, one admin, one user and seeded data (a run in each main status, a repository, a refinement session, a watcher). Install the browser once with `npx playwright install chromium`. `npm test` does not run these tests and passes with no browser installed.
  - **Helpers.** `WIDTHS` (360, 768, 1024, 1440) in one place; `openAs(browser, role, width)` opens a signed-in page; `expectNoSidewaysScroll(page)` fails when the document is wider than the viewport. The navigation spec checks every primary link on `/` and `/user/` at all four widths (through the menu button and drawer at 360 px).
  - **Known failures.** Admin display at 360 and 768 px shows sideways scroll; those two cases are `test.fixme`, to be fixed in UI quality 1b (part 2 of #264).
  - **Compatibility.** No production code change.
  - **Code.** `tests/browser/` (`playwright.config.ts`, `global-setup.ts`, `server.ts`, `seed.ts`, `helpers.ts`, `widths.ts`, `harness.spec.ts`, `navigation.spec.ts`), `package.json` (`test:ui`), `.gitignore`. Test: `tests/browser-seed.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** Design: "Tests".
- Skill planning 2 — a structured skill request in final plans (#173).
  - **Behaviour.** A READY plan now ends with one line `SKILL_REQUEST: {"version":1,"skills":[…]}` naming the skills the coder needs (`id`, `reason`, 1–5 `evidence` entries of the form `catalogue:…`, `issue:…` or `path:…`; at most 20 skills, each id once). The plan review checks it, and a revised plan writes it again and replaces the draft's line (if the revision is not finished, the draft's line is used). `risk_gate` (in `issue-deliver` and `issue-gitflow`) and `post_plan` (in `issue-plan`) check the line with the new tool `tools/skill-request`, add a "Required skills" section to the posted plan (`None.` for an empty request, `None — the plan did not name any.` when the line is missing) and end their output with the canonical line. The line is removed from the notes, send-back and split comments and from created split issues; the coder-facing plan gets no skills section. A line in the issue or its comments is never copied. NEEDS_INFO, NOT_CODE, TOO_BIG and forced-split plans carry no request.
  - **Failure.** A line that is present but not valid fails the gate with "planning failed: the skill request of the plan is not valid" before any coding; nothing is posted. Resuming does not help; start the run again. A missing line is accepted as an empty request.
  - **Limits.** The `risk_gate` step in `issue-deliver` and `issue-gitflow` keeps 60,000 characters of output instead of 20,000, so a maximum request does not cut the plan.
  - **Compatibility.** One extra output line; no config, API or stored format change. Older runs have no line and read as "no request". `planSkillRequest` returns `undefined` for them, and when planning went on after the gate. Nothing loads the requested skills yet, and no skill catalogue is sent to the planner yet, so requests are empty in practice until a later story does that. `issue-code-daily` is unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/request.ts` (schema, `parseSkillRequestLine`, `planSkillRequest`), `tools/skill-request` (the same rules in plain JS; the two must stay equal), `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (prompts, `CHECK_SKILLS`, gates, strips), regenerated `flows/issue-plan.yaml`, `flows/issue-gitflow.yaml` and `tests/fixtures/flows/issue-deliver.yaml`, `tools/create-split`, `src/failure.ts`, `src/engine/guards.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-request.test.ts`, `tests/deliver.test.ts`, `tests/pipeline.test.ts`, `tests/gitflow.test.ts`, `tests/failure.test.ts`; `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` injects the line (`FAKE_SKILL_REQUEST`).
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Skill request" and the unreadable markers list.
- Refinement 9d — the issue changed on GitHub: show both versions and ask (#334).
  - **Behaviour.** Before updating an issue, publish reads it again. When its title or text differs from what the session remembered (line ends and surrounding white space are ignored), nothing is written and the answer is 409 with `changedOnGithub`: the GitHub version, the version the Foundry would write, and `seen` (a hash of the GitHub version). `GET /api/refinement/:id/publish` reports the same `changedOnGithub` in the plan. A change of labels or comments alone does not ask. The body of `POST /api/refinement/:id/publish` accepts `source: { keep: "mine" | "github", seen }`. **Keep mine** publishes only when `seen` still matches GitHub, else it asks again with the newer versions; the replaced GitHub version is the one folded into the comment. **Keep GitHub's** writes nothing, creates no issues, stores the GitHub version as the session's `source` (log kind `source-refreshed`) and leaves the draft unpublished.
  - **Errors.** 409 with `changedOnGithub` as above; 409 for Keep GitHub's while an unfinished update is pending; 400 for a choice when no draft updates an issue; 502 when GitHub gives no valid `updated_at` for Keep GitHub's.
  - **Compatibility.** `source` is optional; sessions and clients that do not send it work as before. Error bodies may now carry extra fields (`HttpError`).
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/publish.ts` (`versionHash`, `sameVersion`, `parseSourceChoice`), `src/refinement/store.ts` (`refreshSource`), `src/server/api-refinement-publish.ts`, `src/server/http.ts`, `src/server/server.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `ui/api.js`, `ui/refinement-publish.js`, `ui/refinement.js`. Tests: `tests/refinement-publish-changed-api.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-publish-changed.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-publish.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-draft.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-publish-update-api.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table and "Publishing on the session page".
- Refinement 7f — after the split on the draft page (#314). The original shows "Split into …" with a button per part, is read-only and lists the criteria that fit nowhere. A part shows "Part n of m of …", the architect's hint and its two warnings. Criteria have "Move to …" (unsaved text is saved first), and a draft has "Merge with …" with a confirmation. The depends-on list does not offer later parts. The log has plain sentences for `criterion-moved` and `drafts-merged`. New `ui/refinement-parts.js`, `moveCriterion` and `mergeDrafts` in `ui/api.js`, and a merge row in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md`.
- Accessibility 1 — automated check helper and accessible shared primitives (#385).
  - **Behaviour.** Dialogs are named by their heading (`aria-labelledby`). The toast is a live region: errors are announced at once (`role="alert"`), other messages politely (`role="status"`). Fields and chart bars show the focus mark for keyboard use (`:focus-visible`). The spinner and the toast and chart-tip transitions stop under "reduce motion". Buttons and links in tables are at least 24 × 24 px. On a screen up to 760 px wide the top bar wraps and a wide table scrolls in its own box, so the page does not scroll sideways at 320 px. The sidebar drawer closes from its links and the scrim takes a mouse press instead of a click, so no custom click target is left without keyboard support.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/helpers/a11y.ts`: `audit(root)` checks a fake DOM tree (`control-name`, `field-label`, `click-needs-key`, `no-positive-tabindex`, `img-name`, `dialog-name`, `focusable-hidden`); `auditHtml(source)` checks an HTML file. `data-a11y-skip="stop"` exempts a click listener that only stops propagation.
  - **Compatibility.** No server or API change. The dialog no longer has `aria-label`. The hidden health button in `ui/index.html` has the text "Health" until the page fills it.
  - **Code.** `ui/dom.js`, `ui/shell.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/user/index.html`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: `tests/ui-a11y.test.ts`, `tests/ui-dom.test.ts`, `tests/ui-shell.test.ts`, `tests/ui-visual-system.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-suggest.test.ts`.

- Runs list 3a — a reversible archive mark on runs (server) (#376).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/runs/:id/archive` marks a finished run (succeeded, failed, cancelled or stopped) and answers 200 with `{ runId, archived: true }`; `POST /api/runs/:id/unarchive` removes the mark and answers `archived: false`. Both answer 409 for a run that is running, waiting, queued or active, and 404 for an unknown run. A repeat call is 200 and changes nothing. A user can do this for own runs only (another account's run is 404); an admin for any run. The mark is shared, one per run. Nothing is deleted.
  - **List.** `GET /api/runs` leaves archived runs out; `GET /api/runs?archived=1` returns only archived runs. The choice is applied before the 200-run cap, and the admin's `?owner=` still works with it. Any other value of `archived` is 400. `GET /api/runs/:id` has `archivedAt` for both roles; `archivedBy` is for admins only. The event stream, diff and transcript work as before. Resume, retry, approve, reject and answer remove the mark. Board, Your turn, "Since you last looked", statistics, the monitor, `POST /api/clean` and the watchers treat an archived run like any other.
  - **Compatibility.** A `run.json` without `archivedAt` is read as not archived; no migration. The mark is written with a temp file and rename, and the file time is kept, so `updatedAt` does not change.
  - **Audit.** New actions `run-archive` and `run-unarchive` (also written for a repeat call).
  - **Code.** `src/engine/state.ts` (`setRunArchived`, `archivedAt`, `archivedBy`, `RunBrief.archived`), `src/engine/runner.ts` (`resumeRun` clears the mark), `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`setArchived`), `src/server/api-runs.ts` (routes, list filter), `src/server/user-view.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts` (two `own` rules), `src/auth/audit.ts`, `ui/audit.js`. Tests: `tests/run-archive.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/run-ownership.test.ts`, `tests/user-view.test.ts`, `tests/ui-audit.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table, "When something goes wrong" and the audit actions table.
- Work tracking 2a — Work page as a list, with filters and saved display choices, behind a setting (#373).
  - **Behaviour.** New install-wide setting `ui.redesign` (default `false`). Off: `#/board` and `#/board/:repo` draw the old board, unchanged. On: the same addresses draw the new Work page (`#/board/:repo` starts with that repository as the filter). The page reads only `GET /api/board`; status, column, sentence and blockers come from the server. Filters: repository, owner, status (any of the nine columns), who has the next move, and a text box for title and `#issue`. Group by status (default), repository, owner, next move or none; order by issue number (default), age or title. Done stays newest first when grouped by status and ordered by issue number. Rows show issue link, title, repository, status, next move, open blockers, owner and age. A row with a run opens `#/runs/<id>` on click and Enter. Group headings show counts; "N of M stories" shows when a filter hides some; an empty result offers **Clear filters**. The list refreshes every 5 s (one request at a time, redraws only on change) and checks the watcher when you return from a GitHub link. A load error with no data shows an error box and **Retry**.
  - **Saved choices.** Filters, grouping and order are kept per account in the browser (`scf.work.<account id>`). Bad or missing storage gives the defaults; a saved repository or owner that no longer exists is dropped.
  - **API.** `GET /api/info` returns `redesign: true|false`. `GET /api/board` now sets `empty` whenever there are no cards, also when repositories are configured (the repositories stay in the answer). No new route.
  - **Compatibility.** New optional config key `ui.redesign`; existing `config.yaml` files load without it and are not rewritten. An unknown key under `ui` is refused. A change of the setting applies after a page reload. `ui/board.js` is unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/config.ts`, `src/board.ts`, `src/server/api-admin.ts`, `ui/app.js` (route switch), `ui/admin.js` (Settings checkbox), new `ui/work.js`, `ui/work-model.js`, `ui/work-prefs.js`, `ui/work-list.js`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: `tests/ui-work.test.ts`, `tests/ui-work-model.test.ts`, `tests/ui-work-prefs.test.ts`, extended `tests/schema.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts`, `tests/board.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`. Four `ui/app.js` line numbers in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` were shifted. The implementing session had no complete full-suite pass; the flow's build and test step gives the result.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Work page (redesign, off by default)".
- UI redesign navigation 3a — shareable URL filters on Runs and Board (#350).
  - **Behaviour.** Addresses take an optional query: `#/runs?repo=owner%2Fname&owner=<accountId>`. Known keys are `repo` and `owner`; unknown keys and bad values (repository not `owner/name`, owner not `[\w-]+`) are dropped. The Board keeps `#/board/<repo>` and gains `?owner=`; its repository buttons keep the owner. Admin Runs: `repo` shows only runs and queued jobs of that repository (case-insensitive); `owner` replaces the in-memory owner filter and still calls `api.runs(owner)`; changing the owner select rewrites the address with `history.replaceState`. My runs honours `repo` and ignores `owner`, so a shared admin address with another account's `owner` shows only the user's own runs and makes no refused request. A filter bar above the list shows one chip per filter with a remove button, and **Clear filters** (not drawn without a filter). A filter with no match shows an empty state that names the filter and offers **Clear filters**. With a repository filter, when the server answer is at the 200-run cap, a note says "Only the newest 200 runs are searched." Back/Forward restore the filter and the 30-second refresh keeps it. The sidebar `aria-current` compares the path without the query.
  - **Compatibility.** No server change, no new route, no new API parameter. Addresses without a query resolve as before.
  - **Code.** New `ui/filters.js` (`parseQuery`, `formatQuery`, `splitHash`, `withQuery`, `sameRepo`, `filterBar`); `ui/ia.js` (`resolve` returns `query`), `ui/auth.js` (`isUserHash`, `userPage`), `ui/app.js`, `ui/user/app.js`, `ui/runs.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`, `ui/board.js`, `ui/shell.js`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: new `tests/ui-filters.test.ts`; extended `tests/ui-ia.test.ts`, `tests/ui-board.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user.test.ts`, `tests/ui-auth.test.ts` and others. The full suite did not finish in the implementing session; the flow's build and test step gives the result.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Filters in the address". `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md`: line numbers of `ui/runs.js` updated.
- Administration 8a — sectioned secondary navigation, admin repositories and a Maintenance page (#362).
  - **Behaviour.** **All repositories** and **Credentials** move from Repositories to Administration (addresses `#/all-repos` and `#/credentials` are unchanged; the sidebar highlights **Administration**). The Administration row is grouped under labels: **Operations** (Problems, Watchers, Models, Dashboard), **People and access** (Users, All repositories, Credentials, Audit) and **System** (Settings, Maintenance). Flows keeps a flat row. New page **Maintenance** (`#/maintenance`) holds the Disk clean-up (Preview and **Clean up**, same call, confirm text and result line); Settings no longer shows it and Save sends the same body. The user display redirects `#/maintenance`, `#/all-repos` and `#/credentials` to `#/runs`.

Tests: new `tests/ui-maintenance.test.ts`, updated `tests/ui-ia.test.ts`, `tests/ui-shell.test.ts`, `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user.test.ts` and other UI tests. `docs/ui-redesign/inventory.md`, `dialogs.md` and `prototypes.md` list the new route and line numbers.

- Run detail 4a — bounded log, diff and transcript rendering on both run pages (#391).
  - **Behaviour.** The live log keeps at most 2,000 lines in the page; older lines are dropped from the top and a line "N earlier lines are not shown" appears above the log. Lines that arrive together are added in one batch per animation frame. The log scrolls to the end only when the reader is at the end; dropping old lines does not move what a reader who scrolled up is looking at. **Changes** shows the stat block and one closed section per file; the lines of a file are built when it is opened. A file with more than 1,500 lines shows the first 1,500 and a "Show all N lines" button. A diff of one file is shown opened; "Diff truncated (very large)." and the empty text stay. A transcript shows the first 200 events and a "Show 200 more" button; a tool result longer than 20,000 characters is cut, with a "Show all" button. A user still gets no transcript and no step output.
  - **Compatibility.** UI only; no server change, no new dependency. Layout and tabs are unchanged. `ui/runs.js` still exports `diffView` and `logLine`. After a reload the log shows only the last 2,000 lines, because the server replays the whole log.
  - **Code.** New `ui/run-output.js` (`createLog`, `diffView`, `splitPatch`, `transcriptView`, `logLine`), `ui/runs.js`, `ui/user/runs.js`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: `tests/ui-run-output.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user.test.ts`. Line numbers in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` updated.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "A run".
- Refinement 7d — merge two drafts of a session back into one (server) (#312).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/merge` with `{ "with": draftId }` merges the second draft into the first, removes the second and answers 200 with the session. No architect run. Title, who, what and why of the first are kept; an empty one is taken from the second with its source. Criteria of the second follow those of the first (a clashing id gets a new id). Out of scope and notes: equal texts are kept once, different texts are joined with an empty line, and the source is `typed` when both were typed, else `accepted-edited`. Depends-on is the union without the two drafts; other drafts that depended on the second now depend on the first. Waiting suggestions, review, impact, split proposal and readiness check of the merged draft are cleared; rejected suggestions of both are kept (newest 30, the first draft's before the second's). When the second was a part of a split, `splitInto` of the original is updated. After a merge no part depends on a later part of its split: such a link is removed and named in the log detail (cut at 120 characters). The log gets `drafts-merged`.
  - **Errors.** 400 for a missing `with`, a draft merged with itself, more than 50 criteria, more than 20 depends-on, or joined out-of-scope or notes text that is too long; 404 for an unknown draft; 409 for a split original (either draft) or a draft already on GitHub. Only the owner can merge (another user gets 404, an admin who is not the owner 403).
  - **Compatibility.** New route only; existing sessions, drafts and clients are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/draft-parts.ts` (`mergeDrafts`), `src/refinement/draft.ts` (exports used by the merge), `src/refinement/store.ts` (`mergeDraftsOf`), `src/server/api-refinement.ts` (route), `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule). Tests: `tests/refinement-merge.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-split-api.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table and "Merging two drafts".
- Skill planning 1 — a ranked, bounded skill catalogue for planners (#172).
  - **Behaviour.** New `buildSkillCatalogue(registry, { profile, task, modules, include, exclude, limits })` turns the active registry entries into a short list a planner can choose from. Each entry has exactly five fields: `id`, `version`, `description` (cut to 160 characters), `capabilities` (at most 6) and `evidence` (at most 2 short lines). The SKILL.md body and package files are never read or included. Ranking uses the repository profile (language, platform, interface and database candidates, limited to the relevant `modules`) and whole-word matches of skill names in the task text; the task text itself never reaches the output.
