Order: pinned first, then score, then id, then version, so identical inputs give identical output.
  - **Limits.** Defaults are 20 candidates and 2,000 estimated tokens (4 bytes per token). Over the token limit, the lowest-ranked unpinned entries are dropped first; then entries are shortened (`compact`, `minimal`), lowest-ranked first. Truncation is visible: `omitted`, `truncated.count`, `truncated.tokens` and `truncated.shortened`, and the rendered header says how many skills were left out. Limits outside the allowed range are clamped (1–50 candidates, 100–20,000 tokens).
  - **Policy.** `include` pins skills: they are always kept and never dropped. `exclude` removes skills and wins over `include`. The result reports `pinned`, `excluded`, `missing` (not in the registry), `conflicts` and `invalid`. Pins that do not fit the limits throw `pinned skills do not fit the catalogue limits`. An empty registry gives an empty catalogue. `renderSkillCatalogue` writes one JSON line per skill, labelled as data, not instructions.
  - **Config.** New `skills.catalogue` with `max_candidates` (default 20), `max_tokens` (default 2000), `include` and `exclude` (lists of skill ids, default empty). The config is refused when ids repeat, when there are more pinned skills than `max_candidates`, or when the pinned skills cannot fit `max_tokens`. `catalogueOptionsFrom(config.skills)` maps it to the options. The settings are installation-wide, not per repository.
  - **Compatibility.** New optional config key only; existing configs, flows and runs are unchanged. Nothing sends the catalogue to a planner yet.
  - **Code.** `src/skills/catalogue.ts`, `src/skills/catalogue-rules.ts`, `src/config.ts`. Tests: `tests/skill-catalogue.test.ts`, `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Skill catalogue".
- Refinement 7e — "Split" on the draft page: the ways, change one or describe your own, confirm (#313).
  - **Behaviour.** A draft with at least 2 criteria has **Split**. It asks the architect and shows the run state like the other asks. Each way shows its cut, the stories with their sentence, what the first story delivers, the criteria per story, the criteria that fit nowhere and its warnings; "out of date" shows with **Ask again** when the draft changed. **Describe my own way** asks again with a short text. **Use this way** (or an empty plan) opens a plan where you rename, add and remove parts, move criteria (also to "Fits nowhere"), change the order and set depends-on. The plan names problems (a criterion in no list, a dependency on a later part, fewer than 2 parts) and **Confirm split** is off until it is right. Nothing is sent before **Confirm split**; afterwards the first new draft opens. The plan is kept in the page only. A split original opens as a read-only record. In "View as user" no button is shown and no changing call is made.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-split.js`; `ui/refinement-draft.js` (split box, read-only card), `ui/api.js` (`askSplit`, `confirmSplit`, both with the `stay` flag), `ui/refinement-talk.js` (kind `split`), `ui/refinement.js` (words for `split-asked`, `architect-split`, `draft-split`), `ui/refinement-ready.js` ("Split" pill), `ui/style.css`. Tests: new `tests/ui-refinement-split.test.ts`; extended `tests/ui-refinement-talk.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-architect.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-ready.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Splitting a draft"; `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` line numbers.
- Refinement 9c — publishing a session that came from an issue updates that issue (#333).
  - **Behaviour.** The draft that stands for the issue replaces the title and text of that issue; no copy is created. Other drafts are created as new issues and may depend on #N. The update goes first; if the marked draft depends on a draft that is not on GitHub yet, it waits and is updated on a later publish. Afterwards the Foundry adds one comment (who refined it, whether the title changed, which sections changed, and the old title and text folded in `<details>`). The new text ends with the same "Refined in …" note and hidden marker as a created issue, so publishing again writes nothing twice. Chosen labels are added, existing labels are kept, and the build label goes last. A closed issue, or one that is being built or built, is not written: the answer is 409 with the reason. A body that is now `null` counts as empty. If the old text plus the comment wrapper would not fit in a comment, publishing is refused with 409 before anything is written.
  - **Retry.** The exact old title and text are stored in the session before the update and cleared once it is recorded, so a retry after a failure between update and comment comments with the text that was really overwritten. A comment counts as ours only if its last non-empty line is the marker and it is from the publishing account. Old text reaches GitHub only as JSON on stdin.
  - **API.** `GET /api/refinement/:id/publish` shows `updates: N`, `willUpdate`, and `notChanged` (when nothing stands for the issue); `willCreate` is unchanged. The session view carries the mark as `source.draft`; the stored old text is never returned. Sessions made before this change get no 409: the marked draft is worked out from the log and drafts (the first draft, if it is not a split part and no draft was ever removed); otherwise the issue is not changed and the other drafts still publish as new issues. If the marked draft is dropped, the issue is not changed and the mark does not move.
  - **UI.** The publish part of the session page shows "Updates #N" for this item and, after publishing, the link to it.
  - **Code.** `src/github.ts` (`updateIssue`), `src/refinement/publish.ts`, `src/refinement/store.ts`, `src/refinement/issue-import.ts` (`splitSections` exported), `src/server/api-refinement-publish.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement-import.ts` (`whyBuilding`), `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule text), `ui/refinement-publish.js`. Tests: `tests/refinement-publish-update-api.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-publish.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-import-api.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-publish.test.ts`; `tests/fixtures/fake-gh.sh` handles the PATCH call.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the two route rows for the publish plan and publish.

- UI redesign workspace 1 — a focused Home for both displays (#256).
  - **Behaviour.** **Home** is the landing page. Admin: `#/home` has **Needs you** (the Your turn list, embedded), **Active**, the "since you last looked" strip, **Problems** (the health answer, without what Needs you already shows), **Recently completed** (closed) and **Metrics** (closed; loads when opened; links to the Dashboard). User: `/user/#/home` has Needs you, Active and Recently completed, and is always the page for an empty address; the old `homeHash` rule (Start work or My runs) is gone. The top shows the counts ("2 need you · 1 active") and one best action: the first item that needs you, then a problem, then the first active run, else **Start work**. Each row has one primary button and a **Details** link to the run; a section shows 5 rows and "+n more". With nothing yet, Home offers **Start work**; when nothing needs you it says "Nothing needs you."
  - **No new rules.** Rows and groups come from each run's next-step record (`run.next`, `job.next`), not from the run status. Users see no cost, model, health or other-account data. Each part of the admin Home loads alone, so one failing part leaves the others. Home asks again every 30 s (Your turn every 5 s).
  - **Compatibility.** No server change, no new route. `#/your-turn` still works and opens Home. The health line opens by itself only when the server does not answer; a problem turns the health chip red.
  - **Not done.** Ask SCF prompts have a slot at the bottom of Home (`askView`), but nothing fills it yet: the Ask SCF module (#350–#353) is not in this tree.
  - **Code.** New `ui/home.js` (`homeModel`, `bestAction`, `sectionView`, `workRow`, `renderHome`) and `ui/home-admin.js` (`renderAdminHome`); `ui/turn.js` (embedded mode), `ui/app.js`, `ui/since.js`, `ui/health.js`, `ui/ia.js`, `ui/auth.js`, `ui/user/app.js`, `ui/user/start.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/user/index.html`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: new `tests/ui-home.test.ts`, `tests/ui-home-admin.test.ts`; extended the UI tests for turn, ia, auth, user, user-start, health, since, and `tests/server.test.ts`. The full suite did not finish in the implementing session; the flow's build and test step gives the result.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Home". UI redesign notes `inventory.md` and `dialogs.md`.
- Refinement 9b — "Refine an existing issue" on the Refinement page (#332).
  - **Behaviour.** The Refinement page has a **Refine an existing issue** button. The dialog asks for a repository and an issue number, checks the number, and shows the server's reason (and stays open) when it refuses. The session page shows "From issue #N" with a link. When the issue has the build label, it shows a warning with **Remove the build label**; the label is removed only when the owner presses it. `POST /api/refinement/:id/source/remove-build-label` removes it with the repository's sign-in: 200 with the session; 404 for a session you cannot see; 403 for an admin who is not the owner; 409 for a dropped session, a session without a build label, or a repository on the server's own login; 502 when GitHub fails. The watcher's behaviour did not change.
  - **Compatibility.** New route; new log kind `source-label-removed`; old sessions and files are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `clearSourceBuildLabel` in `src/refinement/store.ts`, `removeBuildLabel` in `src/server/api-refinement-import.ts`, the route in `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, a rule in `src/server/permissions.ts`; new `ui/refinement-import.js`; `ui/refinement.js`, `ui/api.js`. Tests: `tests/refinement-source-label-api.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-source-label-store.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-import.test.ts`, `tests/repo-watchers-run.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
- Visual system 2 — one icon set, and status that does not depend on colour alone (#323).
  - **Behaviour.** Every status pill drawn by `statusMark` (runs, Your turn, watcher states) shows an icon before its text. The text is unchanged and still comes from the server; no status logic moved to the browser. The running spinner is now the `loader-circle` icon with class `spin` (it stops under reduced motion) instead of the inline-styled `.spinner` span. A failed tool call in a transcript shows a "Failed" icon, and a flow with errors in the sidebar shows a "Has errors" icon.
  - **Icons.** New `ui/icons.js` holds 19 Lucide icons as SVG path data (ISC licence note at the top; no new dependency). `icon(name, { label, small, spin })` returns an SVG with `currentColor`: `aria-hidden="true"` without a label, `role="img"` and `aria-label` with one; an unknown name throws. `iconButton(name, label, props)` refuses an empty label and sets `aria-label` and `title`. `STATUS` gives each of the nine semantics its own icon, `KIND_SEMANTIC` maps every `NextKind` to a semantic (presentation only), `statusPill` draws icon and text, `watcherSemanticOf` maps watcher states, `flowNameMark` draws a flow name.
  - **CSS.** `.ico`, `.ico-sm`, `.ico.spin`, and nine `.pill.sem-*` rules using the colour pairs of #322; the older `who`, `kind` and `state` pill rules come after them and keep today's colours. `sem-disabled` uses `--color-disabled`.
  - **Not checked.** The path data was written from memory and not compared with a Lucide release; it still needs a look in a browser. The demo page `visual-system-demo.html` was not changed.
  - **Code.** `ui/icons.js`, `ui/next.js` (`statusMark`, `nextStatus`), `ui/admin.js` (`watcherStateMark`), `ui/runs.js`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: new `tests/ui-icons.test.ts`; updated `tests/ui-next.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** New `docs/ui-redesign/visual-system.md` (icon list, semantic table, label rules); user guide section 3.
- Refinement 7c-5 — publishing skips split originals and finishes when every part is on GitHub (server) (#319).
  - **Behaviour.** The publish call creates no issue for a split original and never marks it published (`recordPublished` now refuses it with 409 `bad-state`). A draft that depended on the original is created after all its parts. The session becomes `published` when every draft without `splitInto` is on GitHub. A part on GitHub cannot be split again, removed, or lose a criterion by a move, so no criterion gets a second issue.
  - **API.** The answer of `POST /api/refinement/:id/publish` has `leftBehind: [{ draft, title, criteria }]` for split originals that still hold criteria, like the plan, also when nothing is created; the key is absent when there are none.
  - **Code.** `src/server/api-refinement-publish.ts`, `src/refinement/store.ts` (`recordPublished`), `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule text). Tests: `tests/refinement-publish-post-api.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-split.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table and "Publishing".
- UI redesign navigation 1 — a grouped application shell replaces the crowded header (#253).
  - **Behaviour.** Both displays get a sidebar and a compact top bar. Admin: groups **Work** (Home, Board, Refinement, Runs, Repositories) and **Setup** (Flows, Administration); the top bar has the menu button, the page title, **Start work**, a health chip and an account menu. The user display has **My runs**, **My repositories** and **Refinement** in the sidebar and **Start work** in the top bar. The waiting count shows on **Home** and on the menu button. The health chip opens or closes the health line (it stays open when health is bad). The sidebar can be collapsed (kept in `localStorage` as `scf-side`). Up to 760 px wide it is a modal drawer: Escape, the scrim, **Close menu** or a link closes it, Tab stays inside it and the page behind is `inert`. The top bar no longer scrolls sideways.
  - **Accessibility.** A **Skip to content** button. `aria-current` is `page` on the link of the exact address and `true` on the link of its area. `<main>` is focusable and named after the page; after a page change focus moves to it and a polite status region announces the page (a detail page adds its argument, such as `Run: 123`). Focus is not taken while a dialog has it.
  - **Compatibility.** No server change, no new route. Addresses, deep links and back/forward are unchanged; route cleanup and stale-response protections in `ui/app.js` and `ui/user/app.js` are kept (each calls `initShell` once after sign-in).
  - **Code.** `ui/shell.js` (`initShell`, `sideState`, `showPage`, `setCount`), `ui/ia.js` (`GROUPS`), `ui/health.js` (`showHealth`, the chip), new `ui/shell.css`, and `ui/index.html`, `ui/user/index.html`, `ui/style.css`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/user/app.js`. Tests: extended `tests/ui-shell.test.ts`, `tests/ui-ia.test.ts`, `tests/ui-health.test.ts`, `tests/ui-user.test.ts`, `tests/server.test.ts` and `tests/helpers/fake-dom.ts`. The final tree has had no complete full-suite pass in the implementing session; the flow's build and test step gives the result.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Navigation (admin display)" and the user display paragraph.
- UI CSS architecture 1 — `ui/style.css` is split into modules under `ui/css/` (#337).
  - **Behaviour.** No change in how any page looks. `ui/style.css` now holds only `@import url("/css/…")` lines (tokens, reset, layout, components, utilities, then `pages/shell`, `editor`, `runs`, `dashboard`, `turn`, `board`, `start`, `refinement`). Every rule has the same selector and declarations; rules that override each other keep their order inside one module. Variants of shared components (`.pill.who-you`, `.card.failure`, `.table.compact` …) are in `components.css`. The home page rules are in `dashboard.css`; the application shell rules are at the end of `pages/shell.css`.
  - **Tokens.** The token values stay in `ui/tokens.css` (linked before `/style.css`); `ui/css/tokens.css` only points to it. The z-index scale is `--layer-*` and the form and modal width is `--size-form`.
  - **Compatibility.** `/style.css`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/user/index.html` and the server are unchanged; `/css/…` files are served like `/style.css`, without sign-in.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/helpers/ui-css.ts` (`readUiCss()`), `tests/ui-css.test.ts` and `tests/fixtures/ui-style-baseline.css`; the tests that read the stylesheet now use the helper.
  - **Docs.** `docs/DESIGN.md`: new section "UI styles".
- UI states 1 — shared loading, empty, error, permission and stale components (#342).
  - **Behaviour.** New `ui/states.js` with `explainError`, `loadingState`, `emptyState`, `errorState`, `permissionState` and `staleNote`. `explainError` gives a `kind` (`offline`, `permission`, `missing`, `conflict`, `server`, `other`) with its own `safe` and `next` text and keeps the server text in `what`. `errorState` shows what happened, what is safe and what to do, with Retry. When a page cannot be loaded, both displays now show `errorState` with Retry instead of the bare error text; late errors still do not replace a newer page. No other page is changed.
  - **CSS.** `.skeleton` (no animation under `prefers-reduced-motion`), `.state-error`, `.stale-note`, `.sr-only` in `ui/style.css`.
  - **Code.** `ui/states.js`, `ui/app.js`, `ui/user/app.js`, `ui/style.css`. Tests: new `tests/ui-states.test.ts` and `tests/ui-routers.test.ts`, extended `tests/helpers/fake-dom.ts` and `tests/ui-user.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** New `docs/ui-redesign/states.md` (six patterns and the state matrix), listed in `docs/ui-redesign/README.md`; line numbers in `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` moved by one.
- Refinement 7c-3 — move a criterion between a split draft and its parts, and warnings about the parts (server) (#317).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/criteria/:cid/move` with `{ to }` moves one criterion (the same object) to the end of the target draft and answers 200 with the session. It works part → part, original → part and part → original; this is the one change allowed on a read-only split original. 400 `a criterion can only move between a split draft and its parts` when `to` is missing, is the source, or is not in the same split; 400 `send to: the draft to move the criterion to` for a wrong body; 404 for an unknown draft or criterion; 400 `at most 50 acceptance criteria` for a full target; 409 when the source or the target is already on GitHub. The readiness check of both drafts is cleared and the log gets `criterion-moved` with the criterion text. Only the owner can move (another user gets 404, an admin who is not the owner 403).
  - **Warnings.** The view of a split original has `partWarnings` when there are any: `layer` (a part without a criterion) and `same-code` (two parts whose impact areas overlap while neither depends on the other, directly or through other parts; `areas` is at most 5 overlapping areas of the first part).
  - **Compatibility.** New route and new optional field only; existing sessions, drafts and clients are unchanged.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/draft-parts.ts` (`moveCriterion`, `partWarnings`, `PartWarning`), `src/refinement/store.ts` (`moveCriterionOf`), `src/server/api-refinement.ts` (route, `partWarnings` in `draftView`), `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule). Tests: extended `tests/refinement-split.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-split-api.test.ts` and `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the route table and "Moving a criterion between the parts of a split".
- Refinement 7c-4 — the publish plan leaves out split originals and shows what stays behind (#318).
  - **Behaviour.** `GET /api/refinement/:id/publish` has no item for a draft with `splitInto`. A draft that depended on a split original now depends on each of its parts (in `dependsOn`, in the issue text and in the order), never on itself and none twice. A part blocked by a part that is not ready is named as before. Nothing is stored: the expanded dependencies exist only in the plan and in the publish call.
  - **API.** The answer has `leftBehind: [{ draft, title, criteria }]` for split originals that still hold criteria; the key is absent when there are none.
  - **Code.** New `withoutSplits` and `leftBehind` in `src/refinement/publish.ts`; `planOf` and the publish call use them.
- Refinement 7c-2 — a split original is read-only (server) (#316).
  - **Behaviour.** On a draft with `splitInto`, saving fields, accepting or rejecting a suggestion, move to notes, the review label, the readiness check, accept anyway and removing an "accepted anyway" mark answer 409 `this draft is split; its parts are worked on instead` and change nothing. Asking the architect for a suggestion, review, impact or readiness judgement is 409 as well. `DELETE` of the original still works; after the last part is removed the original can be edited again. A part is not affected.
  - **Paused and running runs.** A paused run whose draft was split is cancelled when asked again, marked failed with `The draft was split while the run was paused`, and the next ask is not blocked. A run that ends after its draft was split stores no result and fails with `The draft was split while the architect was reading`. `ready-check` on a split original also resolves a paused readiness run.
  - **Code.** New `refuseSplit` and `END_SPLIT_PAUSED` in `src/refinement/draft-split.ts`; `src/refinement/store.ts` (eight callbacks, `endArchitectRun`); `askArchitect` in `src/refinement/architect.ts`; the `ready-check` route in `src/server/api-refinement.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "A split original is read-only".
- Skill discovery 3 — interface and database candidates from evidence (#171).
  - **Behaviour.** A new library turns a repository profile into likely skill candidates for five capabilities: `rest-openapi`, `kafka`, `ibm-mq` (interfaces), `cassandra` and `oracle` (databases). Evidence is dependencies, observed imports and schema/configuration files (named, never read), matched by ecosystem (npm, Python, Go, JVM, Rust, Ruby). Each candidate has a `score` (0–100), a `confidence` (`high` from 70, `medium` from 40, otherwise `low`) and up to 10 pieces of evidence with a repository-relative path and the detector; `evidenceMore` counts the rest. Kafka and IBM MQ are separate capabilities. Generic web frameworks count only as weak signals for REST. Generic file types (`sql`, Avro, Protobuf, GraphQL, JSON Schema, Prisma, XSD) give no evidence, so Oracle and Cassandra are never inferred from generic database terms. Evidence under `docs`, `examples`, `samples` or similar folders is marked `documentation`, counts half, and a candidate with only such evidence is capped at 30 (`low`). Optional `affectedModules` limits the evidence to those folders; a dependency counts for a module when its manifest is in the module, or in a parent folder and no nearer manifest of the same build system owns it (Maven and Gradle inherit from parents). Unrelated modules give no candidates. A bad module path throws `invalid module path`. The result is sorted by score, then capability, so it is the same for the same profile.
  - **Safe output.** Candidates use only the finding's kind, name, path and detector, never its value, so no version, endpoint, connection string or credential is copied. Findings with odd names or paths are skipped. The profiler is also stricter: a dependency version is kept only if it looks like a version (or a tag such as `latest`, or a `workspace:`/`catalog:` reference), and a command containing a URL or `@` is kept without its text.
  - **Profile detection.** New schema types by file name: OpenAPI/Swagger (`openapi.yaml`, `swagger.json`, `*-openapi.yml`), `oracle-net` (`tnsnames.ora`, `sqlnet.ora`), `kafka-config` (`kafka*.properties` or `.yaml`), `cql`, `mqsc` and `plsql` (`.pks`, `.pkb`).
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing calls the detector yet: no flow step, route or CLI command uses it, and passing candidates to the planner is left to a later story. Flows, runs, config and watchers are unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/integration-candidates.ts` (`detectIntegrationCandidates`, `IntegrationCandidateSchema`, `CandidateEvidenceSchema`) and `src/skills/integration-rules.ts` (rule tables and scoring); changed `src/skills/repo-profile-rules.ts` (`schemaOf`) and `src/skills/repo-profile-detect.ts` (version and command filters). No new route. Tests: new `tests/integration-candidates.test.ts` (positive, negative, polyglot, documentation-only and unrelated-module cases), extended `tests/repo-profile-detect.test.ts`.
- Skill discovery 2 — language and platform skill candidates from evidence (#170).
  - **Behaviour.** A new library turns a repository profile into scored candidates for `java`, `spring-boot`, `typescript` and `nodejs`. Each candidate has a `score` (1–100), a `confidence` (`high` from 60, `medium` from 30, else `low`), the `modules` it applies to and `evidence` entries (signal, strength, weight, repository-relative path, module, reason). Strong evidence outweighs weak: a build dependency or build file (45–60) counts more than imports (20–35) and file extensions (15). A task keyword adds only 10 and never creates a candidate on its own, so a keyword without repository evidence gives no candidate and cannot reach high confidence. Java does not imply Spring Boot (that needs an `org.springframework.boot` dependency or import), and TypeScript does not imply Node.js (that needs a Node runtime or server package, or Node built-in imports). Scoring is per module (the repository root plus every folder with a `package.json`, `pom.xml` or Gradle build file), and a module needs evidence of its own, so a mono-repository does not select every technology. Optional `affectedPaths` limits the result to the modules the work touches. Task text is matched against fixed patterns only and never appears in the output. Limits: 20 modules and 12 evidence entries per candidate, 20,000 task characters, 200 affected paths.
  - **Profile change.** The repository profile now reports a `node` dependency from `engines.node` in `package.json` and, for JS/TS files, one `node` import root for Node built-ins (`fs`, `node:fs`, …). Commented-out and quoted `require(...)`/`import(...)` calls are no longer counted as imports. Existing `repo-profile.json` files stay valid.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing calls the detector yet: no flow, step or CLI command uses it, and passing candidates to the planner is left to a later story. Flows, runs, config and watchers are unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/candidates.ts` (`detectSkillCandidates`, `moduleRoots`, schemas) and `src/skills/candidate-rules.ts` (signal weights, limits, keyword patterns); `src/skills/repo-profile-detect.ts` (`engines.node`, Node built-in imports, comment handling). No new route. Tests: new `tests/skill-candidates.test.ts` (positive, negative, mixed-language, unrelated-module and keyword-only cases), extended `tests/repo-profile-detect.test.ts`, `tests/repo-profile.test.ts` and `tests/diff.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** No user guide change, as users see no new behaviour yet.
- Refinement 9a — start a session from an existing issue (server) (#331).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement` also accepts `{ repo, issue }`. The open issue is read with the repository's sign-in; nothing changes on GitHub. Title and text become the session title and idea. The session keeps `source` (number, link, title, text, `updated_at`, `buildLabel`), returned by `GET /api/refinement/:id`. A story-format text gives one draft, all fields `from: "typed"` (unknown sections go to Notes with their headings; "Accepted anyway", Foundry markers and the "Refined in…" note are dropped); otherwise idea only. Refused: missing (404), pull request (400), closed (409), not the caller's repository (403), text over the idea limit (400). 409 when a live or queued run, a Foundry status label (watcher names respected) or a run with a pull request exists (a pull request closed and not merged does not block); a failed run or failed label alone is accepted. An issue with an open session of the same account is refused with that session's id. The check runs again under the store lock before saving.
  - **Compatibility.** `source` is optional; old sessions load. Publishing a session that came from an issue returns 409 until write-back exists (#333). Imported session titles may be up to 256 characters; rename still stops at 120.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/issue-import.ts` (`parseStory`), `src/refinement/issue-building.ts` (`buildingReason`), `src/server/api-refinement-import.ts`; changed `store.ts`, `errors.ts`, `src/github.ts` (`RestIssue`), `api-refinement.ts`, `api-refinement-publish.ts`, `permissions.ts` (text of `POST refinement`; no new route). Tests: new `tests/refinement-issue-import.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-import-api.test.ts`; extended fake `gh` and `fake-github.ts`.
- Skills foundation 2 — discover approved skill sources and build the registry (#167).
  - **Behaviour.** New config key `skills` (`builtin` default on, `roots` default empty, `repository` default off; unknown keys are refused). Roots must be absolute and cannot have a `.claude` or `.codex` segment. The registry reads `<data folder>/skills`, then `roots` in order, then the built-in skills, then (only when enabled) `<repo>/.claude-factory/skills`. Only packages that pass the SkillPackage schema are listed, keyed by `id@version`. The same key in a lower source is dropped as a `duplicate`. One version per id is active (from the highest source); other versions stay listed with `shadowedBy`. Missing, unreadable, not-a-folder and refused roots, invalid packages and symlinked packages become problems; the scan does not throw. Personal folders (`~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `.claude`/`.codex` segments, and symlinks into them) are never scanned. Limits: 500 folders per root; 1000 packages or 64 MiB of files in all.
  - **Health.** `GET /api/health` has optional `skillProblems` (`source`, `root` label, `package`, `reason`; no paths; at most 20) and `skillProblemsMore`. Skill problems count in `ok` and `summary`, and the health line shows them.
  - **CLI.** `scf skills [--repo <dir>]` lists skills, notes and problems with full paths and exits 1 when there are problems.
  - **Packaging.** `package.json` `files` now includes `skills`. New built-in skill `skills/small-changes/`.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing uses the registry yet. Flows, runs and watchers are unchanged; existing `config.yaml` files work without the new key.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/registry.ts` (`discoverSkills`, `cachedSkillRegistry`, `findSkill`); `src/config.ts` (`skills`, `SkillsConfig`), `src/server/health.ts`, `ui/health.js`, `src/cli.ts`. No new route. Tests: new `tests/skill-registry.test.ts`, extended `tests/health.test.ts` and `tests/ui-health.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Skill sources", the health line and the command table.
- Skill discovery 1 — a bounded repository technology profile (#169).
  - **Behaviour.** A new library builds factual technology evidence from a checked-out repository: file types (recognized extensions only), build manifests, dependency declarations, observed import roots (with a small standard-library exclusion list, not a true "external only" check), schemas, deployment files and the test commands set in `.claude-factory/config.yaml`. Every finding has a repository-relative source path and a detector reason, and goes into `repo-profile.json`. It runs locally, without network access, and never changes the repository. The same commit gives the same profile. Language, schema, Terraform and import findings are grouped per folder, with a source path in that folder and a count. Known build, schema and deployment files are listed first in each folder, so manifests survive the file limit.
  - **Limits.** 20,000 files, 5,000 folders, 12 levels deep, 5,000 entries per folder, 256 KiB per file read, 8 MiB read in total, 500 findings (per kind: language 50, manifest 100, dependency 150, import 100, schema 20, deployment 60, command 20) and a 512 KiB artifact. A caller can only lower a limit; a bad value falls back to the default.
  - **Skipped.** Version-control, dependency, vendor, generated, secret-bearing, binary, oversized and symlinked paths, and files that are not valid UTF-8. Only identifiers that pass a token filter reach a finding; the schemas reject traversal paths and free text, so no host path appears.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing calls the profiler yet: no flow step or CLI command writes `repo-profile.json` during a run, and passing it to the planner is left to a later story. Flows, runs, config and watchers are unchanged.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/repo-profile-rules.ts` (limits and name tables), `src/skills/repo-profile-detect.ts` (detectors) and `src/skills/repo-profile.ts` (`buildRepoProfile`, `serializeRepoProfile`, `writeRepoProfile`, schemas); `readRegular` in `src/skills/package.ts` is now exported. No new route. Tests: new `tests/repo-profile-detect.test.ts` and `tests/repo-profile.test.ts` (mono-repositories, empty repositories, several build systems, determinism, skipped paths, limits).
  - **Docs.** No user guide change, as users see no new behaviour yet.
- UI redesign foundation 1 — the target information architecture (#249). Pages are grouped into places: Home, Board, Refinement, Runs and Repositories for daily work, then Flows and Administration drawn smaller and apart. No role sees more than seven (admin has seven; a user has four plus the Start work button). A second row groups the pages of a place, detail pages get breadcrumbs and a back link, and the tab title names the page. **Your turn** is now **Home** at `#/home`; the old `#/your-turn` is redirected there in the browser. All other addresses are unchanged, so links from GitHub, notifications and run records still work. An empty or unknown address opens Home (admin) or My runs (user). Both displays stay separate and hash-based. No server change, no new route. Ask SCF is not built and no place is reserved for it. Users still have no Home or Board.
  - **Code.** New `ui/ia.js` (pages, destinations, aliases, `resolve`, `primaryFor`, `subnavFor`; no imports) and `ui/shell.js` (`showPage`, tab title and count); `ui/app.js`, `ui/user/app.js`, `ui/index.html`, `ui/user/index.html`, `ui/style.css` and `ui/turn.js` use them. Tests: new `tests/ui-ia.test.ts` and `tests/ui-shell.test.ts`; route tests and `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` updated. `docs/ui-redesign/` tables use the new addresses. The full suite was not seen finishing in the implementing session; the flow's own build and test step gives the result.
  - **Docs.** User guide: page table and a "Navigation (admin display)" paragraph.
- UI redesign foundation 2, part 1 — one visual system: semantic tokens, type scale, light/dark and density (#322).
  - **Behaviour.** New `ui/tokens.css`, loaded before `/style.css` by `ui/index.html` and `ui/user/index.html`, defines every token: colour, typography, spacing, sizing, borders, radii, elevation, motion, density and layering. Each colour is one line, `light-dark(<light>, <dark>)`. Nine colour meanings (neutral, accent, success, warning, danger, information, running, waiting, disabled) each have a text colour and a `-soft` background. The type scale has page, section, body, metadata, code, table and table-head levels. `html[data-theme="light"|"dark"]` forces a theme; without it the operating system decides, as before. `html[data-density="compact"]` changes only tokens (text, padding, gaps, bar height); no selector in `ui/style.css` mentions it. Motion tokens are `0s` under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`. The 20 old names (`--bg`, `--panel`, `--accent`, `--ok`, `--run`, `--radius`, …) stay as aliases, so existing rules and inline `var(--…)` keep working.
  - **Stylesheet.** `ui/style.css` lost both `:root` blocks and the dark-only rules; it has no hex colour, raw `z-index`, `font-size` or `border-radius` length left. The shared rules (`body`, buttons, inputs, `.table` cells, `.card`, `.tile`, `main`, `.top`) use the density tokens. About 12 font sizes were rounded to 7, so small visual shifts are possible.
  - **Browsers.** `light-dark()` needs Safari 17.5, Chrome 123 or Firefox 120. Older browsers lose colours; there is no fallback (the `/defaults` answer to the question on the issue).
  - **Demo.** New `docs/ui-redesign/visual-system-demo.html` shows a runs table and a refinement talk with the real `ui/tokens.css` and `ui/style.css`, with switches for theme and density. Static, opened from disk, fake data, not served.
  - **Tests.** New `tests/ui-visual-system.test.ts` recomputes the WCAG contrast ratios from `ui/tokens.css` and checks the token rules, the stylesheet rules, the `var(--…)` names, the page links and the demo. In `tests/ui-redesign-prototype.test.ts` the "tokens" check now requires that every legacy name `proto.css` defines is still defined in `ui/tokens.css`.
  - **Not done / not checked.** Nothing was checked in a browser. `docs/ui-redesign/visual-system.md` (the token list and contrast table) is not in this change and is still open. No server change, no new route, no new dependency.
- Gitflow: the push to `develop` is tried again (twice, 30 seconds apart) when GitHub answers with a server error or the connection drops, instead of failing a finished story.
- UI redesign research 2 — prototypes (#248). New static pages in `docs/ui-redesign/prototype/` (shell, home, board, runs, run, repositories, administration) with switches for role, theme, width, state and alternative; walkthroughs of the five tasks for both roles and every alternative; accepted and rejected decisions and the proposed direction. Owner approval is still open (status `proposed` in `decisions.md`). Not checked in a browser. New `tests/ui-redesign-prototype.test.ts`. Not served by the server. No behaviour change.
- Refinement 8c — publish on the session page: plan, labels, "Start building this story", links (#309). **Publish** shows only to the owner, while the repository is available, when a draft is ready and not on GitHub. It saves typed text first, then shows the plan (issues in order with title, text, dependencies, labels; drafts that are not ready are listed with the reason). Labels come from the repository, none ticked except a requested review label; the build tick box is off by default and hidden without an enabled issues watcher. Nothing is sent until you confirm. Published drafts show the link and are read only; after a failure the server's message and the drafts already on GitHub are shown. New `ui/refinement-publish.js`, `publishPlan` and `publish` in `ui/api.js`, changes in `ui/refinement.js`, `ui/refinement-draft.js` (`saveAll`, link, read-only state) and `ui/style.css`; new `tests/ui-refinement-publish.test.ts`. No server change. User guide: "Publishing on the session page"; `docs/ui-redesign/dialogs.md` lists the new dialogs.
- Fix: stories spent more than half their time waiting for tests. (1) At most `test_slots` test commands (default 2) run on the machine at once (`tools/test-slot`), and one run of this repository's suite uses half the cores. (2) `quick_test_cmd`: inside a story a quicker command may run (for example `npx vitest run --changed`); the full tests still run before the change and on the merge result. (3) New steps `pretest_merge` and `pretest_tests`: the merge with `develop` is tested before the run takes its turn at `develop`, so an unchanged `develop` is pushed without a second test run.
- UI redesign research 1 — audit of every screen and the main journeys (#247). New `docs/ui-redesign/`: inventory of all routes, modals and states, a list of dialog variants, nine journeys with step counts, findings split into structural and visual, and a measurement plan with modelled times for five tasks. Counts come from reading the code, not from a browser. Measured time, errors, confidence and new screenshots at four widths are still open (follow-ups for the owner). New `tests/ui-redesign-audit.test.ts` keeps the inventory in step with the routes, nav links and dialogs. No behaviour change.
- Refinement 8b — publishing: ready drafts become GitHub issues, once (#307).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/publish` creates an issue for each ready draft, dependencies first, with the repository's sign-in. Optional body `{ drafts: [{ id, labels, startBuilding }] }`; a missing `drafts` publishes every ready draft with no labels. Labels must exist in the repository and are sent in its spelling; the build label (needs a watcher) and the review label are set by the rules, never chosen by hand. A draft without a title refuses the call (409); a bad body or label gives 400; both before any create. Each body ends with a hidden marker, so "publish again" after a failure skips published drafts and takes over an issue made before the answer was lost (newest 100 issues searched). The draft gets `published: { issue, url, at }` (replaces the number form of #306), the session becomes *published* when all drafts have an issue, and the log has `draft-published`. While a publish runs the session cannot be changed (409); publish waits for a queued or running architect run. A published draft, the Epic, and map entries or drafts it refers to cannot be changed or removed (409). Audit action `refinement-publish`.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/publish.ts` (`refinedMarker`, `refinedHashIn`, `parsePublishInput`, `chosenLabels`, `labelsFor`), `store.ts` (`beginPublishing`, `endPublishing`, `isPublishing`, `recordPublished`, `logRoom`, `END_ON_GITHUB`, guards), `architect.ts` (`architectWorking`, guards), `draft.ts`, `draft-ready.ts`, `github.ts` (`listNewestIssues`), `src/server/api-refinement-publish.ts` (POST), `api-refinement.ts` (export `architectDeps`), `permissions.ts` (rule), `src/auth/audit.ts` and `ui/audit.js` (action), `ui/refinement.js` (`logText`). Tests: new `tests/refinement-publish-post-api.test.ts` and `tests/refinement-publish-lock-api.test.ts`, plus extended publish, draft, architect, permissions and UI tests; the fake `gh` has `FAKE_GH_FAIL_CREATE_AT`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Publishing", the route and audit tables, and the States paragraph.
- Skills foundation 1 — the portable skill package format: schema and loader (#166).
  - **Behaviour.** A skill package is a folder with a required `SKILL.md` (frontmatter `name` and `description`, plus the standard optional `license`, `compatibility`, `metadata`, `allowed-tools`), a `skill.yaml` with the Foundry's own fields, and optional `references/`, `scripts/`, `assets/` and `evals/` folders. It has no provider-specific fields, so Claude, Codex and local targets can all use it. `skill.yaml` holds `id`, `version`, `category` (default `general`), `capabilities`, `detectors` (`file` or `content` globs), `roles` (`planner`, `coder`, `reviewer`, `tester`), `dependencies` (`id`, optional `min_version`), `conflicts`, `tool_profile` (`shell`, `network`, `filesystem`: `none`, `read` or `write`) and `risk` (`low`, `medium`, `high`; default `low`). A valid package parses to one normalized `SkillPackage` that also holds every file's bytes.
  - **Formats.** A skill id is a lower-case slug of 1–64 characters (letters, digits, single hyphens). A version is SemVer `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH` with an optional `-prerelease` and no build metadata, at most 64 characters; `1.0.0-01` is rejected. The `name` in `SKILL.md` and the folder name must match the skill id when loaded from disk.
  - **Errors.** All problems are collected into one `SkillPackageError`, each with a precise path and reason: missing or malformed files, duplicate entries and duplicate list items, paths that leave the package (`..`, `./`, `a//b`, `a\b`, `C:/`, absolute), unsupported entries (unknown folders, empty or hidden folders), and oversized files. Limits: `SKILL.md` 64 KiB, `skill.yaml` 16 KiB, 256 KiB per file, 2 MiB in total, 200 files, paths up to 200 characters and 6 segments. Sizes come from the real byte length. `.DS_Store` is ignored. The loader does not follow symlinks and checks the open file before reading.
  - **Compatibility.** Nothing loads these packages yet. Flows, runs, config and watchers are unchanged, and an older `run.json` without a skill field still loads.
  - **Code.** New `src/skills/schema.ts` (Zod schemas, types, `SKILL_LIMITS`) and `src/skills/package.ts` (`parseSkillPackage`, `loadSkillPackage`, `SkillPackageError`, `splitFrontmatter`). No new route. Tests: new `tests/skill-schema.test.ts` and `tests/skill-package.test.ts` with the `minimal` and `full` fixtures in `tests/fixtures/skills/`.
  - **Docs.** A separate guide for skill authors is not written yet.
- Refinement 7c-1 — confirm a split: the parts become drafts, the original is kept (server) (#315).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/split/confirm` takes `{ way?, parts, unplaced }` and answers 201 with the session; no architect run starts. `parts` has 2 to 6 entries `{ title, sentence?, criteria, dependsOn }` (400 `a split has 2 to 6 parts`, `each part needs a title`); `unplaced` must be a list of criterion ids; `way` is absent or 0–2 and names a stored way. `dependsOn` holds 1-based part numbers, none twice, none unknown, none its own, none of a later part. Every criterion of the draft is in exactly one part or in `unplaced` (400 for an unknown, doubled or missing one; the message shows the stored text on one line, cut to 60 characters, never an id from the body). A draft with zero or one criterion can be split by an own plan. More than 20 drafts after the split gives 400 `at most 20 story drafts`. Each part is a new draft at the end, in plan order: title, its criteria (same objects), copies of the original's out of scope and notes, and `part: { of, hint? }`. The first part also gets copies of the original's dependencies; links between parts are `{ draft }` items. Sources are `accepted` / `accepted-edited` when the title (or link) matches the way at that position, otherwise `typed`. The original keeps the unplaced criteria, loses `split`, gets `splitInto`, and the log gets `draft-split`.
  - **Rules.** Asking for ways or confirming on a draft that is already split, is a part, or is published gives 409. A split run that ends after a confirm or a publish fails with `END_NO_SPLIT_DRAFT` and the file stays valid. A draft with `splitInto` is never ready (`state: "split"` in its view) and the session state is worked out over the other drafts. Removing a part shrinks `splitInto` (the last one removes the key); removing the original strips `part` from its parts. A part cannot depend on a later part or on its original (saving or accepting such a suggestion gives 400; a stored file with one does not load). A stored `splitInto` with an unknown id, or a `part.of` without the matching `splitInto`, does not load; files without the new fields load unchanged. Only the owner can confirm (404 for another user, 403 for an admin).
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/draft-parts.ts` (`confirmSplit`); `src/refinement/draft-split.ts` (`splitRefusal` gains three state reasons, `confirmRefusal`, `partOrderProblem`); `src/refinement/draft.ts` (`splitInto`, `part`, cross-draft rules, log kinds `draft-split` and `criterion-moved`, `dropDraft`); `src/refinement/draft-ready.ts`; `confirmSplitOf` in `src/refinement/store.ts`; the route, `state` in the draft view, and a rule in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Confirming a split" and the route table; `docs/DESIGN.md` §18.
- Gitflow: the tests before a change and after a merge are not run again when exactly that code (a clean tree) already passed them in another run; `reuse_test_results: no` switches it off. Saves one full test run for most stories.
- Watchers: `auto_defaults: true` lets a watcher answer the Foundry's questions with its own recommendations (a `/defaults` comment on the issue) instead of waiting for a person; at most twice per issue.
- Platform 3 — Documentation: first part of the admin and user guides work (#22). Added the start of the screenshot tooling: a typed shot list in `scripts/screenshots/shots.ts` with `validateManifest()` (reports duplicate shot names, prepare steps for unknown shots, and shots without an expected locator), tests in `tests/screenshot-manifest.test.ts`, and `tsconfig.json` now also checks `scripts/screenshots`. The shot list is still empty. The guides, the screenshots and the README links are not written yet.
- Platform 2d-3 — agent steps of a user's run work and sign in inside the sandbox; SR-O1 closed in the docs (#305).
  - **Behaviour.** Claude and Codex steps of a user's run start inside the same `sandbox-exec` profile as shell steps, with the same refusal and the same setting; whatever the agent starts is held too. The agent's own sandbox is off for these steps (profiles do not nest): Claude gets no `--settings` sandbox entry (one log line when the flow asked for it), Codex gets `sandbox_mode="danger-full-access"`. A Codex step that would have been `read-only` gets a profile that writes only in `<runDir>/home` and `<runDir>/tmp`. Each run has private, empty agent folders, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<runDir>/home/.claude` and `CODEX_HOME=<runDir>/home/.codex`, set by the server; a later step of the run can still resume. The agent program and its install folder are readable inside the profile. Agent steps of runs that keep the machine's login, and the server's own agent calls, are unchanged.
  - **Sign-in.** Token variable only. Claude on `anthropic` needs `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` or `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`; Codex on `openai` needs `OPENAI_API_KEY` or `CODEX_API_KEY` (a lone `OPENAI_API_KEY` is also passed as `CODEX_API_KEY`); an `anthropic-compatible` provider needs its `api_key_env`; local providers need none. Without one the step is refused at once, with no fallback try: "This agent has no key on this server. An admin must set <NAME> for the server; a user's run cannot use the Mac's own agent login." A signed-out or 401 answer gives the same sentence. A login variable in a flow's `agent_env` is ignored. When the budget is reached, only a local model counts as free in such a run.
  - **Upgrade.** Users' agent steps stop until a token variable is set for the server. The admin's `~/.claude` and `~/.codex` (MCP servers, skills, plugins, global `CLAUDE.md`, `config.toml`) no longer reach a user's run; flows must carry what they need. Check by hand on a Mac that the real `claude` and `codex` finish a step inside the profile.
  - **Code.** New `src/agents/boxed.ts`; `src/agents/run.ts` (`runOn`, `runWith`, `runAgentStep`), `src/engine/os-sandbox.ts` (`agentProgram`, `workspaceReadOnly`, `installDir`), `src/engine/runner.ts`, `src/steps/claude.ts` and `src/steps/codex.ts` (`sandboxProfile` option). No new route. Tests: new `tests/agent-sandbox.test.ts`, new `tests/os-sandbox-agent-run.test.ts` (macOS only), `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts`, extended fake `claude` and `codex`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` shows SR-O1 as fixed in #302 with the limits that remain; "Trust" in the user guide and the "Runs that never use the machine's login" entry in `docs/DESIGN.md` are rewritten.

- Refinement 7b — ask for ways to split a draft and keep them with the draft (server) (#310).
  - **Behaviour.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/split` starts (or resumes) an architect run of kind `split` and answers 202 with the session. The body may carry `{ "own": "…" }`, at most 500 characters; no body is fine, a wrong media type gives 415, a too large body 413, invalid JSON or an `own` that is not text 400. The rules of a review apply: owner only (admin 403, another account 404), a brief must exist (409), one architect run per session and per account, a paused run is resumed by the same call. 409 when the draft has fewer than 2 acceptance criteria. When the run ends, the checked ways are stored with the draft as `split: { at, mark, ways }` and replace older ones; C numbers are stored as criterion ids. No field of the draft and no session state changes. The session answer shows `split` without the mark, with `outOfDate: true` when the draft changed since it was asked, and hides it while the repository is not in My repositories. A wrong form fails the run with "The architect's answer did not have the agreed form" and the old ways stay; a draft that is gone fails with a fixed sentence. The log has `split-asked` and `architect-split`; an orphan `split` run is adopted like a review. The run uses the $1 limit (`SUGGEST_MAX_COST_USD`). An old `refinements.json` loads unchanged (`split` is optional). Nothing is split by this.
  - **Not yet.** The 409 for a draft that is "already split" or published cannot exist yet: the draft has no marker for either state. `splitRefusal` is where it goes. The server rejects more than 30 warnings in one way; `tools/refine-round-check` has no such limit.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/draft-split.ts` (`SplitSchema`, `setSplit`, `splitView`, `SplitRefs`, limits; checks the answer again by the checker's rules; imports only types from `draft.ts`) and `src/refinement/split-text.ts` (`splitText`, `splitOf`; `split` added to `TALK_FIRST_LINE`). `draft-impact.ts` exports `TIME` and `sentences`; `draftMark` is reused. Extended `store.ts`, `architect.ts`, `draft.ts` (log kinds, `split` field), `src/server/api-refinement.ts` (route), `src/server/http.ts` (new `readOptionalJson`) and `src/server/permissions.ts` (rule). Tests: new `tests/refinement-split.test.ts` and `tests/refinement-split-api.test.ts`, plus `tests/permissions.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Ways to split a draft" and the route table.
- Fair use 4 — Dashboard: usage and cost per user and per repository against the limits (#298).
  - **Behaviour.** The admin **By user** card is full width and shows, per account, runs and cost over 30 days plus today's runs, runs active now and today's cost, with the effective limits next to them (`2 / 5`). An account at a limit (runs at the same time, runs per day or cost per day) gets an "at limit" pill. Accounts with limits or an active run but no runs are listed too; a deleted account keeps its line as "deleted user" with no limits and no mark. The **By repository** card adds today's runs and cost. Today uses the same local-day rule as `spentToday`. The at-limit check uses the exact cost, not the rounded value. Only admins get these numbers; `GET /api/stats` stays `user: "no"`.
  - **Code.** `src/stats.ts` (`today: { runs, costUsd }` on `byUser` and `byRepo`; costs still add up to the totals; user today-runs leave out architect runs), `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`startedTodayByOwner()`, counted like the per-day limit), new `src/server/usage-view.ts` (`userUsage()` merges names, `active`, `limits`, `atLimit`), `src/server/api-admin.ts` (`stats` route; a broken `limits.json` is logged and gives empty limits), `ui/dashboard.js` (`byUserCard()`, new `byRepoCard()`; missing fields from an old answer show as zeros). No new route; `permissions.ts` did not change. Tests: `tests/stats.test.ts`, new `tests/usage-view.test.ts`, `tests/ui-dashboard.test.ts`, `tests/run-ownership.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: the Dashboard paragraph and "By user and limits".
- Platform 2d-2 — shell steps of a user's run work inside a macOS sandbox profile (#304).
  - **Behaviour.** Every shell step of a run owned by a user starts under `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec` with a profile made for that step. Reads are denied in the Mac account's home, the whole data folder and the runs folder, and allowed again for the run's own folder, `TOOLS_DIR`, the hooks folder, the run's learnings file, the lock folder, the step's `gh` folder, the folders of `node`, `git`, `claude` and `codex` under the home, `known_hosts` in the data folder (deploy keys) and the paths in `sandbox.user_read`. Writes are denied everywhere and allowed in the run's folder, `<runDir>/tmp`, the learnings file (and its folder), the lock folder, the step's `gh` folder and `/dev/null`, `/dev/tty`, `/dev/dtracehelper`; `run.json`, `live.log` and `logs/` stay read-only. The server's temp folder is denied for reads except the step's own `gh` folder. The Keychain lookups of `com.apple.SecurityServer` and `com.apple.securityd` are denied, and so are hard links, clones, unix sockets, opening apps (`lsopen`), Apple events and launchd job creation. The network stays open. The step gets `HOME=<runDir>/home` and `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP` = `<runDir>/tmp` (mode 0700). A shell step with `sandbox: true` and a Docker image is not wrapped; the container holds it. Admin runs and runs that keep the machine's login are unchanged. Agent steps are not held yet.
  - **Refusal.** With no usable sandbox (not macOS, no `/usr/bin/sandbox-exec`, or the trial run fails) and `sandbox.user_runs: required`, a user's run fails in `drive()` before any step, also on resume, with "This computer cannot hold a user's run in a sandbox, so the run was not started. An admin can allow user runs without it in Settings."
  - **Setting.** New `sandbox.user_runs` in `config.yaml`: `required` (default, also for existing installs) or `off`; new `sandbox.user_read`: up to 50 extra paths a step may read. The server variable `SCF_USER_SANDBOX=off` (or `FACTORY_USER_SANDBOX=off`) means the same as `off`. Settings has the checkbox **Allow user runs without the OS sandbox**; saving keeps both values. `tests/setup.ts` sets `SCF_USER_SANDBOX=off`.
  - **Area locks.** A sandboxed step cannot read other runs' `run.json`, so each running run has a small marker file in the lock folder (run id only; new `src/engine/running.ts`). `tools/area-lock` follows the marker for a lock made while it exists, falls back to `run.json` for other locks, and counts a `run.json` it may not read as running.
  - **Upgrade.** The sandbox is on for every user's run after the upgrade. To switch it off, tick **Allow user runs without the OS sandbox** in Settings, set `sandbox.user_runs: off`, or set `SCF_USER_SANDBOX=off` for the server. A server that is not macOS (or has no working `sandbox-exec`) must do this, or user runs are refused. A flow that reads files outside the run folder (a tool in the home, a config file) needs the path in `sandbox.user_read`. A step's `HOME` and temp folder are now inside the run folder, so caches in `~` start empty.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/os-sandbox.ts` (`sandboxProfile`, `sandboxAvailable`, `sandboxedRun`, `wrapsStep`); `src/steps/process.ts` and `src/steps/shell.ts` (`sandboxProfile` option), `src/engine/execute.ts`, `src/engine/runner.ts`, `src/config.ts`, `ui/admin.js`, `tools/area-lock`. No new route. Tests: `tests/os-sandbox.test.ts`, `tests/running.test.ts`, `tests/os-sandbox-run.test.ts` (macOS only; it also shows that a profile started inside a sandboxed step fails, so profiles do not nest), `tests/ui-settings.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`.
  - **Still open.** Tests for clone and push with a token or deploy key inside the sandbox, and for the pre-push hook there, are not written yet. The marker sweep runs only when a run starts.
- Refinement 7a — the architect proposes ways to split a draft: `ask=split` in the round flow (read-only) (#308).
  - **Behaviour.** `refine-round` accepts `--var ask=split`. The task is the talk with the draft; its criteria are C1, C2, …, counted from the lines `- C<n>: ` under `### Acceptance criteria` in the part `## The draft to split`. The answer is `{ "ways": [ … ] }` with 1 to 3 ways, each with a `cut` (`step`, `interface`, `data`, `rule` or `spike`; no two ways the same), 2 to 6 `stories` (`title` at most 120 characters, `sentence` at most 300, `criteria`, `dependsOn`), `first`, `unplaced` and `warnings` (`layer`, `same-code`). When the task has "The person's own way", the first way works it out. The architect writes no plan, asks no questions and proposes no map entries. This is the flow only: no server code and no page yet.
  - **Check.** `tools/refine-round-check` fails the run with one plain sentence when a C number is in two stories, in a story and in `unplaced`, missing or not in the task; a story depends on itself, a later or an unknown story; a cut or warning kind is unknown, a cut is used twice or a warning names an unknown story; a text is too long, has a line break or names hours, days or weeks; or there are more than 3 ways or an unknown field. All warnings are checked and kept.
  - **Files.** `flows/refine-round.yaml` is generated from `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (new `split` section). `tests/fixtures/fake-claude.mjs` answers `split` with two ways (`step` and `rule`); `FAKE_ROUND` still replaces the answer. Tests: `tests/refine-round.test.ts`. No new route; the flow format does not change.
  - **Docs.** User guide: `ask=split` in the built-in flow `refine-round`.
- Fair use 3 — daily budget per user (#297).
  - **Behaviour.** When the cost of a user's runs that started today (local day) reaches their effective `dailyBudgetUsd`, no new job of that user starts; it waits in the queue until the next day. A working run of that user stops before its next agent step with "your daily limit is reached — resume tomorrow", like the global budget: shell and approval steps still run, a free fallback model is used when the router allows it, and the run can be resumed the next day. A watcher starts no new work (issues, CI, schedules) for an owner whose budget is used up; the issue is held. `cost_limits: false` turns the per-user budget off. The global `daily_budget_usd` still applies on top. Runs without an owner (also a run that falls to the first admin inside `runFlow`) are only under the global budget.
  - **Display.** The user sees "waiting — your limit for today is reached", with no amount and no word about money, on run pages, the queue, the log and the GitHub status comment. The admin sees "waiting — the owner's daily budget" with the amount on the queue and run pages only; `/api/next`, the board, "Your turn", watcher status and notifications do not show the amount yet. The stop reason maps to the kind `user_limit`.
  - **Code.** `spentTodayBy`, `USER_BUDGET_REASON` and `isUserBudgetStop` in `src/engine/state.ts` (same day rule, `slice(0, 500)` window and early break as `spentToday`); optional `userDailyBudget` run option in `src/engine/runner.ts` (the engine does not import `src/auth/limits.ts`); `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`limit: "budget"`, `spentTodayBy` option, `userDailyBudget()`); `src/queue/watcher.ts`; `src/failure-explain.ts` (the owner's remaining amount limits the small model call); `src/next-step.ts`, `src/words.ts`, `src/issue-record.ts`, `src/server/user-view.ts`, `src/server/next.ts`, `src/queue/status-comment.ts`, `src/refinement/architect.ts`, `src/server/api-users.ts`, `ui/users.js`. No new route; the description of `GET /api/users/limits` changed. Tests: extended usage, engine, agents, failure-explain, scheduler-fair-use, watch, next-step, words, status-comment and user-view tests.
  - **Docs.** User guide (status table, Users page, Budget & capacity, route table).
- Refinement 8a — the publish plan (#306).
  - **Behaviour.** `GET /api/refinement/:id/publish` answers with the plan of a session: one item per draft in the order the issues would be created (a draft comes after the drafts it depends on, otherwise the session order stays), each with its title, full issue text, state (`ready`, `not-ready` with a reason, or `on-github`), what it depends on, and its labels. Readiness is worked out now with the repository's current Definition of Ready; the stored state is not used. A ready draft that depends on a draft that is not ready is not offered. The text is the story format plus a note "Refined in Spaghetti Code Foundry by <display name> on <date>." and holds only what a person typed or accepted. The answer also has the repository's labels (read from GitHub with its own sign-in), the build label (the label of the first enabled `issues` watcher, or `noBuildLabel`) and the review label. It only reads: nothing is created and no session changes. Only the owner gets it (another user 404, an admin 403). A repository that is not in My repositories, a dropped session, a repository that uses the server's own login, and a GitHub failure (502) are refused with a plain sentence.
  - **Code.** New `src/refinement/publish.ts` (`publishOrder`, `issueText`, `planOf`), `src/server/api-refinement-publish.ts`, `src/server/repo-sign-in.ts` (`asOwnedRepo`, for part 8b too). `preview()` has an optional fourth parameter to print a dependency on a draft; `notReadyReason` in `draft-ready.ts`; `buildLabelOf` in `build-limits.ts`; `repoLabels` in `github.ts`; `guardedAsync` and `limitsOf` exported from `api-refinement.ts`; server option `ghTimeoutMs`. A draft has an optional `published` (issue number) that nothing writes yet; the plan shows such a draft as `on-github`. Tests: new `tests/refinement-publish.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-publish-api.test.ts`; `tests/refinement-draft.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-ready.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-build-limits.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`; the fake `gh` answers the labels call.

- Refinement 5d — readiness on the draft page: check, results, accept anyway (#293).
  - **Behaviour.** An open draft has a part **Definition of Ready**: the items of the repository's list in order, each with Met, Not met, Unsure or Not checked yet, its reason, and whether code or the architect judged it. **Check readiness** saves unsaved text first, then asks for the check; the busy, paused (**Ask again**) and failed (**Try again**) lines work like Review draft. Items that are not met or unsure have **Accept anyway** (a dialog that needs a reason); an accepted item shows its reason and **Remove reason** (after a confirmation). The item with the rule `no-plan` has no such button and says a plan belongs in the build step. The draft, the list of drafts and the session show Ready or Drafting; after an edit the results are gone, the draft shows Drafting and the accepted reasons stay. The preview shows the "Accepted anyway" section. The part shows only where the draft fields show; elsewhere the results show as text. The log tells a check asked for, the architect's judgement, an item accepted anyway and a reason removed. The Context brief part does not show a readiness run's line. Every text is set as text.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-ready.js`; `ui/api.js` (`checkReady`, `acceptAnyway`, `removeAccepted`), `ui/refinement-talk.js` (kind `ready`), `ui/refinement.js` (words and log sentences), `ui/refinement-draft.js`, `ui/style.css`. The session answer of `src/server/api-refinement.ts` now has `readyList`, the repository's list, while the repository is available. No new route; `permissions.ts` did not change. Tests: new `tests/ui-refinement-ready.test.ts`; `tests/ui-refinement-draft.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-ready-api.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-api.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "Readiness on the page" and the **States** paragraph.
- Platform 2d-1 — users cannot run `worktree` flows, and the server's git calls for a workspace run clean (#303).
  - **Behaviour.** A run of a `workspace: worktree` flow owned by a user never runs a step and never calls `git worktree add`; it fails with "This flow works in a branch of the server's folder, so only an admin can run it." The refusal comes before `prepareWorkspace`, so no worktree and no branch is made, and it holds on start, resume, approve, reject, answer and the queue, like `inplace`. `POST /api/runs` answers 403 for a user (or an admin with `likeUser: true`) who starts a published `worktree` flow, and `GET /api/flows` does not list such a flow for users. Admin runs and runs without an owner are unchanged. The default of `workspace` is still `worktree`.
  - **Clean git.** The git calls in `prepareWorkspace` and `repoRoot` use the clean environment of the diff (`PATH`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`) and `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false`. A `post-checkout` hook or `fsmonitor` setting in the repository does not run when a worktree is made.
  - **Upgrade.** Users who start flows with `workspace: worktree` (or without `workspace`) now get a refusal; change those flows to `workspace: empty` and clone in a `repo_access` step. For admin `worktree` runs the global and system git settings no longer apply when the worktree is made: a Git LFS repository is checked out with pointer files instead of the real files, and `core.autocrlf` and `safe.directory` from `~/.gitconfig` are not used. Later steps of the run still use them.
  - **Code.** `WORKTREE_REFUSED` and `workspaceRefused` in `src/engine/isolation.ts`; `src/engine/runner.ts`, `src/engine/diff.ts` (`cleanEnv` exported), `src/engine/workspace.ts`, `src/server/api-runs.ts`, `src/server/api-flows.ts`. No new route; the flow format does not change. Tests: `tests/security-review.test.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/diff.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`, `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` and `docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md` say `worktree` is refused for users.
  - **Still open.** A filter in the server folder's own `.git/config` is still read (SR-O12).
- Fair use 2 — the scheduler shares slots fairly and enforces runs at the same time and runs per day (#296).
  - **Behaviour.** A user never has more runs active than their effective `maxConcurrent`; further jobs wait and start by themselves. Once a user started `maxRunsPerDay` new runs today (local day), further new runs wait until the next day. Resuming, answering or approving a run is not a new run. Limits apply to admins and to watcher runs (they count for the watcher's owner). Architect runs count for "at the same time" but not "per day". Jobs without an owner are not limited. A run that falls to the first admin counts for that admin. Lowering a limit does not stop working runs.
  - **Order.** Free slots go round-robin: priority block first, then the owner with the fewest active runs, then the owner whose last start is oldest, then queue order. Locks, the global `concurrency`, the hold for blocked accounts and bug-story priority still apply; a priority job is still held by its owner's limits. With no limits and one user queued, the order is as before.
  - **Display.** New status kind `user_limit` ("waiting — your limit for today is reached" and matching texts for the at-the-same-time limit and for the admin view) on run pages, the runs page, the board, the queue and the GitHub status comment. No numbers or money. `Scheduler.queue()` adds `limit` (`concurrent` or `per_day`) to pending entries; the user's queue answer does not carry it. The monitor's `queue-stalled` ignores jobs that wait for a user limit.
  - **Code.** New `src/queue/usage.ts` (`runsStartedToday`); `src/queue/scheduler.ts` (`userLimits` and `startedToday` options, `recheck()`), `src/server/server.ts`, `src/server/api-users.ts` (a changed limit pumps the queue), `src/next-step.ts`, `src/words.ts`, `src/board.ts`, `src/issue-record.ts`, `src/server/next.ts`, `src/queue/status-comment.ts`, `src/monitor/work-detectors.ts`. The CLI and evals pass no options, so they stay unlimited. Tests: new `tests/scheduler-fair-use.test.ts` and `tests/usage.test.ts`; extended words, next-step, board, status-comment, monitor and user-view tests.
  - **Docs.** User Guide (status table, Users page, route table) and Design (queue).

- Refinement 5c — the architect judges the readiness items that code cannot (server) (#292).
  - **Check readiness.** `POST /api/refinement/:id/drafts/:did/ready-check` runs the code checks first and stores them. When no item is left `unsure` it answers 200 and makes no AI call (it also cancels a paused ready run for that draft). Otherwise it starts an architect run (`refine-round`, `ask=ready`, at most $1) and answers 202 with the session. The rules of the other architect runs apply: owner only, a brief is needed, one run per session and per account, a paused run is resumed by the same call, and a failed or cancelled run changes only the failure mark.
  - **What the architect does.** It sees only the items code left `unsure`. For each it answers `met`, `not-met` or `unsure` with one sentence (at most 300 characters), the field, and the criterion when the sentence is about one. It proposes no text and writes no plan. Results have `by: "architect"` and store the text they were about. A result of code is never overruled, and the run never changes a field or an "accepted anyway" reason.
  - **Changed draft.** If the draft, a criterion or the map changed while the run worked, the run fails with "The draft changed while the architect judged it; check readiness again" and nothing is stored. An item the admin reworded or removed during the run is skipped.
  - **Bad answers.** An answer that misses an item, names an unknown item, adds extra keys or breaks a limit fails the run with one plain sentence that holds no text of the answer (`tools/refine-round-check`), and nothing is stored.
  - **Log.** New log kinds `ready-asked` and `architect-judged`, with the counts of met, not met and unsure.
  - **Files.** `flows/refine-round.yaml` is generated from `scripts/build-flows.mjs` (new `ready` ask). The permission rule text for the route and its row in `USER_GUIDE.md` changed. No new route; the flow format does not change.
- Platform 2b — steps of a user's run see only a short list of environment variables (#300).
  - **Behaviour.** A step of a run owned by a user no longer gets a copy of the server's environment. A shell step gets `PATH`, `HOME`, `USER`, `LOGNAME`, `SHELL`, `TMPDIR`, `TMP`, `TEMP`, `LANG`, `LANGUAGE`, `LC_ALL`, `TZ`, `TERM`, the data-folder variables (`FACTORY_HOME`, `FACTORY_LOCK_DIR`, `SCF_HOME`, `SCF_LOCK_DIR`), the step's own `FACTORY_…` and isolation variables, and the names an admin lists. An agent step gets the same plus exact names for its own agent and provider: Claude on an Anthropic provider `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN`, `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN`, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`; Codex on OpenAI `OPENAI_API_KEY`, `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, `CODEX_API_KEY`, `CODEX_HOME`; other provider kinds only the config-folder variable. Keys of other providers are not passed. Admin runs and runs without an owner are unchanged. A step in Docker still gets only `FACTORY_`/`SCF_` names and the commit identity; `step_env.pass` does not go into containers.
  - **Setting.** New `step_env` in `config.yaml`: `pass` (names for all steps) and `agent_pass` (names for agent steps only). A name may end in `_*` to match a prefix. Names starting with `FACTORY_`, `SCF_`, `GH_`, `GITHUB_`, `GIT_` or `SSH_` are refused, and a provider key variable is never passed by a list. The server variable `SCF_STEP_ENV_PASS` (or `FACTORY_STEP_ENV_PASS`) adds extra names, comma separated.
  - **Upgrade.** A flow that relies on an inherited variable in a user's run (`JAVA_HOME`, `NVM_DIR`, a proxy setting) stops seeing it. Add the name to `step_env.pass` (or `step_env.agent_pass` if only agents need it).
  - **Redaction.** The values of the `api_key_env` variables named in the config and of the well-known key variables are hidden in logs, live output and API answers, like stored credentials. A value shorter than 8 characters is not hidden; the server logs one line for each such variable.
  - **Code.** New `src/engine/short-env.ts`; `src/config.ts`, `src/agents/targets.ts` (`providerKeyVars`), `src/agents/run.ts`, `src/credentials/redact.ts` (`hideKeyVars`), `src/steps/process.ts` (`cleanEnv`), `shell.ts`, `claude.ts`, `codex.ts`, `src/engine/execute.ts`, `runner.ts`, `src/server/server.ts`. Tests: `tests/short-env.test.ts`, `tests/key-vars-api.test.ts`, and extended redact, agents, isolation and refine-access tests. The fake `claude` and `codex` have a `SHOWALLENV` directive that prints their variable names. Two assertions changed: an admin run on a configured key variable shows `[redacted]`, and a user's step no longer inherits `GIT_TRACE_REDACT`.
  - **Docs.** `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` shows SR-O2 as fixed and what stays open; the user guide says which variables a user's run gets and how an admin adds one.
  - **Still open.** The agent's own provider key is visible to the agent's shell tool unless the agent CLI hides it.
- Refinement 6e — "Architect's view" on the draft page (#289).
  - **Behaviour.** Each open draft has a part "Architect's view" with a button to ask for it and the usual architect status line. It shows the areas with their files, what the draft depends on and what depends on it, the risks with their reasons, the size with estimated files and lines, the fit sentence and the overlaps with other open stories and drafts. Every statement is marked *found in the code* or *estimate*; the size and fit sentence are always *estimate*. A found overlap says "Cannot be built at the same time"; an estimated one says "May touch the same code — likely cannot be built at the same time". When a plan review is recommended, the page says why and offers the checkbox "Add the review label when this story is published" (off until ticked; stored through the existing review-label route). A view written before the last change shows "out of date" with Ask again. No button changes a field and nothing is disabled; viewers who cannot change the session see the view without buttons. No hours or days are shown.
  - **Out of date now includes the notes.** `draftMark` in `src/refinement/draft-impact.ts` also covers the notes for the builder, because the architect reads them. Views stored before this change show "out of date" once until asked again.
  - **Code.** New `ui/refinement-impact.js`; `ui/refinement-draft.js`, `ui/api.js` (`askImpact`, `setReviewLabel`), `ui/refinement-talk.js`, `ui/refinement.js` (words for the `impact` log kinds), `ui/style.css`. No new route; the flow format did not change. Tests: new `tests/ui-refinement-impact.test.ts`; `tests/ui-refinement-talk.test.ts`, `tests/ui-refinement-architect.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-impact.test.ts`, `tests/refinement-impact-api.test.ts`.
  - **Docs.** User guide: "The architect's view on the page".
  - **Not tested.** A viewer whose repository is gone has no view (the server hides the drafts), so there is no read-only case for it.
- Platform 2c — the admin decides which GitHub App repositories an account may connect (#301, SR-O3).
  - **Behaviour.** Each account has a list for the app method: `owner/name` or `owner/*`, stored in the new `app-repos.json`. Adding a repository, changing its method to the app and testing an app connection answer 403 ("the administrator has not allowed your account to connect this repository through the GitHub App…") when the repository is not on the caller's list; GitHub is not asked. The check runs again under the lock that saves the connection. An account without a list can connect nothing through the app. An admin's own connections are not limited. Runs, watchers and the admin's transfer are not checked, so existing connections keep working, also after an entry is removed. `acme/*.git` is refused; only exact names lose `.git`.
  - **Admin.** Users page: "App repositories" button. Repositories page: a "not on the app list" mark on an app connection whose owner is a user and is not on the list. Deleting an account removes its list, and stops if `app-repos.json` is corrupt.
  - **API.** `GET /api/users/:id/app-repos` and `PUT /api/users/:id/app-repos`, admin only. New audit event `app-repos-change`, written only when a list changes.
