  - **Page.** New page **Refinement** (`#/refinement`, `#/refinement/<id>`) for every account. The list shows title, repository, state and last change (an admin also sees the owner), with **Open sessions** and **Dropped** filters and **New session**. The session page shows the idea, the story drafts (empty for now) and the log (who, what, when), with **Rename**, **Drop** and **Restore**. A user now sees **Refinement**, **Runs** and **My repositories**.
  - **Calls.** `GET /api/refinement` (200 `{sessions, repos}`; a short summary per session, `repos` are the account's GitHub repositories). `POST /api/refinement {repo, idea, title?}` (201; 400 bad input or the 200-session limit, 403 repository not yours, 404 no account). `GET /api/refinement/<id>` (200 with idea, drafts and log; 404). `PUT /api/refinement/<id> {title}` (200; 400, 403 not the owner, 404, 409 dropped). `POST /api/refinement/<id>/drop` (200; 404, 409 already dropped). `POST /api/refinement/<id>/restore` (200; 403 not the owner, 404, 409 not dropped). A missing session answers `no such refinement session`. A store problem answers a plain 500; the log names the file and the kind only.
  - **File.** New `refinements.json` (version 1, mode 0600) in the data folder, copied unchanged by the data-folder move. States: exploring, drafting, ready, published, dropped. A new session is *exploring*; for now only Drop and Restore change the state.
  - **Limits.** 200 sessions per account (dropped ones count until removed), idea 10,000 characters, title 120, 1,000 log entries per session (after that Rename and Restore are refused and the session can only be dropped; nothing is cut).
  - **30 days and who may do what.** A dropped session is removed after 30 days (on read, at start and on a timer). An owner sees and changes only their own sessions. An admin sees all sessions and the owners, and may drop; rename and restore stay with the owner. The owner sees an admin's log entry as "an administrator".
  - **Account and repository.** Deleting an account deletes its refinement sessions, and stops with nothing changed when `refinements.json` or `repos.json` cannot be read. Removing a repository keeps its sessions; the page says the repository is gone. Only GitHub repositories can be used.
  - **Code.** `src/refinement/store.ts`, `src/server/api-refinement.ts`, `ui/refinement.js`; rules in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The architect steps, story drafts and publishing to the backlog. Nothing here calls an AI agent or GitHub.
- My repositories page (#66). New page **My repositories** (`#/repos`) for every account: it lists your repositories with the URL, the authentication method and the connection status ("Not tested yet" until the connection test exists). **Add repository** asks for the URL and the method: a GitHub fine-grained personal access token (repository permissions Contents, Issues and Pull requests, each "Read and write") or an HTTPS user name + token. The token is typed in a password field and never shown again. A repository can be removed (with a confirmation) and its authentication changed. When a removal reports that an old key is still in the Keychain, the message stays on the page with **Try again**. An admin can also choose "The server's own access". A user now sees **Runs** and **My repositories**. Code: new `ui/repos.js`; errors thrown by `ui/api.js` now carry the HTTP status; no API change. Not included: the connection test, SSH deploy keys, the GitHub App, per-repository settings. The dialog fields are spaced like the other dialogs, and an admin who opens a longer address such as `#/repos/x` gets the list.
- Users API for admins (#63).
  - **Calls.** `GET /api/users` (200, a list of accounts with `id`, `name`, `email`, `role`, `status`, `created`, `lastSignIn`, `runs`, `hasPassword`; never a hash or a token). `POST /api/users {name, email, role}` (201 `{user, token, expires}`; 400 for bad input, 409 for a taken e-mail). `PUT /api/users/<id> {name?, email?, role?}` (200 `{user}`; 404, 409 for the last admin or a taken e-mail). `POST /api/users/<id>/block {stopWork?}` (200 `{user, cancelled}`; 409 for the last admin). `POST /api/users/<id>/unblock` (200 `{user}`). `POST /api/users/<id>/link` (200 `{user, token, expires}`; 409 when the account has a password). `DELETE /api/users/<id>` (200 `{ok, credentials, cancelled}`; 409 for the last admin). All are admin-only; a user gets `403 {"error":"not allowed for your role"}`. A field that is in the body must be valid; `null` is a bad value. An admin may act on their own account within the last-admin rule; blocking or deleting yourself signs you out.
  - **At once.** Block and delete cancel queued jobs before they answer; `stopWork` also cancels running and waiting runs and returns the numbers (`cancelled: {queued, running, waiting}`). The server log says `account <id> blocked|deleted|stop-work: cancelled …`, with the account id only.
  - **Audit.** `by` is the admin's account id; new action `edit` for a name or e-mail change. **Changed:** such a change wrote no line before. A call that fails writes no line, so a block or delete that stops with an error may already have signed the account out or removed its repositories and stored credentials.
  - **Old key.** Delete answers 500 with `the account was deleted, but ` and the credentials sentence when an old key stays in the Keychain; the account is gone.
  - **Errors.** A store problem answers a plain 500; the log names the file and the kind (`users: audit.jsonl cannot-write`), never a value or a path.
  - **Code.** `src/server/api-users.ts`, `cancelAccountNow` in `src/server/account-work.ts`, `OLD_KEY_LEFT` in `src/server/api-credentials.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The Users page (part 5), password reset for an account that has a password, changing the watcher `owner` when an e-mail changes.
- Failures explained (#39).
  - **Behaviour.** A failed run explains itself in plain words: what failed, why, the kind of problem, what the Foundry already tried ("3 fix attempts", tries inside a step, resumes) and four options (Retry, Retry with a hint, Change the plan, Close). It is shown as a card at the top of the run page and in the failure comment on the issue. The four kinds: a problem in the code, a problem with the environment or the Foundry, a limit, a person's decision. When the fix needs a new run (for example a used-up run budget), Retry says "start a new run" instead of "resume".
  - **Model call.** After a failed run whose rules say "code", one short call to a small model (`failure_summary.model`, default `haiku`) reads the end of the failing output and writes one sentence. The call has no tools (`--tools ""`), no MCP servers and no GitHub tokens, runs in an empty temp folder with a 45 second limit, and costs at most 0.05 USD or what is left of the run and daily budgets (no call below 0.01 USD). It does not run for stopped or cancelled runs, for limits, decisions and Foundry failures, or for Codex models. The cost is added to the run. The answer is redacted and cleaned (no Markdown, links, mentions or folders) and kept as `failureNote`; the page and the comment say when a model wrote the sentence. A model answer of "environment" turns a code failure into a Foundry failure. The terminal state is saved first, the note in a second save. Resuming removes the note. The log of the call is `logs/failure-summary.log`.
  - **Option.** `failure_summary.enabled` (default `true`) and `failure_summary.model` in `config.yaml`. Switch the call off for tests and special setups with `SCF_NO_FAILURE_MODEL=1` / `FACTORY_NO_FAILURE_MODEL=1`. Codex-only installs get the rules text; a local model works with `model: ollama:<model>`.
  - **Formats.** New optional `failureNote` (`kind`, `why`, `by`) in `run.json`; optional `retried` (`blips`, `models`) and `unreachable` on a step record; new `failure_summary` key in `config.yaml`. Old files load unchanged. An older build rejects a `config.yaml` that has the new key.
  - **Changed.**
    - On a failed run page the summary card replaces the "What happens next" block, the page opens on Steps (unless you picked a tab), and the raw reason moved into the collapsed **Raw details** of the card. "Show the failed step" opens that step.
    - The failure comment has What happened, Why, Kind of problem, Already tried and Your options, also when the Foundry itself failed.
    - A step budget failure counts as a limit, a rejection as a person's decision, and repeated rejections that use up `max_visits` stay a decision. At `max_visits` the summary names the step that failed last.
    - Git or gh login and network errors, wrong model or provider settings and a missing `claude` or `codex` program count as a Foundry failure.
    - A limit, an unreachable service or a `max_budget_usd` stop inside a parallel or sub-flow step is read from the failing child step.
    - A signed-out agent reads "paused — signed out" and an unreachable AI service (also an overloaded one, HTTP 529) "paused — AI service not reachable"; both read "paused — usage limit" before. A real quota or rate limit (429) still reads "paused — usage limit".
    - The comment's "Last failing step" and its raw details come from the step that explains the failure, not from a parallel or sub-flow wrapper.
  - **Code.** New `src/failure-explain.ts` (`explainFailure`, `explainPrompt`, `parseNote`, `noteFrom`); `src/failure.ts` gets `failureSummary`, `triedOf`, `triedText`, `safeSentence`, `hideFolders`; `runClaude` gets the `noTools` option and a stable "claude CLI not found" error; `isQuotaError` in `src/agents/targets.ts`; `failure` on the next-step record (`NextStep.failure`); `failureCard` and `failedStepIndex` in `ui/runs.js`.
  - **Not included.** Buttons for Change the plan and Close (both work through GitHub today and are described in words); a failure card for paused runs; a field for `failure_summary` on the Settings page.
  - **Needs a check with the real CLI.** It assumes the installed `claude` accepts `--tools ""`. If not, the call fails and the rules text is used.
- Checked with real situations (#42).
  - **Scenario list.** `tests/helpers/scenarios.ts` holds 14 real situations (questions, risky plan, failed baseline, session limit, signed out, issue closed on GitHub, interrupted run, watcher error, release, …). `tests/scenarios.test.ts` checks each against the next-step record (who, action, sentence, first line of a comment), the Your turn list and the Your turn page (buttons, link). Epic #31 could not be read, so cases recorded in this repository were used; scenarios with no dated case say `kind only`.
  - **Measure.** The server takes a sample every minute into `clarity.json` in the data folder (local only). A *wait* is the time an item is on Your turn; it ends when the item leaves the page or moves to "Done — continuing". A *miss* is something that needs you (on the Board, in a watcher, or a run) but is not on Your turn, seen in two samples in a row; it should be 0. Dismissed waits are counted apart. A file of a newer version is left alone; a broken one is replaced. `GET /api/clarity` (admin only) and a "Your turn in numbers" card on the Dashboard show it. Code: `src/clarity.ts`, `src/server/clarity.ts`.
  - **Fixed:** a signed-out agent now says "Sign in again: …" as the action instead of "Nothing — it continues by itself".
  - **Fixed:** an issue closed on GitHub while its run is still busy (running, queued or waiting) is now also on Your turn. It was only in the health line and on the Board. It can be dismissed and goes away when the run ends.
  - **Usability check.** `docs/USABILITY_CHECK.md` has the five screens, the 10-second procedure, a result table, an answer key and the source of each scenario.
  - **Not included:** the check with the owner itself. The follow-up issue could not be opened by the Foundry; `docs/USABILITY_CHECK.md` has a pre-filled link to open it by hand.
  - **Not included.** (The Users page came with #65.) Password reset for an account that has a password, changing the watcher `owner` when an e-mail changes.
- One status comment per issue (#53).
  - **Behaviour.** An issue watcher keeps one comment on every issue it follows with the next step (the record the app shows, in plain words for a reader of the issue). It is edited, never posted again. It also covers done issues, a wait for the scheduled release, an issue closed while its run works, and an issue the watcher no longer follows (last text). Two watchers on one issue share the comment. Status comments never count as questions, answers or approval requests. Extra status comments of the Foundry's own account are removed (at most 5 per read); a comment of another account is never touched.
  - **Option.** `status_comment` on a watcher, `true` by default. Off, removed or disabled watchers leave their comments as they are.
  - **First check after the upgrade.** One new comment (one notification) per open issue a watcher tracks, at most 30 per check; the rest follows at the next check. A check spends at most 60 seconds on comments, and a failing comment only shows in the log.
  - **File.** `status-comments.json` in the data folder keeps the issue numbers (per repository and watcher) that have a status comment, so a last text is written after a restart. Bodies and comment ids stay in memory.
  - **Needs a check on a real repository.** It assumes `gh issue view --json comments` returns `url` and `viewerDidAuthor`, `gh issue comment` prints the comment link, and `gh api … --input -` takes JSON on stdin.
  - **Code.** `src/queue/status-comment.ts` (`statusBody`, `leftBody`, `statusTargets`, `StatusComments`), `src/issue-record.ts` (`issueRecord`, shared with `src/server/next.ts`), `upsertStatusComment`, `isStatusComment`, `commentId` and `sameBody` in `src/github.ts`.
- Fixed: a run that stepped aside for a busy code area was restarted again and again while the other run still worked. The endless checks used up GitHub's request limit and showed as a watcher error. It is now restarted only when the other run has stopped working.
- A watcher error caused by GitHub's request limit now says so, and that it continues by itself, instead of asking to check the login.
- Fix flaky tests: the "next story starts right away" tests wait for the event without a tight time limit (they failed on a busy machine and blocked stories).
- Gitflow: tests before the change and after the merge into `develop` run once more when they fail (a timing-sensitive test can fail by chance on a busy machine); a fix attempt that changes nothing just tests again instead of failing.
- One-time set-password link for a new account (#62).
  - **The link.** `<address>/#/set-password/<token>`. The token is 43 characters, works once and for 7 days, and sits after `#`, so it is in no request line or log. The page asks for the new password twice, then shows the sign-in form. Every link that is not live (used, expired, replaced, unknown, malformed, blocked or deleted account) gets the same answer: `this link is not valid any more; ask your admin for a new one`.
  - **`POST /api/set-password`** with `{token, password}`. No session or CSRF token is needed. `200 {"ok":true}` without a cookie; no session is started. `400` for a dead link (also when the password is short) or a password that breaks the rules with a live link (the link stays), `429` when the client is over its limit or the server is busy. The limits are shared with sign-in; a used link clears the wrong-tries count of that e-mail. If the audit line cannot be added after the password was set, the answer is `500`; try to sign in with the chosen password first.
  - **`scf user list`** shows `no password yet`. **`scf user password`** also works for such an account and removes its link.
  - **Formats.** In `users.json`, `passwordHash` may be missing, and there is a new optional `passwordLink` (`id` = SHA-256 of the token, `expires`). Two accounts cannot have the same link id. Old files load unchanged; an older build rejects a `users.json` that has such an account.
  - **Audit.** New action `link`. A password set through a link is a `password` line with `by` = the account id.
  - **Changed.** An admin without a password does not count as another admin for the last-admin rule.
  - **Code.** `createUserWithLink`, `newPasswordLink`, `redeemPasswordLink`, `LINK_TTL_MS` in `src/auth/users.ts`; `linkToken`, `linkHash` in `ui/auth.js`.
  - **Not included.** The admin's "Add user" call and dialog (parts 4 and 5), password reset for an account that has a password, changing your own password, e-mail, a link from `scf user create`.
- A run that waits for a person (questions, a decision) ends by itself when its issue is closed on GitHub, so it no longer shows as needing attention.
- Gitflow merge: changes left on the feature branch are committed (never dropped); a failed switch to `develop` or a push error stops the run with the real message instead of looping; only a `develop` that moved meanwhile is merged again.
- Claude's "session limit" (and weekly / 5-hour limits) pause a run like a usage limit instead of failing it.
- A briefly unavailable AI service ("at capacity", overloaded, a network blip) no longer fails the story: the step is tried again after 1 and 3 minutes, and only then paused like a usage limit.
- Flows cleaned up: only the gitflow pipeline (`epic-questions`, `issue-gitflow`, `release-daily`) and the human-in-the-loop pipeline (`issue-plan`, `issue-code-daily`, `daily-pr`) ship. Retired: `quick`, `feature`, `cross-review`, `github-issue`, `github-pr`, `github-auto`, `issue-deliver`, `pr-feedback`, `ci-fix`, `chore`, `jira-ticket`, `linear-ticket` (kept as test material). Watcher defaults: issues → `issue-gitflow`, schedule → `release-daily`. A flow in use by a watcher (also a disabled one) or by another flow can't be deleted.
- Blocking or deleting an account stops its work (#61).
  - **What is cancelled.** The jobs the account queued are dropped; its running runs finish; runs that wait for approval stay. The server checks every 2 seconds (`accountSweepMs`) and at start, before any job starts. A job queued by a blocked or deleted account never starts, also after a restart. Deleting an account does the same.
  - **`scf user block <e-mail> --stop-work`** also cancels the account's running runs and its runs that wait for approval. Workspaces are kept. The server handles the request once and removes it; `scf user unblock` removes a request that was not handled yet and restarts nothing.
  - **Admins** can still resume, approve or reject a blocked account's run, and that job runs. An admin's action made before the server handles a `--stop-work` request is cancelled with the rest.
  - **Formats.** New optional `stopWork` on an account in `users.json` (only while a request waits for the server; an older build rejects a `users.json` that has it) and optional `queuedBy` in `queue.json`. Old files load unchanged.
  - **Audit.** A `block` line in `audit.jsonl` has `stopWork` (true or false). Older block lines still parse.
  - **Log.** `account <id> blocked|deleted: cancelled <q> queued, 0 running, 0 waiting` and `account <id> stop-work: cancelled <q> queued, <r> running, <w> waiting`. Account ids only; no names, e-mails or paths.
  - **Code.** `Scheduler.cancelAccount` and `enforceAccounts`, the `accountActive` option, new `src/server/account-work.ts`, `takeStopWork` in `src/auth/users.ts`.
  - **Not included.** The UI (Part 4). Watchers of a blocked account keep working: disable the watcher or change its owner.
- First line in more comments (#58): the plan, result and split comments of the ticket flows and the split comment of `create-split` start with `**Nothing needed from you**` or `**What you need to do:** …`. The `github-auto` split says `Start the new issues when you want them built.` when they are not labelled. New variables `FACTORY_FIRST_INFO`, `_MERGE_PR`, `_OPEN_PR`, `_START_PARTS`, `_START_CODING`, `_SHIPS`, `_LOOK`, `_MERGE_RELEASE`, `_DRAFT` (also `SCF_FIRST_…`), from `reportFirst()` in `src/next-step.ts`. `FACTORY_FIRST_DRAFT` is a "Nothing needed from you" line (changed in #59). A finished split is recognised with and without the first line. A test (`tests/helpers/comment-scan.ts`) finds comments that miss the line (no exceptions left since #59). Headings, closing sentences and hidden markers are unchanged.
- First line in the remaining comments (#59): the plan of `issue-plan` starts with `**What you need to do:** Add the code label to start coding.`; the result of `issue-code-daily`, `issue-deliver` and `issue-gitflow` with `**Nothing needed from you** — it goes to main with the release pull request.`; the reply of `pr-feedback` with `**What you need to do:** Look at the changes.`; the daily report (`daily-pr`) and release check (`release-daily`) with `**What you need to do:** Merge the release pull request when you like.` when the checks pass, else `**Nothing needed from you** — it stays a draft until the checks pass.`
  - The daily report prints "Merge whenever you like" only when the checks pass.
  - Result comments say "release pull request", not "daily pull request" or "Foundry pull request".
  - The `issue-code-daily` implement prompt says the bold first line is for the owner, not a step of the plan.
  - `build-flows.mjs` fails when a `.replace` text is not found (`swap()`).
  - Headings and hidden markers are unchanged.
- Answer and approve inside the app (#34). On **Your turn**, an item of a watcher that has an issue now has buttons that do what you would do on GitHub.
  - **Questions:** **Show questions** lists each question with its options and recommendation. **Accept all recommendations** posts `/defaults`. **Answer…** gives one box per question (one "Your answer" box when the comment has no numbered questions), posted as one comment. Every question needs its own answer; **Use recommendation** fills a box with "Go with the recommendation." where you can see it. Nothing is filled in for you.
  - **Plans, splits and requests:** **Show plan**, **Show split** or **Show request** shows the risk score and reason (a split has a score but no reason), why you decide, and the text. **Approve** (optional notes) posts `/approve <notes>`; **Reject** needs what to change and posts `/reject <text>`.
  - **Failed issues:** **Retry** removes the failed label (the watcher's own name for it). **Retry with a hint…** posts the hint as a comment first, then removes the label. If the label call fails after the hint was posted, pressing Retry again does not post the hint twice.
  - **Normal comments.** Every action is a normal comment on the issue, posted under the GitHub login the Foundry uses and ending with "— <your name>, via Spaghetti Code Foundry". The text goes to `gh` through stdin. The GitHub way keeps working, and the watcher's own check stays the only path that resumes a run.
  - **Checks before posting.** Approve and reject need write access for the Foundry's GitHub login; without it (or when GitHub answers 403/404) the answer is 409 and nothing is posted; other GitHub errors are 502. Only comments by that login count as the Foundry's (the hidden marker can be copied). The server re-reads the issue and refuses (409) when the comment changed since you opened it, or when someone already answered (for approvals: someone with write access already sent `/approve` or `/reject`). It also refuses when the item changed (stamp), when the watcher no longer holds it, while the same item is being handled, and for text over 60 000 characters or with a Foundry marker. Calls to GitHub time out after 15 s.
  - **Done — continuing.** The watcher is asked to check after 1 s, and the item moves to a **Done — continuing** group without buttons. It stays there until the watcher picks it up, or for at most 10 minutes (then it shows as waiting again). This state is in memory only.
  - **API.** `GET /api/your-turn/detail?key=…` returns the questions, plan or split with a `stamp` and a `digest` of the comment shown. `POST /api/your-turn/act` takes `key`, `action` (`defaults`, `answer`, `approve`, `reject`, `retry`, `retry_hint`), `stamp`, `digest`, `text` and `answers`, and returns the page. Both are admin-only. Items have a new `acts` list, and the page a new `continuing` list; both are additive.
  - **Code.** New `src/turn-actions.ts` (reading and writing the comments), `src/server/turn-actions.ts` (routes and checks) and `ui/turn-act.js`. `src/github.ts` gets `repoPermission`, `mayWrite`, `ghLogin`, `commentOnIssue` and timeout parameters; `canWrite` behaves as before.
  - **Not included.** Actions for users with the role `user`; runs without a watcher (they keep the run page); Markdown rendering of plans; **Accept all recommendations** for planner questions (answer them in the box); a reason for a split's risk score (the comment has none).
- Repository records (#64).
  - A repository is a record: id, owner, URL, method and time added. URLs: `https://host/path`, `ssh://[user@]host[:port]/path`, `git@host:path`, or `owner/name` (GitHub). The URL is kept as written. Local paths, `file:`, `ext::`, other transports, a user name or password in the URL, an empty user name or port, and control characters give 400.
  - Methods: `github-token`, `https-token` and `none`. The token is stored as `repo:<id>` in the credential store and never shown in answers or logs.
  - 409 when the repository is already yours or belongs to another account (same repository in any case, with `.git`, in https or ssh form).
  - `PUT /api/repos/<id>/auth` (fields not given keep their value; the address may change to another form of the same repository) and `DELETE /api/repos/<id>` wipe the old token. A retry also removes an old Keychain key that a first try could not remove. `POST {name}` and `DELETE /api/repos/<owner>/<name>` still work.
  - An explicit method `none` is for admins only (403 for a user); without a method a repository has `none`, which means the server's own access for an admin and "needs authentication" for a user.
  - **Changed:** `GET /api/repos` answers records instead of names, and `POST /api/repos` answers the record instead of `{name}`. The UI does not use these calls yet.
  - **Changed:** one repository can belong to one account only; old double entries stay. A credential name must not start with `repo:`.
  - `repos.json` is now version 2; version 1 loads as is and is rewritten on the first change. An older build cannot read version 2. A data-folder move copies `repos.json` unchanged.
  - Not included: the page, SSH deploy keys, the GitHub App, the connection test, admin settings, transfer, and runs that use the token.
- Account rules and audit log (#60).
  - **`scf user role <e-mail> admin|user`** changes the role. It counts from the next API call; the account stays signed in.
  - **Last admin.** The only admin that is not blocked cannot be demoted, blocked or deleted. The command says "make another admin first", changes nothing and exits 1. **Changed:** blocking the only admin was allowed before, and a blocked admin no longer counts as another admin when deleting.
  - **`scf user list`** shows the last sign-in of each account ("never" when there is none), as a new last column.
  - **`audit.jsonl`** in the data folder (mode `0600`, copied unchanged by the data-folder move). Every `scf user` action that changes something (create, password, role, block, unblock, delete) adds one line: `time`, `by` (`cli`), `action`, `userId`, and for a role change `oldRole` and `newRole`. It never holds a password, hash, token, name or e-mail. A failed action writes no line, and a change that does nothing writes none either.
  - A command that cannot open `audit.jsonl` stops before it changes anything. If the line cannot be added after the change (for example a full disk), the command says so and exits 1.
  - **Code.** New `src/auth/audit.ts`, `openAppendLocked` in `src/auth/store.ts`, `updateUser` (name, e-mail, role) and the `by` option in `src/auth/users.ts`. Existing `users.json` files load unchanged.
  - **Not included.** Editing name or e-mail on the command line, the number of runs in `scf user list`, and an audit page.
- No money or setup for users (#11). What a user's API answers hold is now cut on the server, not hidden in the page.
  - **A user's run** (`GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:id` and the `update` events of the stream) has only: `runId`, `flow`, `task`, `status`, `startedAt`, `finishedAt`, `branch`, `resumes`, `owner`, `superseded`, `vars`, `flowDef` (`name`, `publish.enabled` and `version`, and each step's `id`, `type` and `description`), `history`, `state.next`, `waiting`, `next` and `questions`. No costs, tokens, budgets, prices, model, provider or agent, session ids, folders, `reason`, flow defaults, limits or prompts. `history` holds `id`, `type`, `visit`, `ok`, `startedAt`, `durationMs`, `parent` and a plain `error` sentence; there is **no step output**. A step of type `claude` reads `agent`. `vars` keeps only the variables the flow shows to users (`fixed` and `input`), plus `github_repo` and `issue`. Hidden variables are no longer in the owner's view (they stay in `run.json`). Folders of the run in the task, the approval message and the record are replaced by `(folder)`.
  - **Log.** A user's live log is rebuilt line by line from known shapes: steps starting and ending, tool names (never their arguments; an unknown tool reads `tool`), blocked tool names, waiting for approval and resumes. Cost, token, model, budget-fallback and start-error details are left out; a start error the Foundry cannot explain reads "something went wrong on the server; ask the administrator". Only the first line of an event is read, and a line of unknown shape is dropped.
  - **Questions.** When a run stops at `send_back` or `ask_for_info`, `questions` holds the output of that step (at most 4000 characters), and the run page shows it.
  - **Transcripts.** `GET /api/runs/:id/transcript/:n` is now admin only (403 for a user, also for their own run). The Steps tab for a user has no output and no transcripts.
  - **Wording for users.** A run stopped by the daily limit reads "paused — the administrator's limit was reached"; a run or step limit reads "stopped — the administrator's limit was reached". A signed-out agent reads "The Foundry is signed out of its AI account" and says to ask the administrator; a failure of the Foundry itself says the same. Failures use fixed sentences (`userError`), never the raw reason. The record's `kind` for the daily limit is `usage_limit` for a user. No `$`, "budget", model or agent name, Settings link or login command. The admin's words are unchanged. Watcher holds no longer replace a user's limit or failure record.
  - **Queue.** `GET /api/queue` for a user returns `{pending, active}`: no `concurrency`, and each pending job has only `runId`, `kind`, `enqueuedAt`, `waitingFor`, `githubRepo`, `issue`, `task`, `next` and `ahead`; `active` is `{runId}` only. A record's `repo` is empty unless it is `owner/name`.
  - **Errors.** An unexpected error on a call by a user is logged and answered `500 {"error":"something went wrong on the server; ask the administrator"}`; admins still get the message. The moved-data-folder 503 does not name the folder for a user. The "old key left" answer on `DELETE /api/credentials/:id` has a user wording without Keychain or `scf`. A saved flow that cannot be read answers a user 404 "flow not found" (not a file path). A resume, approve or reject of a run that is already queued or running answers `400 run <id> is already queued or running` for both roles.
  - **New rule for published flows.** An approval message is shown to users, so in a flow with `enabled: true` it may use only `{{task}}`, `{{vars.<name>}}` for a `fixed` or `input` variable (or `github_repo` or `issue`), and `{{steps.<id>.output}}`. Other placeholders (`{{vars}}`, `{{workdir}}`, `{{run.*}}`, `{{learnings}}`, other step fields) are refused on save. **After the upgrade** a published flow that breaks this is refused when loaded and leaves the users' list until you fix it; runs already started keep running.
  - **Run page.** A user has no Cost column or cost in the header, the tab is "Steps", and a step row cannot be opened.
  - **New code.** `src/server/user-view.ts` (`userRun`, `userLogLine`, `userError`, `userRecord`, `hidePaths`, `movedText`, `USER_ERROR`); `forUser` in `explainError`, `nextFor`, `queueWithNext` and `runNextStep`; user words in `src/words.ts`; `tests/user-view.test.ts`.
  - **Not included.** Notifications still go to the administrator's channels with the administrator's wording, also for runs users started, so do not point the Slack webhook at a channel users read. Free text a user can still read: the task, the approval message (and step output it prints), the questions and the diff. Hand-made queue or run files are not changed.
- Fix a race when several processes take over a dead lock at once (one could remove the fresh lock of another).
- Publish flows to users (#12). An admin chooses which flows users may start and what they may fill in, with a new `publish:` section in the flow YAML (so it travels with the file) and a "Publish to users" panel in the editor.
  - **After the upgrade:** users see no flows until an admin publishes them. Built-in and internal flows are not available to users unless published (save a copy and publish it).
  - **Format.** `publish: {enabled, version, name, description, vars}`. `name` and `description` are what users see (they fall back to the flow's own). Each entry in `publish.vars` must be a variable of the flow and has a `mode`: `hidden` (the admin's value; the same as leaving it out), `fixed` (shown to the user, cannot be changed) or `input` (the user fills it in; with `label`, `help`, `default` and `required`). An empty `default: ""` is kept. Labels and names are trimmed and cannot be empty. Unknown keys and keys that do not belong to a mode (for example `required` on `fixed`) are refused. The built-in flows are not changed.
  - **Versions.** `publish.version` is set by the server on save (`PUT /api/flows/:name`): the first publish writes `version: 1`; a changed definition gets one more than the highest version of every same-named copy in all scopes; saving an unchanged flow keeps its number; a higher number written by hand is kept. Turning `enabled` off keeps the number, and turning it on again counts as a change. A run keeps the flow it started with, so a later save does not change a running or waiting run; the run page shows "Flow version". A file edited outside the server does not get a new number by itself.
  - **Rules for published flows.** A flow with `enabled: true` cannot have `type: flow` steps (a sub-flow is loaded from disk when the step runs, so it would not be pinned). Names of variables users fill in use letters, digits, `_` and `-`, and must not give the same `FACTORY_VAR_*` environment variable as another variable of the flow. A shell step may not put such a variable into the command with `{{vars.name}}`; use `$FACTORY_VAR_NAME`. The flow is refused on save with a message for each of these.
  - **Answers changed.** `GET /api/flows` for a user is now a list of `{name, title, description, version, fields}` (before: `name` and `description`), for published flows only. `GET /api/flows` for an admin has `published: true` on published flows. `PUT /api/flows/:name` answers `{name, path, scope, yaml, version?}`: `yaml` is the text that was saved and `version` is there only for an enabled flow. `POST /api/runs` as a user: an unpublished flow gives 404; setting a variable that is not an `input` gives 403 `you cannot set the var "<name>"`; an empty required input gives 400 `fill in "<label>"`; hidden and fixed variables keep the flow's (or the folder's) value. A `github_repo` that is not an input and not the user's gives 403 `this flow works on a repository that is not one of yours`. A var named `__proto__` now gives 400 for everyone (it was dropped without a message).
  - **Editor.** The panel has "Available to users", "Name for users", "Description for users" and a row per variable (mode, label, help, and for inputs "Required" and "Own default"). The sidebar shows a `published` pill, and the save message shows the version. "+ Variable" now keeps an empty value instead of dropping it on the next visual edit.
  - **New files.** `src/flow/publish.ts`, `tests/publish.test.ts`, `tests/ui-publish.test.ts`.
  - **Not included.** A page for users to start runs, hiding values from the owner of a run (hidden values are still in the run's `vars` and flow definition, which the owner can read), and a warning when two admins save the same flow (the last save wins). This is not a security boundary: a user can still steer a run through the task text and the inputs they fill in.
- Run ownership (#10). A user sees only their own runs; an admin sees all of them.
  - **After the upgrade.** Runs without an owner are given to the first admin at the next server start, when the first admin is created on the setup page, and within a minute of `scf user create --admin` while the server runs. A run that is live is given over after it ends. Nothing changes for what admins see.
  - **Who owns a run.** The account that started it; for a watcher the account set as its `owner`, else the first admin; for the CLI and evals the first admin.
  - **New watcher setting `owner`.** The e-mail of an account; only admins can set it, and a new or changed value must match an account (else `PUT /api/config` answers 400 with the watcher id). `config.yaml` gets the key only when you set it; an older build rejects a config that has it.
  - **404 instead of 403.** A run that is not yours now answers `404 {"error":"run not found"}`, the same as an unknown run, on every `runs/:id` call (read, events, diff, transcript, cancel, resume, approve, reject). This replaces the `403 "not your run"` of #6. For a user, the id of another account's run is also left out of records, logs, step output and transcripts (a code-area wait reads "waiting for another run").
  - **Queue for users.** `GET /api/queue` is open to users and returns only their own queued and active runs; each queued run has `ahead`, the number of other accounts' runs in front of it. The Runs page shows "n runs ahead of you".
  - **For admins.** `GET /api/runs` has `ownerName` ("deleted account" when the account is gone) and `?owner=<account id>`; new `GET /api/run-owners` lists the accounts that have runs with their counts (over all runs). The Runs page has an Owner column and an owner filter.
  - **New code.** `src/auth/run-owner.ts`, `adoptRun()` in `src/engine/state.ts`, `firstAdmin()` in `src/auth/users.ts`, `ownRecord()`, `ownQueue()` and `hideForeign()` in `src/server/next.ts`. New setting `adoptEveryMs` for the server (default 60000).
  - **Not included.** An owner row on the run page, changing a run's owner, a list of accounts to pick the watcher's owner from, and a lock between processes that write the same `run.json`.

- Roles and permissions (#6). The role of an account (`admin` or `user`) is now checked on every API call, and the UI shows a user only **Runs**.
  - **After the upgrade:** accounts with the role `user` can only use Runs, their own repository list and their own credentials. Change a role with `scf user role <e-mail> admin|user`, or create an admin with `scf user create --admin` under another e-mail if you need one.
  - **What users may do.** List and read their own runs, start a saved flow, follow, cancel, resume, approve and reject (with a note) their own runs, list the published flows (name and description only), and manage their repositories and credentials. Everything else answers `403 {"error":"not allowed for your role"}`. A run that is not theirs, unknown, without an owner or with an unreadable `run.json` answers `403 {"error":"not your run"}`. Starting a run: `yaml` gives 403 "only an admin can run a flow that is not saved", `repo` gives 403 "only an admin can choose the folder", a bad flow name gives 400, an unknown flow 404, and a `github_repo` that is not one of the user's repositories gives 403 `"<name>" is not one of your repositories` (or `set the var "github_repo" to one of your repositories`). A flow without `github_repo` runs in the server's default folder, with the variables it had when it was queued.
  - **A user account is for people you trust with the machine.** A run executes commands as the server's Mac user, with the server's GitHub access. Roles limit the API and the pages, not what a run can do. To switch user runs off, change the rule `POST runs` to `no` in `src/server/permissions.ts`.
  - **404 for calls without a rule.** The table in `src/server/permissions.ts` decides first. A call with no rule now answers `404 {"error":"not found"}` for every role, also paths with extra segments (`/api/info/x`) and `POST /api/flows`, `GET /api/blocks/x` and `PUT /api/credentials`, which answered 405 before.
  - **`owner`** (the account id) is saved in `run.json` and `queue.json`, and is in the run briefs. Runs of watchers, the CLI and older versions have none and are admin-only. Old files load unchanged. The scheduler never gives the same run id twice, and a bad `run.json` no longer breaks the admin runs list.
  - **Repositories.** New `repos.json` in the data folder (mode `0600`) and `GET /api/repos`, `POST /api/repos {name}` (201, 409, 400; at most 50) and `DELETE /api/repos/<owner>/<name>`. `scf user delete` also removes the list, and stops before anything else changes when `repos.json` cannot be read.
  - **New files.** `src/auth/repos.ts`, `src/server/permissions.ts`, `src/server/api-repos.ts`, `tests/permissions.test.ts`, `tests/repos.test.ts`. The guide has a new part "Roles and permissions" with the generated table.
  - **Not included.** Runs that use a user's stored credentials, changing your own password in the UI, pages for users (starting runs, repositories), an admin view of other accounts' repositories, and isolation of user runs. The repository list is not a security boundary.

- First line in comments (#51): every Foundry comment that asks you something or reports a failure now starts with one bold line, `**What you need to do:** …` — questions up front, the planner's questions, a risky plan, a split, the push approval and the failure comment. A plan that starts coding by itself starts with `**Nothing needed from you** — it is being worked on.` The line comes from the next-step record (`firstLine()` in `src/next-step.ts`). Steps get it as `FACTORY_FIRST_QUESTIONS`, `FACTORY_FIRST_PLANNER_QUESTIONS`, `FACTORY_FIRST_APPROVE_PLAN`, `FACTORY_FIRST_APPROVE_SPLIT`, `FACTORY_FIRST_APPROVAL` and `FACTORY_FIRST_NOTHING` (also as `SCF_FIRST_…`). The failure comment no longer has a separate "What you can do" bullet; the first line says it. Headings, closing sentences and hidden markers are unchanged; `failureComment()` now takes the record. Runs started earlier keep their old text when resumed.
- Access from other computers (#20). New `server` settings (Settings → Network, or `config.yaml`): `listen` (default `127.0.0.1`; one of `127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `0.0.0.0`, `::`; needs a restart), `allowed_hosts` (replaces the fixed localhost check; live) and `allow_insecure_http` (default off; live). The user guide has a new section "Access from other computers" with a Caddy setup, what any proxy must do, and troubleshooting.
  - **HTTPS rule.** A request from another computer is refused with `403 HTTPS required` unless it comes over HTTPS through a proxy on this Mac (loopback peer with `X-Forwarded-Proto: https`), or `allow_insecure_http` is on. Forwarded headers are trusted only from a loopback peer. Unlisted hosts still get `403 forbidden host`.
  - **Origin check is stricter.** A change needs the exact origin of the request: scheme, name and port. Before, the scheme was ignored and `localhost` and `127.0.0.1` counted as the same.
  - **Headers.** Over HTTPS the session cookie is `Secure` and responses have `Strict-Transport-Security: max-age=31536000`. Every response has a strict `Content-Security-Policy` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. The plain-HTTP cookie is unchanged. The import map is gone: `ui/app.js` and `ui/library.js` import `/vendor/yaml/index.js`.
  - **Safety.** The server does not start on `0.0.0.0` or `::` without an admin account (it exits with a message naming `scf user create --admin`). `POST /api/setup` works only from the Mac itself. `PUT /api/config` answers 400 when the change would lock out the sending browser. `GET /api/info` has `listening`. A malformed URL such as `/%` now gets 400 instead of an error.
  - **Sign-in limits.** Besides the limit per e-mail: 60 tries per client address in 15 minutes (the address comes from `X-Forwarded-For` only for a proxy on this Mac), at most 16 password checks at once (else 429), and an e-mail longer than 254 characters is a wrong sign-in.
  - New `src/server/net.ts` holds the access rules. Not included: built-in TLS, a proxy on another machine, listening on one LAN address, a public URL for Slack links. After the first save `config.yaml` has a `server:` block, which an older build would reject.
- Stored credentials (#13). A user can store a token or an ssh key; it is encrypted, never shown again, and hidden from logs and output. There is no UI page yet, and runs do not use stored credentials yet. **macOS only:** the key lives in the macOS Keychain.
  - **Storage.** `credentials.json` in the data folder (mode `0600`, copied unchanged by the data-folder move). Each secret is encrypted with AES-256-GCM. The 32-byte master key is in the macOS Keychain (service `claude-factory-credential-key`, one item per key id), never in the data folder. The id, owner, type, name, created time and fingerprint are bound to each ciphertext, so changing any of them makes decryption fail. Writing the key is read back to check it.
  - **API.** `GET /api/credentials` (your own), `POST /api/credentials {type, name, secret}` (201) and `DELETE /api/credentials/<id>`. An answer holds only `id`, `type`, `name`, `created`, `lastUsed` and `fingerprint` (the first 16 hex digits of the SHA-256 of the secret). Types are `token` (8 to 4096 printable ASCII characters, one line, trimmed) and `ssh-key` (a PEM private key, up to 16384 characters, stored with LF line ends). 400 for bad input, 409 for a name you already use, 404 for an unknown id. If the store fails the answer is a plain 500 and the server log names the file and kind, never a value.
  - **Redaction.** Stored secrets (also JSON-escaped and URL-encoded, and the lines of a multi-line key) become `[redacted]` in step output, step logs, transcripts, the live log, run errors and reasons, scheduler and server log lines, the CLI's top-level error, and every authenticated API answer (`send()`), including text saved before the secret was stored. Output of a process is filtered line by line through one filter for the combined log, so halves on stdout and stderr cannot join. A partly printed line is held until its newline. Redaction follows `credentials.json` while a process runs. If credentials exist and the Keychain cannot be read, runs fail before they start ("the stored credentials cannot be read") and the API and logs show nothing from the changed store (fail closed).
  - **Wiping.** Deleting a credential or a user (`scf user delete`) replaces the master key (or drops it when no credential is left), so an older copy of the file cannot be read. An old key that cannot be removed from the Keychain is kept in `retiredKeyIds` and retried on every write; `DELETE` then answers 500, the CLI exits 1, and repeating the call or running `scf credential rotate-key` cleans it up.
  - **CLI.** `scf user delete <e-mail>` (account, sessions and credentials; refuses the only admin), `scf credential rotate-key` (re-encrypts everything under a new key) and `scf credential check` (stores, reads and removes a probe item).
  - **Code.** New `src/credentials/{keychain,store,redact,cli}.ts` and `src/server/api-credentials.ts`; `runProcess` takes an optional `redactor`; `withAuthLock` users call `removeCredentialsLocked`. `SCF_SECURITY_BIN` replaces `/usr/bin/security` and is for tests (the fake is `tests/fixtures/fake-security.mjs`).
  - **Not covered.** A UI page, using credentials in runs, other platforms, text written before a credential was saved (it is hidden in API answers but stays on disk), the task text in `run.json`, and other encodings such as base64 of `user:token`. Anyone who runs commands as your macOS user, flow steps included, can read the key.
  - **Please check once.** The real `/usr/bin/security` was not run when this was written: run `scf credential check` and `scf credential rotate-key` from a Terminal window.

- A health line at the top of every page (#56). Under the top bar every page says "All good", or the number of problems of the Foundry itself and one sentence for each, with its action and a link. It refreshes on every page change and every 30 seconds, and says so when the server does not answer. The problems are:
  - a server that waits to restart ("A new version is waiting — it restarts after 2 runs");
  - a usage limit, one per agent (Claude, Codex), for runs that stopped in the last hour, with the time it continues; a used-up daily budget (the link goes to Settings);
  - a watcher error, which names the repository, and a watcher that has not checked for 3× its interval (not while the server waits to restart);
  - an issue closed on GitHub while its run still works, with a **Cancel run** button that asks for confirmation;
  - runs that failed because of the Foundry (newest per issue, not replaced, finished in the last 7 days, at most 5). The line names the failure and its fix without the reason; the run page has the reason.
  - The time of the last successful check is shown per repository.
  - New `GET /api/health` (`ok`, `summary`, `problems`, `repos`), built in `src/server/health.ts` with `src/next-step.ts`. It holds only record fields: no titles, settings, tokens or paths, and links are only `https://…` or `#/…`. `GET /api/next` is unchanged.
  - The **Server** card on the Dashboard is gone; the health line replaces it.
  - A watcher error now names its repository everywhere ("The watcher for acme/app has an error"). A connection problem (`cannot access … with gh`, no host, `dial tcp`, timeout, HTTP 5xx) reads "The watcher for acme/app can't reach GitHub" with the action "Check the network and `gh auth status`". **Error details** now also show gh's first output line (`errorLine()` in `src/errors.ts`).

- Sign-in (#47): everyone signs in to the web UI with their own account. **After the upgrade, open the UI once and create the admin account on the setup page (or run `scf user create --admin`).** Until an admin exists the UI shows only that page, and `scf serve` / `scf ui` print a hint. There is no setting that turns sign-in off. The CLI, the watchers and runs that are already going need no account.
  - **Sign-in page and sign-out.** The UI shows a sign-in form, or "Create the admin account" while there is no admin. The signed-in name and a **Sign out** button are in the top bar. A call that gets 401 (the session ended) reloads the page, which then shows the form.
  - **API.** `GET /api/session` (`{ user | null, csrfToken?, setupNeeded }`), `POST /api/session {email, password}` (sign in), `DELETE /api/session` (sign out) and `POST /api/setup {name, email, password}` (the first admin; 409 once an admin exists) are public, like the static files. Every other `/api/…` route answers 401 `sign in first` without a session, also when the data folder has moved (the 503 for a moved folder, which names the new folder, is only shown to signed-in users). Sign-in answers 401 `wrong e-mail or password` for a wrong password and for an unknown e-mail alike, 403 for a blocked account (only after the password was right) and 429 after 10 wrong tries for one e-mail in 15 minutes (kept in memory; a restart clears it). Passwords are used exactly as typed, spaces included.
  - **Cookie.** `scf_session_<port>` with `HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Path=/; Max-Age=604800`. The port is in the name because cookies on `localhost` are shared between ports. There is no `Secure` flag, because the server speaks plain HTTP on 127.0.0.1. A new sign-in always makes a new token and ends the session of the cookie it came with.
  - **Sessions.** Kept in `sessions.json` in the data folder (mode `0600`, only the SHA-256 of the token). They last 7 days, are not renewed, survive a restart and move with the data folder. A missing, corrupt or invalid file counts as no sessions.
  - **CSRF.** Every call that is not a GET or HEAD needs the header `X-CSRF-Token` with the token from `GET /api/session` or from sign-in, else 403. Sign-in and setup are covered by the Origin check and the JSON content type.
  - **Sessions end** after sign-out, expiry, a password change or a block. `scf user password` and `scf user block` remove that account's sessions too. A sign-in that was checked against the old password cannot create a session after the change. An open run log stream is closed within 5 seconds (`ServerOptions.sessionRecheckMs`, default 4000, is for tests).
  - **Errors.** If the account files cannot be read or written, or are locked, sign-in answers a plain 500 `sign-in is not working; see the server log`. The server log has the file name and the kind of problem, never a path, password or hash.
  - **Roles are stored, not enforced.** Every active account can use every route until permissions are added.
  - New: `src/auth/sessions.ts`, `src/server/api-auth.ts`, `ui/auth.js`; `adminHint()` in `src/auth/cli.ts`; `checkSignIn()` and `startSession()` in `src/auth/users.ts`. Out of scope: managing accounts and changing a password in the UI, permissions per role, access from other machines, TLS.
- Plain error messages (#50): a failed run and a watcher error now read as what happened, why and what you can do — in the next-step record, notifications, the run page, the watcher card and the failure comment on the issue. The raw text is under **Details** on the run page and inside each step of the step list, under **Error details** on the watcher card, and in the collapsed **Details** of the issue comment. When the fix is a change to the flow, or the run has no step to resume at, the message says to start over instead of resume. The watcher's recent activity shows the plain text too. Stored reasons are unchanged, so older runs show the new text too. New module `src/errors.ts` (`explainError`); the failure comment is built by `failureComment()` in `src/queue/watcher.ts`.

- Plain status names in the app (#49): the Runs list (also "Needs you" and the queue card), the run page, the Dashboard **Waiting** card and the watcher cards show the status name from the next-step record instead of the run state ("stopped", "waiting for approval"). A **?** next to each status shows the record's two sentences; it opens with a click, Enter/Space or a tap. `GET /api/watchers` has a new `state` on each watcher (`name`: `active`, `error` or `disabled`, plus `status` and `help`), so the watcher's own state has a **?** too; an error is called "watcher error". The run page's "Next step" row is now "Current step" or "Resumes at step" and always says what the step is: its description, or the kind of step. Your turn says "2 stories wait for this" instead of "Holds back 2 stories".
- Board (#37): a new page shows where every story is, one board per repository (tabs when there is more than one). Columns: Your turn, Waiting for another story, Queued, Planning, Coding, Reviewing, Merging, Done (grouped Today / This week) and Failed. A card shows the issue number and title, the next-step sentence, the current step ("coding — step 12 of 29"), its open dependencies as small links ("after #88") and the usual link of the record. Clicking a card opens its run page (a story that never had a run is not a link). "What is in the way of #89?" on a card highlights the whole chain of stories that hold it back and dims the rest; the chain line lists every story in it, also those without a card, and "Show all" clears it. The page asks again every 5 seconds, one request at a time; after a GitHub link it asks the watcher to check at once.
  - Tracked issues show at any age. Other runs on an issue (hand-started, or of a watcher that no longer tracks it) show for 7 days after they end; a succeeded run that still waits for the scheduled release stays in Merging. A live run takes over a story before the watcher has checked again. Eval runs and runs without an issue are not shown. A run you cancelled yourself is under Your turn on the board, although the Your turn page does not list it.
  - The column of running work comes from the step names (for example `plan…` is Planning, `implement` is Coding, `review…` is Reviewing, `commit` and `push…` are Merging); other step names stay in the phase of the step before, and flows with unknown names show running work under Coding. The step line is also shown for failed or stopped runs (the step they resume at).
  - New API: `GET /api/board`. `GET /api/next` is unchanged. Tracked-issue entries now carry the issue's newest `runId` internally, and `knownRuns` is shared by Your turn and the board; Your turn behaves as before.
- A run that failed because of the Foundry says so and suggests the fix (#55). New module `src/failure.ts` (`classifyFailure`) sorts why a run did not finish into four causes: code, factory (the Foundry or its environment), limit and decision. Factory is chosen only on known messages: a blocked agent command, a push blocked by the protected-branch guard, a marker the Foundry could not read (no `PLAN_STATUS` line, no questions to ask, no `SUBTASK` lines), an internal error, an unknown step, a run that failed before any step ran (workspace, bot identity, GitHub App token) and an interrupted run. Failing tests, guards, reviews, too many visits and the secret scan stay code; budgets and the usage limit are limit; questions and approvals are decision. When in doubt it is code.
  - For a factory failure the next-step record reads "The Foundry failed, not the code: …", its action is the suggested fix followed by how to retry, and who is "Something is wrong". The same sentence shows on the run page, the Runs list, the watcher card, the Dashboard and in notifications. The `failed` help text has a factory version. Code failures keep their wording. Records of failed or interrupted runs carry the new field `cause` (`code`, `factory`, `limit` or `decision`) in `GET /api/runs`, `/api/queue`, `/api/watchers` and `/api/next`. An interrupted run gets `cause: "factory"` but keeps its own record, because a watched one resumes by itself. Runs saved before this change get a record too.
  - Step records have a new optional `denied`: the tool calls Claude Code refused (at most 5, `git push` left out), read from `permission_denials`. The live log shows each as `⚠ blocked: …`. The sentence shows only the tool and program of a blocked command (e.g. `Bash: curl`). When a command was blocked in a step before a code failure, the sentence gets a short hint. Absent for Codex, older Claude Code and older runs.
  - The failure comment on the issue starts with "itself failed on this issue, not the code" for a factory failure and ends with the record's sentence, so the fix is in it. The hidden marker is unchanged. No new label: the issue keeps the failed label and `status_labels` settings keep working.
- Fix a flaky test (run end time compared to the millisecond).
- Since you last looked (#41): after a break of 30 minutes or more, a strip under the header sums up what changed since your last visit, with links. It has five groups: stories done, stories merged into develop, releases to main, failed runs and things newly waiting for you (the same items as Your turn, without dismissed ones).
  - **What counts.** A story is *merged into develop* when its run has a successful `push_develop` step (even if a later step failed) and *done* when the run succeeded with a `commit` step; a story shows in one of the two, from its newest run. A run that failed shows under failed, unless a newer run of the same issue replaced it or it was interrupted. A *release* is a merged pull request whose body has the `release` or `daily` Foundry marker; ordinary Foundry pull requests do not count. Eval runs are left out. Flows without steps named `commit` or `push_develop` never show under done or merged.
  - **Per browser.** The last visit is kept in this browser (`localStorage`, key `scf.since`) and shared by its tabs. **Dismiss** hides the strip in all tabs and moves the visit forward. Nothing shows when nothing changed.
  - New API: `GET /api/since?since=<time>` (400 when the time is missing or invalid; a future time counts as now). The answer has `groups`, `total`, `complete` and `notes`. Nothing is stored on the server. Releases are read from GitHub for the repositories of all watchers (also disabled ones) and of the newest runs, at most 20, with an 8 s time limit, and a good answer is reused for 15 s.
  - When GitHub could not be read or a limit was hit (20 repositories, 200 merged pull requests, 2000 finished runs), the strip says so and `complete` is false. An empty answer like that is not shown, keeps the visit start and is asked again after 5 minutes.
  - `gh()` and `ghJson()` in `src/github.ts` take an optional time limit (`timeoutMs`); existing callers are unchanged. The modified time of `run.json` is now rounded to the millisecond instead of cut off (it could read 1 ms too low).
- Notifications only when it is your turn (#40): macOS and Slack now tell you only when something lands in Your turn (question, risky plan or split, release pull request, failure, watcher error), grouped ("3 things need you") and at most once per 5 minutes. A click opens the item.
  - **Success notifications are now off by default — switch them on in Settings** ("Also notify when a run succeeds"). Runs that finished before you switch it on are not told.
  - **The server (`scf ui` / `scf serve`) sends these messages. `scf run` without a running server no longer sends macOS or Slack messages; the command still runs.**
  - `notify.on` now applies to the command only, which is unchanged: it still runs for every finished run with the chosen outcomes.
  - New settings: `notify.successes` (default off), `notify.throttle_minutes` (default 5, at least 1), `notify.quiet_hours` (`from`, `to`; may cross midnight) and `notify.daily_summary_at` ("HH:MM", optional short summary: done since yesterday, waiting for you, expected today). Old settings still load.
  - Click-through needs `terminal-notifier` (`brew install terminal-notifier`); without it the macOS message shows as before. Slack messages carry an "Open" link. The Slack text no longer has the run id and cost line. `GET /api/info` has `clickThrough` on macOS.
  - A notice that no channel could deliver (failed program, Slack error or timeout) is not marked as told and is tried again at the next check.
  - New state file `notifications.json` in the data folder (what was told, last send, summary day). A broken file reads as empty. The first start after the upgrade tells everything currently in Your turn once, as one grouped message.
  - Fix: a run's "last written" time (`updatedAt`, and `finishedAt` of an interrupted run) is rounded to the millisecond instead of cut off.

- "Waits for …" texts of long dependency chains name each story once and the real cause at the root ("held up by #61 (…), #64 (…)"), and say which of them need you.
- Faster stories: when a run ends its repository is checked at once; a run waiting for a code area steps aside instead of holding a slot; docs and whole test folders are not locked; low-risk plans skip the plan revision and the second Codex round unless round 1 found a [high] problem. Before starting an issue again, its current state is read directly (the issue list can lag).
- Steps no longer inherit a Claude host session's variables (running the CLI from inside the Claude app could sign the command-line Claude Code out); the sign-out pause has its own clear message.
- A run whose agent is signed out (expired Claude Code or Codex login) pauses and says how to sign in, instead of failing; it continues by itself afterwards.
- Fix: find the Claude Code bundled with the Claude desktop app in its new folder layout (runs fell back to an old `claude` that can't use the newest models).
- Your turn (#33): a new page, the first one in the navigation, lists only what waits for you: questions, approvals, failed or stopped work, release pull requests (one item per pull request) and watcher errors. It never lists running, queued, limit-paused or waiting-for-another-story work, nor evaluation runs. Each item shows what, why (from the next-step record), one primary link and since when. Items are sorted by how many stories they hold back (also through other stories), then by age, and grouped by repository. The page refreshes every 5 seconds; after a GitHub link it asks the watcher to check at once. Hand-started runs (UI, `scf run`) count when they wait for approval (any age) or failed or stopped in the last 7 days; failed or waiting runs of non-issue watchers count the same way, and runs without a recorded origin are treated like watcher runs (eval runs named in eval reports are skipped). The empty state names the stories being built and the next release time (in the schedule watcher's time zone). A count badge shows in the navigation and the tab title ("(3) Foundry"), and the app opens on the page when something waits, also when the first item shows up after the watchers' first check.
  - **Dismiss** and **Show again**: dismissals are saved in `your-turn.json` in the data folder (written by rename, entries for gone items dropped after 30 days; a broken file reads as empty). An item reappears when its time or situation changes; watcher errors cannot be dismissed.
  - New API: `GET /api/your-turn`, `POST /api/your-turn/dismiss` (`{ key }`; 404 for an unknown item) and `POST /api/your-turn/restore`. `GET /api/next` is unchanged.
  - New `source` in `run.json` and on `GET /api/runs/:id`: who started the run (`ui`, `ui approve`, `cli`, `watcher <id> …`, `eval …`). Absent on runs of older versions. An interrupted run now gets a `finishedAt` (when `run.json` was last written). New `Scheduler.briefs()` and `listRunBriefs` list every run cheaply.
  - Watcher holds gain `since` (a time from GitHub) and `seen` (when this server first saw it); watcher status gains `pausedBy` (the pull request that pauses new work) and `errorSince`.
- Words (#48): one glossary (`src/words.ts`) decides the Foundry's words. Every next-step record has two new fields: `status`, a short plain status name such as "waiting for you — questions" or "paused — usage limit", and `help`, two sentences on what it means and what happens next. `GET /api/runs`, `/api/queue`, `/api/watchers` and `/api/next` return them. A pull request that brings finished work to main is now always called "release pull request" (the watcher's line said "daily pull request"). Label descriptions on GitHub say what the label means and what to do; the trigger label and, when the watcher sets `vars.review_plan_label`, the review label get one too. Existing labels get the new text the next time the server starts. The user guide has a new section "Words the Foundry uses".
- Watchers keep labels true and show their last successful check (#54): every check now compares the status label with the run. A run that is running or queued (e.g. approved or resumed in the UI) gets the working label, and the right label when it ends. A label that does not match the newest run is corrected (e.g. failed but the run waits for approval). A done label is left alone, and an issue without a status label starts over as before. An issue closed on GitHub while its run is running, queued or waiting for approval is not changed: the watcher reports one record (`closed_elsewhere`, "cancel the run if the work is no longer wanted") on its card, the Dashboard Waiting card, the run page, `GET /api/next` and the queue — unless the run closed the issue itself (report, split, merge). Cancel now also works on a run that waits for approval (the run becomes `cancelled`; removing a queued approval from the queue cancels it too), and a run cancelled on a closed issue only loses its status labels, with no failure comment. `WatcherStatus` has `lastOk` and `startedAt`; the Watchers page shows "last successful check …" instead of "last check". A watcher that has not finished a check for more than 3× its interval shows the new `watcher_stale` record (not together with an error, and not while the server waits to restart). A failed closed-issue scan and a wrong interval now show as a watcher error, and a check that takes longer than 10 minutes is given up so "Check now" works again. Activity lines name the label they set.
- Accounts (#46): new `scf user create|list|password|block|unblock`. Accounts are stored in `users.json` in the data folder (mode `0600`; a new data folder gets `0700`). Passwords are hashed with scrypt (N=32768, r=8, p=3, 64-byte key, 16-byte random salt per user). The password is asked twice on a terminal, or read from the first line of stdin — never an option or an environment variable. A file that cannot be read or is not valid is an error, never "no accounts". Two processes that change the store at once do not undo each other. Nothing needs an account yet. The data-folder move copies `users.json` and `sessions.json` unchanged. Two processes that take over a dead lock at the same moment can no longer both get in.
- Expected times (#38): a run that is not finished shows its progress ("Step 12 of 29" — the step's number in the flow). A running run also shows an estimate from earlier succeeded runs of the same flow in the same repository ("Estimate: about 20 min left (usually 25–40 min in total)"), and a "Taking longer than usual" note when a step runs more than 3× its usual time (and over 2 minutes) — a hint, not an error. Waiting items say how long the run they wait for still needs ("Continues: after #88 (about 20 min left)"), now also in the Runs list and its queue; only the first queued job behind a run shows it. Estimates need at least 3 earlier runs among the newest 500 and are hidden until then; they start again when the steps of a flow change, and a repository is told apart from the `owner/repo` placeholder. A new succeeded run counts at once. New: `next.timing` on runs, `afterRun` on records that wait for a run, `stepStartedAt` on a running run (in `run.json`; older runs simply lack it).

- Next step in comments (#45): the comments that ask you something — questions up front, the planner's questions, a risky plan, a split and the push approval — now end with the same sentence as the UI and notifications (from `src/next-step.ts`). Steps get these sentences as `FACTORY_NEXT_QUESTIONS`, `FACTORY_NEXT_PLANNER_QUESTIONS`, `FACTORY_NEXT_APPROVE_PLAN`, `FACTORY_NEXT_APPROVE_SPLIT` and `FACTORY_NEXT_APPROVAL` (also as `SCF_NEXT_…`); blocks, flows and `tools/post-questions` print the variable instead of their own "Reply …" text. The sentence is fixed per reason, so it has no question count or approval message. Headings and hidden markers are unchanged, and runs started earlier keep their old text when resumed.

- Next step in the UI (#44): the Dashboard **Waiting** card, the watcher cards, the Runs list and the run page show the next-step record through one renderer (`ui/next.js`): who has the next move, the action, the why, "Continues: …" when set, and the link from `where` (GitHub links open in a new tab, `#/…` links in the same tab). Lines where the move is "You" come first. A watcher error is a line from its record, with the raw error under "Error details". The run page has a "What happens next" block at the top that updates with each `update` event; it replaces the "Waiting for approval" card and the red reason line, and the raw reason is a "Reason" row. The Runs table shows the record's sentence under each task ("Task / what happens next"), and the queue line uses it. A Server card on the Dashboard shows when the server waits to restart. "Needs you" and the "Needs a human" tile now count runs whose next move is "You": no longer runs paused by a limit, the budget or a release without a pull request; now also cancelled or interrupted runs that no watcher resumes. New: `next` on each pending job of `GET /api/queue`, and `status.next` on `GET /api/watchers` for a watcher error.
- Next step record (#43): the new module `src/next-step.ts` works out one record for every issue the watchers track and every run — `kind`, `who` has the next move, `why`, `action`, `where` (a label and a link), `until` and one-sentence `text`. Every waiting reason has its own kind (questions, planner's questions, risky plan, split, other approval, dependency chain, one at a time, code area, usage limit, daily budget, release, failed, restart, watcher error), and so do the states that do not wait. Watcher holds carry it as `Hold.next` (`reason` and `url` are filled from it). Runs paused by a usage limit, the daily budget, a code area, an interruption or a release now also show as holds. New `GET /api/next`; `next` on `GET /api/runs`, `GET /api/runs/:id` and every `update` of the run event stream; `GET /api/watchers` holds include `next`. Notifications (`FACTORY_MESSAGE` too) and the failure comment on issues use the record's sentence, so their wording changes: a failed issue now says to remove the label to start over, or resume the run to continue at the failed step. The server also records when it waits to restart (new version, moved data folder); a moved data folder now stops the watchers while it waits. `Scheduler.queue().pending` gains `githubRepo`, `issue`, `repo` and `task`, and `waitingFor` also names an earlier queued job that holds the same lock.

- The repository is now `MeloMar-IT/spaghetti-code-foundry`. The clone command in the README and the `repository`, `homepage` and `bugs` links in `package.json` use the new address. GitHub redirects the old one; in an existing clone run `git remote set-url origin https://github.com/MeloMar-IT/spaghetti-code-foundry.git`.
- Commits from the ticket, chore and ci-fix flows end with "Automated by Spaghetti Code Foundry (run …)", commits from the cross-review flow end with "Written by Claude, reviewed by Codex (Spaghetti Code Foundry run …)", and the plan and result comments on Jira and Linear say "Spaghetti Code Foundry". The subject lines ("Resolve #N", "Fix CI", "factory: …") stay as they are.
- Comments from the ticket flows (`github-issue`, `github-pr`, `github-auto`) say "Spaghetti Code Foundry": questions, plan, approval request, result and split. The hidden `<!-- claude-factory run=… -->` marker and the `claude-factory` label stay.
- The web UI, the app's own messages (CLI help and banner, notifications, the push-protection message, label descriptions, the failure comment on issues) and the docs say "Spaghetti Code Foundry". Label names, folders, branch names and the hidden `<!-- claude-factory` marker stay as they are.
- The data folder is now `~/.spaghetti-code-foundry` (`SCF_HOME`, else `FACTORY_HOME`, still win). On the first start of any `scf` command except help, `~/.claude-factory` is copied there in one step (config, runs, flows, blocks, learnings, queue, locks, evals) and kept as a backup with a note file `MOVED-TO-SPAGHETTI-CODE-FOUNDRY.txt`. Paths into the old folder in copied `*.json` files and in `config.yaml` are rewritten, and run worktrees are repaired with `git worktree repair`. The move waits while a run is running (runs now record their `pid`), space is short or the folder is busy, and is tried again on later starts and every minute by an idle server; any failure leaves everything as it was. A server on the old folder refuses changes after the move and restarts onto the new one. If the note exists but the new folder is missing, `scf` refuses to run and says how to recover. `scf service status` and `tools/area-lock` follow the new folder; run `scf service install` once. See "Upgrading from claude-factory" in the user guide.

- New command `scf` (Spaghetti Code Foundry) and package name `spaghetti-code-foundry`; `factory` keeps working and prints a short note. Settings work as `SCF_…` or `FACTORY_…` (`SCF_…` wins); steps and notify commands get every `FACTORY_…` variable also as `SCF_…`. The login service is now `com.spaghetti-code-foundry.server` — run `scf service install` once to replace the old one; if that fails, the old service is put back.

- Comments and pull requests from the issue pipelines say "Spaghetti Code Foundry" (PR titles "Foundry: #…"). Both the old `<!-- claude-factory` and the new `<!-- spaghetti-code-foundry` markers are recognised, so approvals, answers and splits work with comments from before and after the rename; new comments still carry the old marker.
- Setting `cost_limits: false` (Settings → Enforce cost limits): costs are still reported, but no run, step or daily budget stops anything.
- A server waiting to restart on a new version stops starting new runs, so it restarts once the active ones are done.
- Dependencies named by a shortened title ("Story 7 — …" for "Website Story 7 — …") are found.
- Gitflow: an issue is closed as soon as it is merged into `develop`.
- Gitflow: a feature branch is deleted on GitHub once it is merged into `develop`.
- Fix: a split is no longer skipped when a comment merely mentions the split marker; issues that depended on a split issue now wait for its parts.
- Coding agents may run the project's build and tests; `agent_env` passes settings such as `JAVA_HOME`.
- Gitflow pipeline (`issue-gitflow`, `release-daily`): a feature branch per issue merged into `develop`, parallel runs on different code areas, a daily release pull request `develop` → `main`, a size limit with automatic splitting.
- Too-big issues are split into new issues automatically when the split risk is low.
- One-label pipeline (`Factory_go`) with questions up front, a 0–100 risk score and a human check above 75.
- Flow-writing guide for AI assistants (`docs/FLOW_AUTHORING.md`, `factory flow-guide`).
